    ell_cap'=(6C^2+4C-2)/(3C+1)^2>384/625,
    r_cap'=2/(C+1)^2<=2/81

for C>=8, proving ell_cap-r_cap is strictly increasing. The first lower
bound uses3C+1<=(25/8)C and6C^2+4C-2>6C^2. At C=87/10 the exact
values of r_cap,t_cap,ell_cap-r_cap,k_cap-t_cap are respectively

    77/97,
    1552559/1081453,
    19728/131435,
    7318711/10814530,

which are all positive. Positivity in(30) also forces a,d,b,c positive:
their pairwise sums are positive, and their pairwise products are positive.
Consequently the full-curvature constraint(26) requires C*ad*bc<=1.
But the increasing lower bound from(30), evaluated at87/10, exceeds1 by

    9782467331765167/1375525927835685125>0.

This proves(29) for every larger sum, without assuming saturation in(26).

The monotonic formulas(9),(12) now refine the step upper bounds to

    1/2<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (31)

Finally p_i<9 and4s<154/5 in the exact circulation identity(16). Its
coefficients still exceed1/(4*9^3)=1/2916, so

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<449064/5<90000.               (32)

Thus(29),(31),(32) supersede the earlier coarse compact box; the sharper
pointwise rational cut bounds remain available inside that box.

## 11. Adjacent triangle witness, rederived in this joint argument

The shared UNVERIFIED C80 finding7b447329aac48edd suggested this construction.
It is awareness only: the geometry, every interpolation normal, and the
energy identity needed here are rederived in this section. The only fact
used for realization is1252b8823bb5a290.

Write h=(a,b,c,d), where b,c>1 and a,d>=0. Define

    P=a+1, B=b-1, E=c-1, D=d+1,
    U=b+c+d, C=P+U.

First suppose D>B*E and set

    Kappa=B^2*E+U*(D-B*E), t=B^2*E/Kappa,
    u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B*u+D*w)/c.

Kappa>0 and0<t<1. The convex hull of u,v,w contains0, since
B*u+c*v+D*w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Set

    x=(P*u,u,-B*u,D*w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and include the zero optimum jet. The four GD identities follow from
c*v=-B*u-D*w and D=d+1. Direct inner products give

    ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
    <u,v>=-(B+D*t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)*t/c.

All sampled interpolation inequalities follow by checking that x_i-g_i
is an outward normal to this triangle at g_i. At i=0 that normal is
a*u, with nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u. At i=1,4 it is zero.
At i=2 put y=-B*u-v. Expanding the displayed inner products gives

    <w-v,y>=(-B^2*E+Kappa*t)/c^2=0,
    <u-v,y>=-B*(1-t)*E/c<0.

For the first expansion, the coefficient of t is
D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B*c^2, which equals Kappa after substituting
E=c-1,U=B+c+D. For the second expansion the difference between the
two sides, multiplied by c^2, is-B^2*E+Kappa*t=0. At i=3 the normal
d*w pairs with u-w to zero and with v-w to
-d*t*(1+(B+D)/c)<=0. This exhausts the vertex comparisons. As0 is in
their convex hull, it also exhausts the optimum comparisons.

Indeed for these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>, so all the
original ordered Q are nonpositive, Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)<=0.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one global convex1-smooth function with
a minimizer at0 and precisely these GD data. The terminal x_4=g_4=w,
so(3) gives V=P^2-C^2*t. A direct expansion gives

    C^2 B^2 E-P^2 Kappa
       =U*{B*E[P^2+B(P+C)]-P^2 D}.

Consequently feasible full certificates must satisfy

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2] <= D.                         (34)

This condition applies without imposing D>B*E in advance. If instead
D<=B*E, the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at P,
has x=(P,1,-B,B*E,B*E-D+1) and g=(1,1,-B,1,1). Its final point
is at least1, and(19) gives

    V=P^2-[P+(B+1)(E+1)]^2<0.

Thus the complementary regime is impossible, proving(34) for every
candidate with b,c>1. The inequality itself implies D>B*E. No previous
word's Gram, an assumed primitive subword, or a numerical proposal has
been adopted in this argument.

The same construction proves(34) at any adjacent pair of steps>1, with
P=1+the sum of all earlier steps, D=1+the sum of all later steps,
B=the first selected step minus1, E=the second selected step minus1,
and C=1+the sum of the whole word. Before the selected pair every
gradient is u and each iterate is(1+the remaining prefix sum)u; its
proximal point is a nonnegative multiple of u, so the same two vertex
normal comparisons hold. After the selected pair every gradient is w
and each iterate is(1+the remaining suffix sum)w, with the same
nonnegative-multiple normal argument. All original intermediate samples
and all directed Q are thus retained. The initial point is P*u and the
terminal point is w, giving the same energy and(34). This paragraph
justifies applying the construction to the first pair as well as the
middle pair of the four-step word, without assuming either unselected
block is primitive.

## 12. The first endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose for a contradiction h_0<=1. The endpoint-sum consequence(28)
forces h_3>1. Since h_0-1<=0 and h_3-1<=r_cap(C), the same consequence
forces ell_cap(C)<=r_cap(C), or2C^3-15C^2-16C-3<=0. This polynomial
equals11/2 at17/2 and its derivative is positive for C>=7. Therefore
C<17/2. In the notation of Section11,

    1<P<=2, 2<D<3, B=h_1-1>0, E=h_2-1>0.

Rearranging the second-step cut and using monotonicity in P<=2 gives

    B<=P(C-P)/(C+P)<=2(C-2)/(C+2)<26/21.             (35)

The derivative with respect to P of P(C-P)/(C+P) has numerator
C^2-2CP-P^2>0 on C>=193/25,P<=2; the last expression increases in C.
Also(31) gives E<1552559/1081453<23/16, with the latter positive
difference32475/17303248. Thus both B and E are less than23/16.

We first show D<13/5. The suffix-pair inequality(18) becomes

    E <= (C-D)/(1+C(D-2)),                            (36)

since P+1+B=C-D-E and D>2. By the capacity identity and(35),

    E=C-P-D-1-B >= H(C)-D-1,
    H(C)=C(C-2)/(C+2).

Therefore feasibility requires

    F_sep(C,D)=H(C)-D-1-(C-D)/(1+C(D-2))<=0.           (37)

For C>=193/25 and13/5<=D<=3, this separation expression strictly
increases with C. Indeed H'=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81, while the derivative
of the final rational term with respect to C is
(D-1)^2/[1+C(D-2)]^2<=25/169; their difference is positive.
For C_0=193/25 the expression is concave in D: its second derivative
is-2C_0(C_0-1)^2/[1+C_0(D-2)]^3<0. Its endpoint values are

    F_sep(C_0,13/5)=2269/66825>0,
    F_sep(C_0,3)=1166/662175>0.

Concavity puts its value above the chord joining these positive values.
It is therefore positive throughout this interval, contradicting(37).
This proves D<13/5.

Now B+E=C-P-D-1>53/25. Since B,E<23/16, multiplication of
(23/16-B)(23/16-E)>0 yields

    B*E>6279/6400, B>273/400.

For P<=2 and C>=193/25, (P+C)/P^2>=243/100; the expression decreases
with P>0 and increases with C. Consequently

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2]
       >(6279/6400)*(106339/40000)
       =13/5+2102581/256000000 >D,

contradicting the full triangle condition(34). Hence

    h_0>1.                                           (38)

Together with Section7, the first THREE steps exceed1. No reversal of
the schedule or symmetry of the certificate was used.

## 13. The last endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose now h_3<=1. Set alpha=h_0-1>0,b=h_1-1>0,c=h_2-1>0,
delta=h_3-1<=0. As in Section12, the endpoint-sum bound gives C<17/2.
The last-middle cut and the capacity identity imply

    c<=2(C-2)/(C+2),
    b=C-5-alpha-delta-c>=L(C,alpha),
    L(C,alpha)=C-7-alpha+8/(C+2).

Apply the adjacent triangle(34) to the first two steps, with prefix
capacity1 and suffix capacity1+h_2+h_3=C-2-alpha-b. It yields

    b<=J(C,alpha),
    J(C,alpha)=(C-2-alpha)/(1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1)).       (39)

We claim alpha<7/10. If7/10<=alpha<77/97<4/5, put
R=1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1) and N=C-2-alpha. On the rectangle
193/25<=C<=17/2,7/10<=alpha<=4/5 one has R>5 and

    J_C=[1+alpha+alpha^2(3+alpha)]/R^2<1/5,
    L_C=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81.

Thus L-J increases with C. It also increases with alpha. Indeed
J_alpha=-(R+N R_alpha)/R^2, while L_alpha=-1. Here

    R<=197/25, N>=123/25, R_alpha>=1651/125,
    N R_alpha-R^2>=9028/3125>0.

Hence-J_alpha>1 and(L-J)_alpha>0. Finally the exact lower corner value is

    L(193/25,7/10)-J(193/25,7/10)=58247/22677975>0.

This contradicts(39), proving alpha<7/10.

For the middle adjacent pair now put P=1+h_0=2+alpha<27/10,
D=1+h_3<=2, and x=b+c=C-P-D-1. Then

    x>101/50, c<26/21,
    821/1050<b<783/560,
    (P+C)/P^2>=1042/729.                             (40)

The lower bound on b is x-c. For the upper bound use the cut
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P); this function increases in P<=27/10 and C>=193/25
on the current rectangle, and its value at(P,C)=(27/10,17/2) is783/560.
For its P derivative the numerator C^2-2CP-P^2 is positive at the
worst corner(C,P)=(193/25,27/10) and increases with C and decreases
with P; the C derivative is2P^2/(C+P)^2>0. The final inequality in(40)
uses monotonic decrease in P and increase in C of(P+C)/P^2.

Define the fixed cubic

    F_pair(b)=b*(101/50-b)*(1+(1042/729)b).

Since b<783/560<101/50, (40) implies

    b*c*[1+b(P+C)/P^2]>=F_pair(b).

The second derivative of F_pair is strictly decreasing in b and at
b=821/1050 equals-124646/42525<0. It is therefore concave on the
entire interval in(40), so its minimum is at an endpoint. The two
exact endpoint margins above2 are

    F_pair(821/1050)-2=211017368/4219543125>0,
    F_pair(783/560)-2=266455141/439040000>0.

Thus the middle-pair left side in(34) is greater than2>=D, a contradiction.
This proves h_3>1. All four original steps therefore satisfy

    1<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (41)

This conclusion is derived; it was not assumed during the search-domain
reduction. It uses actual legal functions with all interpolation pairs.
All prefix differences |p_i-p_j| now exceed1. Since p_i<9 and4s<154/5,
the exact multiplier-energy identity(16) further gives

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij <112266/5<22500.              (42)

## 15. A backward extension strengthens the middle triangle

This is a new joint DRAFT calculation in the seventh launch's final round.
It is not a fact and consumes no verifier or numerical allowance. All four
steps retain their order. Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0,
    C=5+a+b+c+d, P=2+a, D=2+d, U=C-P,
    H=U-b=1+c+D, eta=b/H,
    kappa(a)=(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,
    kappa_plus=max(kappa(a),0),
    R=P^2-kappa_plus, J=P^2-kappa_plus*(1+eta).

The preceding full-domain derivation gives a<1 and the b-cut gives
eta<=P/C, hence a*eta<1 (indeed a*P/C<3/7). Then a stronger necessary
inequality than the middle adjacent triangle is

    b*c*[R+b(P+C)] <= D*J.                           (43)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1 this is exactly(34). For a>sqrt(2)-1 the positive
term subtracted from the old triangle slack is strictly positive.
No relation between c and b, or saturation assumption, is imposed.

Here is the entire new interpolation check. The case D<=b*c is already
excluded by the explicit capped function in Section11. Thus D>b*c. Set

    K=b^2*c+U*(D-b*c)=U*D-b*c*H,
    r=b^2*c/K,
    u=(1,0,0), w=(r,sqrt(r*(1-r)),0),
    v=-(b*u+D*w)/(1+c).

We have 0<r<1, <u,w>=||w||^2=r, and 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}.
These are exactly the middle triangle vectors, but its initial prefix
will now change. Choose t in[-eta,1] and put

    q=(1+(a-1)*t)/a,
    z=t*u+sqrt(q-t^2)*(0,0,1).

The radicand is (1-t)(1+a*t)/a>=0 because a*eta<1. Define the actual
positions and gradients by

    x=(u+(1+a)*z, u, -b*u, D*w, w),
    g=(z,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The four GD equalities hold. Add the optimum jet(0,0,0). Each proximal
point y_i=x_i-g_i must support conv{z,u,v,w} at g_i. This suffices for
all ordered Q because Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

At i=0, y_0=u+a*z. Since t+a*q=1+a*t and every old vertex y in
{0,u,v,w} has <u,y><=1,

    <y-z,y_0>=(1+a*t)(<u,y>-1)<=0.

At i=1 and i=4 the proximal point is zero. At i=3 it equals(1+d)*w.
Its pairing with z-w is (1+d)*r*(t-1)<=0; pairings with u-w and v-w
are respectively zero and negative, as explicitly computed in Section11.

At i=2 let n=-b*u-v. The two old vertex comparisons are

    <w-v,n>=0, <u-v,n>=-b*(1-r)*c/(1+c)<0.

The remaining comparison with z is exact. Put A_num=D*r-b*c. Then

    <u,n>=A_num/(1+c),
    <v,n>=r*(D-b*c)/(1+c),
    A_num=-b*c*H*(D-b*c)/K<0,
    r*(D-b*c)=-eta*A_num.

The fresh coordinate of z is orthogonal to n. Consequently

    <z-v,n>=(t+eta)*A_num/(1+c)<=0.

The optimum comparisons follow as 0 is already a convex combination
of u,v,w. This checks every original ordered pair, including those
involving the modified initial gradient. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes
the complete array by one global convex1-smooth function with minimizer0.
No gradient Gram from any other schedule has been reused.

The energy is ||x_0||^2-C^2*r by the directly derived identity(3), and

    ||x_0||^2
      =1+2(1+a)t+(1+a)^2*q
      =P^2+kappa(a)*(t-1).

Choose t=1 if kappa(a)<=0 and t=-eta otherwise. The initial squared
norm is exactly J. Nonnegative energy therefore requires

    C^2*b^2*c <= J*K.

Using K=U*D-b*c*H, C-P=U, and J*H=P^2*H-kappa_plus*U gives

    C^2*b^2*c-J*K
      =U*{b*c*[P^2-kappa_plus+b(P+C)]-D*J},

which proves(43). Compared to the original triangle's forbidden-side
expression, the new left-minus-right expression increases by

    kappa_plus*[D*(1+eta)-b*c]
      =kappa_plus*K/H.

This is strictly positive whenever a>sqrt(2)-1. Thus(43) is a genuine
strengthening throughout that region, rather than a relabeling of the
old test. This last algebraic comparison is a strength comparison only;
it does not by itself prove there exists a word passing every old test
and failing the new one.

For rational elimination on the a>sqrt(2)-1 branch, use

    R=a+3+1/a,
    J=R-kappa(a)*b/(C-2-a-b),
    c <= J*(C-3-a-b)/(J+b*[R+b(C+2+a)]),              (44)

where c+d=C-5-a-b and D=2+d. All denominators are positive: H>0,
J=||x_0||^2>0, b>0, R>0. The strict positivity of J follows also
because its fresh coordinate is nonzero at t=-eta. Equations(43),(44)
retain the independent variables C,a,b,d (with c recovered by the sum).

## 16. A second extension retains both initial gradients and their normals

Continue the same joint draft, with h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) and the same
positive a,b,c,d. This section constructs a different legal witness,
with its last triangle at steps2 and3. Its symbols are local to this
section and do not change the definitions in Section15.

The old adjacent triangle at the middle pair implies b*c<2+d, and the
last adjacent triangle implies c*d<1. Set

    U_tail=c+d+2,
    K_tail=c^2*d+U_tail*(1-c*d),
    r_tail=c^2*d/K_tail,
    eta_tail=c/(d+2),
    A_pre=1+a+b, H_pre=A_pre+1=2+a+b,
    Z_tail=(c+r_tail)/(1+d),
    q_late=(1+(1-b)*eta_tail)/b,
    q_early=[eta_tail+(A_pre*eta_tail-1)*Z_tail]/A_pre,
    q_pre=max(q_late,q_early),
    J_pre=1-2H_pre*eta_tail+H_pre^2*q_pre.

Every denominator is positive. We have 0<r_tail<1, b*eta_tail<1, and

    q_late-eta_tail^2
      =(1+eta_tail)(1-b*eta_tail)/b>0.

The resulting new necessary inequality is

    C^2*c^2*d <= J_pre*K_tail.                       (45)

The old last-pair triangle is retained too. Equivalently the right
side can be replaced by min(J_pre,(3+a+b)^2)*K_tail. This use of a
minimum means choosing the stronger of two independently legal witnesses.
It imposes no equality between formal gradients in the full certificate.

For the complete construction take

    u=(1,0,0),
    w=(r_tail,sqrt(r_tail*(1-r_tail)),0),
    v=-(c*u+w)/(1+d),
    z=-eta_tail*u+sqrt(q_pre-eta_tail^2)*(0,0,1).

As before 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}. Define all five original samples by

    x=(u+H_pre*z, u+(1+b)*z, u, -c*u, w),
    g=(z,z,u,v,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and add the zero optimum jet. Each GD step has exactly its original
length. We check the complete normal list for conv{z,u,v,w}.

At indices2 and4 the normal is zero. At index3 it is n=-c*u-v.
The triangle calculation gives

    <w-v,n>=0,
    <u-v,n>=-c*(1-r_tail)*d/(1+d)<0,
    <z-v,n>=0.

For the last equality the Section15 calculation uses first-pair
shift c, second-pair shift d, suffix capacity1, and t=-eta_tail.
It is independent of q_pre because the fresh coordinate is orthogonal
to n. Explicitly, with B_num=r_tail-c*d<0,

    <u,n>=B_num/(1+d),
    <v,n>=r_tail*(1-c*d)/(1+d),
    r_tail*(1-c*d)=-eta_tail*B_num.

At index1 the normal is u+b*z. For an old vertex y in{0,u,v,w},

    <y-z,u+b*z>
      =(1-b*eta_tail)<u,y>+eta_tail-b*q_pre.

The coefficient of <u,y> is positive and its maximum over the old
vertices is at u. The choice q_pre>=q_late makes that maximum<=0.

At index0 the normal is u+A_pre*z. Its comparison with y is

    (1-A_pre*eta_tail)<u,y>+eta_tail-A_pre*q_pre.

The old values <u,y> lie between -Z_tail (at v) and1 (at u).
The v comparison is nonpositive by q_pre>=q_early. At u, even the
smaller q_late gives the exact value

    (1+eta_tail)*(1-A_pre/b)<0,

since A_pre>b. These two endpoints control the entire interval,
including0 andw, for either sign of1-A_pre*eta_tail. The pairs
between indices0 and1 have the same gradient and hence Q=0. This
exhausts all original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the single global convex1-smooth function.

The saturated-value energy identity gives

    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||w||^2=J_pre-C^2*r_tail.

This proves(45) without a numerical Gram or a loss of any original
coefficient freedom. If it is violated, the displayed data constitute
an explicit full-pair legal negative-V witness in three dimensions.

There is a useful closed subregion where the max disappears. If
A_pre*eta_tail<=1, the earliest normal has a nonnegative u coefficient,
so the calculation above proves q_early<=q_late. In that subregion put

    kappa_pre=H_pre*[2b+H_pre*(b-1)]/b.

Then

    J_pre=(3+a+b)^2-kappa_pre*(1+eta_tail).            (46)

Thus the old last-pair triangle is strictly strengthened whenever
A_pre*eta_tail<=1 and b>H_pre/(H_pre+2).

In particular this happens throughout the branch

    0<a<=sqrt(2)-1, b>=1                            (47)

of any remaining candidate. To check this assertion without numerical
constants, write P=2+a, L_mid=(P+C)/P^2. The old middle triangle says
b*eta_tail<=1/(1+L_mid*b). On(47), a<1/2 and P<5/2, so

    L_mid>(2+193/25)/(25/4)=972/625>3/2>1+a.

Consequently b+L_mid*b^2> b+1+a=A_pre, proving A_pre*eta_tail<1.
Also b>=1>H_pre/(H_pre+2). This is a full-domain implication on the
stated branch, not an assumption about the relative order of the steps.

The two extensions therefore give explicit strict improvements in two
complementary useful regimes: Section15 covers h0>sqrt(2), and this
section covers h0<=sqrt(2) with h1>=2. Neither result eliminates its
whole regime; they strengthen necessary tests there. Outside(47), the
full max(q_late,q_early) in(45) must be retained unless separately proved
inactive. A positive remaining value of one witness is not a certificate.

## 17. Repairing a quadratic prefix by the last triangle

There is a second full-pair way to use the same last triangle, which
does not identify the first two auxiliary gradients. Retain the local
symbols r_tail,eta_tail,K_tail,u,v,w from Section16 and put

    Q_pre=3+a+b,
    J_quad=[(1-b*eta_tail)*Q_pre^2
             +(1+eta_tail)/(a^2*b)]/(1+b).

Then every full feasible seed must satisfy

    C^2*r_tail <= J_quad.                            (48)

Here is its direct construction. Base data have

    x_base=(Q_pre*u,(2+b)*u,u,-c*u,w),
    g_base=(u,u,u,v,w).

Auxiliary data have

    x_aux=(u,-a*u,a*b*u,-a*b*c*u,a*b*w),
    g_aux=(u,-a*u,a*b*u,a*b*v,a*b*w).

Both arrays follow the actual h, and values are assigned in each array
by f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. The auxiliary normals vanish except at
index3, where the normal is a*b*n with n=-c*u-v. Write

    k=<u,n>=(r_tail-c*d)/(1+d)<0.

The triangle gives <v,n>=-eta_tail*k>0 and <w,n>=<v,n>.
All nonzero auxiliary finite interpolation residuals have target3.
For sources0,1,2,4 respectively they are

    a*b*k*(1+a*b*eta_tail) <0,
    -a^2*b*k*(1-b*eta_tail) >0,
    a^2*b^2*k*(1+eta_tail) <0,
    0.

Thus the ONLY positive auxiliary pair is(1,3), since b*eta_tail<1.
The incoming optimum residual at3 is -a^2*b^2*<v,n><0 and the other
optimum residuals are zero. The base array is legal by the triangle
normal check with all prefix nodes retained. Its pair(1,3) equals
(1+eta_tail)*k<0.

Choose

    epsilon=(1+eta_tail)/(a^2*b*(1-b*eta_tail))>0.

Take the orthogonal sum of base data and sqrt(epsilon) times auxiliary
data, with values added with weight epsilon. The pair(1,3) cancels
exactly. Every other original finite or optimum residual is nonpositive.
The GD identities are preserved in both coordinates, so the complete
array comes from one legal function by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Its energy is

    Q_pre^2-C^2*r_tail
       +epsilon*(1-C^2*a^2*b^2*r_tail).

Multiplication by the positive a^2*b*(1-b*eta_tail) yields

    a^2*b*(1-b*eta_tail)*Q_pre^2+(1+eta_tail)
       -C^2*a^2*b*(1+b)*r_tail.

Its nonnegativity is precisely(48). All auxiliaries are derived for the
same current schedule, with the actual first gradients u,-a*u,a*b*u.

The same repair can be made with the scalar last-cut base. In that
case take w=v=-eta_tail*u, x_3=(2+d)*w, x_4=w, and keep the same
quadratic prefix in the auxiliary. The same residual calculation holds:
its n=-(c-eta_tail)*u has k<0 and <v,n>=-eta_tail*k. This gives

    C^2*eta_tail^2 <= J_quad,                        (49)

which is the full j=2 cut repair in explicit rational coordinates,
rederived here rather than adopted from an unverified finding.

The two independently legal functions can therefore be combined into
the stronger necessary scalar inequality

    C^2*max(eta_tail^2,r_tail) <= J_quad.             (50)

The active branch has an exact schedule-independent description. Since
K_tail=(c+d+2)-c*d*(d+2), direct multiplication gives

    d*(d+2)^2-K_tail=(c+d+2)*(d*(d+2)-1).

Consequently r_tail>eta_tail^2 exactly when d>sqrt(2)-1, with equality
at that boundary. Thus(48) strictly strengthens the old scalar j=2
repair throughout h3>sqrt(2). On the complementary branch(49) is
stronger. This is a strict comparison of necessary inequalities, not a
claim that either test is sufficient. The displayed functions themselves
prove infeasibility whenever the relevant inequality is violated.

## 11. Exact elimination of the entire lower-clipping parameter

The new inequality is not an isolated parameter choice. The whole
one-parameter scalar family can be eliminated exactly. Let
h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), with all shifts positive, P=2+a,
H=3+c+d, C=P+b+H. Assume cd<1; the earlier legal last-triangle
condition forces this on any candidate. For every alpha>0, take the
same function with derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]), start at x0=P,
and perform all four original steps. Then its energy is nonnegative
for EVERY alpha>0 if and only if the following conditions all hold:

 C b/H <=P,
 C b d/(2+c)<=P,
 C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
 and, when bc>=1, C d<=P.                              (10)

This equivalence concerns only this particular one-dimensional function
family; it is not a characterization of full primitive feasibility.
Every strict violation gives a finite positive alpha and one actual
negative-energy function. Equality and zero limits are retained.

Proof. Every sample of every function in this family has saturated
value f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Thus V=P^2-Y(alpha)^2, where
Y(alpha)=x4+s g4, s=C-1. The first two steps reach x1=1 and x2=-b.
For alpha>=b the remainder is independent of alpha: x3=bc and
x4=bc-(1+d)min(bc,1). The assumption cd<1 guarantees x4>=-b:
if bc<=1 use x4=-bcd; if bc>=1, then b>=1/c>d and x4>=-d>-b.
Consequently the final lower clipping is inactive. If x4>1 then
Y=x4+s>C>P and the third condition in (10) fails too. Otherwise
Y=C x4, exactly the third condition.

For 0<alpha<=b, g2=-alpha and x3=-b+(1+c)alpha. Split at

 alpha_A=b/H, alpha_B=b/(2+c),
 alpha_C=(b+1)/(1+c) when bc>=1.

Their relevant order is 0<alpha_A<alpha_B<=b, and, when defined,
alpha_B<alpha_C<=b. For alpha<=alpha_B the third query remains
on the lower flat part, so g3=-alpha and x4=-b+(2+c+d)alpha.
For alpha<=alpha_A one has g4=-alpha and
Y=-b-(P+b)alpha, a decreasing function, reaching -C b/H.
From alpha_A to alpha_B, x4 increases and g4 follows x4 until
possibly reaching the upper cap1. Thus Y increases throughout that
interval. Its endpoint at alpha_B has x4=d b/(2+c), with
Y=C d b/(2+c) if x4<=1, and Y=x4+s otherwise. If x4>1 the
second condition in (10) and nonnegative energy both fail. The
limit |Y(0)|=b is smaller than C b/H since C>H.

For alpha between alpha_B and min(b,alpha_C), with alpha_C omitted
when bc<1, the third query is on the quadratic part: g3=x3 and
x4=-d x3 decreases with alpha. On each final clipping branch,
Y is one of x4+s, C x4, or x4-s alpha. Each is decreasing, and
they agree at their boundaries. Thus |Y| attains its maximum at
an endpoint of this interval. At its lower endpoint we already
have the second condition. If its upper endpoint is b, we already
have the third condition. Otherwise x3=1 at alpha_C, and cd<1
implies alpha_C>=1/c>d; hence x4=g4=-d and Y=-C d.

For alpha_C<=alpha<=b when bc>=1, g3=1, and x4=x3-(1+d)
increases with alpha. Also x4+alpha>0 at alpha_C and increases,
so the final lower cap stays inactive. Y increases, first as C x4
and then, if x4>1, as x4+s. Again its absolute maximum is at an
endpoint, already covered by the last and third conditions.
These regimes exhaust every alpha, and prove (10).

For the compact candidate box b<1552559/1081453<2<2+c, the second
condition is implied by Cd<=P when bc>=1. On bc<=1, the third
condition is Cbcd<=P, so the two new/old return constraints are
precisely

 C b d <= P min(2+c,1/c).                              (11)

The max over c>0 of min(2+c,1/c) is1+sqrt(2), attained only at
c=sqrt(2)-1. This follows from the increasing/decreasing branches
and their unique positive intersection. With the separate full
quadratic bound Cabcd<=1 one can strengthen (11) to

 C b d <= P min(2+c,1/(m c)), m=max(1,a P),
 C b d <= P [1+sqrt(1+1/m)].                           (12)

These are necessary inequalities only; no end of the capacity range
is fixed. The first inequality in (12) retains the actual c and is
stronger than replacing it by its maximum in the second.



# Appendix B. Reproduced all-curvature construction and continuum sign proof

The following is reproduced proof text, not an accepted predecessor. Local equation labels and local notation restart within each historical transcript. Its scope is the one stated in the main inventory; no historical allocation or handoff instruction is renewed.

## 1. Data and statement of the necessary condition

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word, let
s=sum h_i,A=1+s, and select an index j with h_j>1. Define

 P=sum_(i<j)h_i, R=sum_(i>j)h_i, H=P+h_j=s-R,
 eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R)>0, C0=eta(1+eta),
 V0=(1+P)^2-A^2 eta^2.

For kappa in(0,1] let

 p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa h_t) for0<=i<=j+1,
 E=1+kappa R, q=kappa p_(j+1)/E,
 t_kappa=(1-kappa)/(2kappa).

If R>0 put

 beta=max{0, max_(0<=i<=j)
       [q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R]}.

If R=0 put beta=0. Finally define

 V_kappa=
   kappa sum_(i=0)^j h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
   -H[s+R+1+1/kappa]q^2.                               (1)

Every full primitive certificate for the ORIGINAL word necessarily
satisfies, for every such j and every0<kappa<=1,

             beta V0+C0 V_kappa >=0.                    (2)

For beta=0 the stronger explicit interpretation is V_kappa>=0,
which is equivalent to(2) because C0>0. No other step is assumed
positive, above1, symmetric, ordered or part of a primitive prefix.

## 2. The legal scalar cut coordinate

Use the scalar convex function with derivative clip(x,-eta,1),
minimum0 at0, and initial point1+P. Put
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and R_i=sum_(t>=i)h_t. Its actual data are

 x_i^0=1+P-P_i,    g_i^0=1,             i<=j,
 x_i^0=-eta(1+R_i), g_i^0=-eta,         i>j,
 f_i^0=x_i^0 g_i^0-(g_i^0)^2/2.

The selected step sends1 to1-h_j=-eta(1+R), and the remaining
updates keep the points on the negative constant-gradient segment,
ending at-eta. All zero steps retain their original sample indices.
This verifies the full GD chronology on one actual function.

For this coordinate the ordered residual is

 Q_ik^0=(g_i^0-g_k^0)(x_k^0-g_k^0).

It is0 inside either block. From the prefix to a suffix target k>j,
it is exactly-C0 R_k. In the opposite direction it is
-(1+eta)(P-P_k)<=0. All optimum comparisons are nonpositive.
The norm-telescoping identity, or direct substitution, gives its
primitive energy V0 exactly as defined above.

## 3. A quadratic prefix and constant-gradient suffix

Define a second set of scalar data, not yet claimed to be legal:

 x_i^1=p_i, g_i^1=kappa p_i, f_i^1=kappa p_i^2/2,       i<=j;
 x_i^1=q(1/kappa+R_i), g_i^1=q,
 f_i^1=q x_i^1-q^2/(2kappa),                           i>j.

The point after the selected step is p_(j+1)=q(1/kappa+R),
so these data follow every original GD update and end at q/kappa.
Include the optimum sample (x_star^1,g_star^1,f_star^1)=(0,0,0).

Write Q_ik^1=f_i^1-f_k^1+g_i^1(x_k^1-x_i^1)
                  +(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2/2.
Direct substitution gives the COMPLETE list of residual types:

 * i,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2<=0.
 * i,k>j: Q_ik^1=0.
 * i>j,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(q-kappa p_k)^2<=0.
 * i<=j,k>j:
   Q_ik^1=R_k q(kappa p_i-q)
                    -t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2.
 * Q_(i,star)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2<=0 for all i.
 * Q_(star,i)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2 for i<=j, and
   Q_(star,i)^1=-(R_i+t_kappa)q^2 for i>j.

Thus only the fourth type can be positive. If R_k=0 it is
nonpositive. For R_k>0, its quotient by R_k is

 q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R_k,

which is nondecreasing as R_k increases. Since0<R_k<=R,
its largest possible value is at k=j+1, whose R_k equals R.
This proves that beta is the exact largest nonnegative ratio of
a possibly bad residual to its corresponding suffix mass.

In particular beta=0 makes ALL auxiliary data, including optimum
comparisons, legal. If R=0 there are no positive suffix masses,
so the convention beta=0 has exactly this same meaning.

## 4. Orthogonal repair gives an actual globally legal function

If beta>0 choose epsilon=C0/beta and set

 X_i=(x_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)x_i^1),
 G_i=(g_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)g_i^1),
 F_i=f_i^0+epsilon f_i^1,

with the zero optimum sample. GD still uses the same full word.
Every original residual is Q_ik^0+epsilon Q_ik^1. All types except
prefix-to-suffix are sums of nonpositive numbers. For that type
with R_k>0 the sum is at most

       -C0 R_k+epsilon beta R_k=0;

when R_k=0 the auxiliary residual itself is nonpositive. Therefore
all directed comparisons, including the optimum and original terminal
sample, hold. A maximizing i in beta and k=j+1 show that epsilon
is also the largest permitted weight of this fixed auxiliary data
against this fixed base coordinate. No residual was discarded.

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2, these planar jets have
an everywhere finite convex differentiable1-smooth interpolant with
minimum value0 at0. Concretely, with I={star,0,...,n}, define

 c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i,
 J_i(v)=c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2,
 J(v)=max_(i in I) J_i(v),
 F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-J(v)}.

The cited fact proves F(X_i)=F_i and grad F(X_i)=G_i from exactly
the residual convention just verified. Thus these are actual GD data
of one function, not a sum of separately illegal functions.

If beta=0, apply the same accepted interpolation construction directly
to the auxiliary scalar jets, since their full residual list is legal.
No division by beta occurs in this case.

## 5. Exact energy calculation

For the auxiliary suffix, f_i^1-f_n^1=q^2 R_i and
f_n^1=q^2/(2kappa). The elementary finite identity

 2sum_(i>j) h_i R_i = R^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2

follows by expanding R^2. Thus the combined suffix contribution of
the weighted value differences and gradient diagonals is R(R+1)q^2.
The prefix contribution is

 kappa sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2-H q^2/kappa.

Subtracting the terminal s(s+1)q^2 gives(1), because
s(s+1)-R(R+1)=H(s+R+1).

The primitive energy is linear in all values and Gram entries.
The repaired planar trajectory therefore has energy

          V0+(C0/beta)V_kappa

when beta>0, and the legal auxiliary trajectory has energy V_kappa
when beta=0. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, says an original
full nonnegative flow/PSD certificate forces nonnegative energy on
every actual trajectory. This proves(2), including beta=0.

## 6. The unit-curvature slice and exact low-degree polynomial interface

At kappa=1, q=p_(j+1)/(1+R)=-eta p_j, t_kappa=0, and

 beta=max{0,max_(i<=j)q(p_i-q)}, V_1=1-A^2 q^2.

For the last identity, the prefix sum in(1) telescopes to
1-p_(j+1)^2, using p_(i+1)^2=(1-h_i)^2 p_i^2. Then
(1+R)^2+H(s+R+2)=A^2. This rederives the unit-curvature
repair without treating the shared max2 draft as a correctness premise.

For R>0 the entire curvature interval can be expressed through
finitely many POLYNOMIAL branches. Define

 d_i(kappa)=[E p_i-p_(j+1)]/kappa,                     0<=i<=j,
 T_i(kappa)=2R p_(j+1)d_i-(1-kappa)d_i^2,
 T_max(kappa)=max{0,T_0(kappa),...,T_j(kappa)},

 P_cut(kappa)=E^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
              -H[1+kappa(s+R+1)]p_(j+1)^2.

The numerator defining d_i vanishes at kappa=0, so d_i is a
polynomial, including at0. It has degree at most j. Consequently
T_i has degree at most2j+1, and P_cut degree at most2j+3.
The exact identities are

 beta=kappa^3 T_max/(2R E^2),
 V_kappa=kappa P_cut/E^2.

Since kappa,R,E are positive, (2) is equivalent to

 kappa^2 V0 T_max(kappa)+2R C0 P_cut(kappa)>=0,
                                             0<kappa<=1. (3)

At each branch where0 or a specific T_i is the maximum, (3) is
a polynomial inequality of degree at most2j+3. For a four-step word,
the two middle cuts j=1,2 therefore require degrees at most5,7.
Their branch selection inequalities are the explicit comparisons
T_i>=0 and T_i>=T_k, with the zero branch selected when all T_i<=0.
This gives a precise exact real-algebraic interface for further work;
no polynomial-root search or numerical interval scan is performed.

## 1. The exact curvature inequality with smaller degrees

Use a fixed nonnegative word, a cut j with h_j>1, and the C83 definitions

    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, R=sum_(i>j)h_i, H=P+h_j,
    s=H+R, C=1+s, eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R),
    V0=(1+P)^2-C^2*eta^2, C0=eta*(1+eta),
    p_i(k)=product_(t<i)(1-k*h_t), E(k)=1+k*R.

Here k is curvature in (0,1]. Put p=p_(j+1) and define

    B(k)=sum_(i<=j)h_i*[1+k*(1-h_i)]*p_i(k)^2,
    Pcut(k)=E(k)^2*B(k)-H*[1+k*(s+R+1)]*p(k)^2.

The polynomial Pcut has a double zero at k=0. To check this, let
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then

    B(0)=H,
    B'(0)=sum_(i<=j)h_i*(1-h_i-2P_i)=H-H^2,
    p(0)=1, p'(0)=-H.

The derivative of E^2*B at zero is 2R*H+H-H^2. The derivative of
the second term is H*(s+R+1-2H)=H*(2R+1-H), the same number.
Both constant terms are H. Consequently

    Qcut(k):=Pcut(k)/k^2                            (1)

is a polynomial of degree at most 2j+1, including at zero. This is
exact division in the polynomial ring, not a limiting numerical test.

For R>0 define the polynomials

    d_i(k)=[E(k)*p_i(k)-p(k)]/k,
    T_i(k)=2R*p(k)*d_i(k)-(1-k)*d_i(k)^2,
    Tplus(k)=max(0,T_0(k),...,T_j(k)).

Each numerator in d_i vanishes at zero. The complete all-pair C83
calculation gives

    beta=k^3*Tplus/(2R*E^2),
    V_k=k^3*Qcut/E^2.

Since the canceled factor k^3/E^2 is strictly positive for the
original curvature domain, the exact necessary inequality becomes

    V0*Tplus(k)+2R*C0*Qcut(k)>=0, 0<k<=1.            (2)

The two middle cuts of a four-step word therefore have degree at most
THREE and FIVE on every branch, rather than five and seven. Branch
selection and beta=0 are unchanged. If Tplus=0, (2) is exactly
Qcut>=0 because R,C0>0; no division by beta has taken place.

At R=0 there is no repair credit or Tplus definition: the auxiliary
data are legal by themselves and their exact energy is

    V_k=k^3*Qcut(k),

so Qcut>=0 remains the necessary test. This retains the last cut.

## 2. The entire interval before any scaled prefix step exceeds one

Suppose 0<k<=1 and k*h_i<=1 for every i<=j. Then

    V_k>=0.                                         (3)

This statement does not require the base cut inequality or a bound on R.
Here is a direct proof. For i<=j write r=k*h_i and
t=k*sum_(i<l<=j)h_l. All factors 1-k*h_l are nonnegative. The product
of the later factors is at most 1/(1+t): indeed
1-u<=1/(1+u) for 0<=u<=1, and product(1+u_l)>=1+sum u_l.
It follows, without dividing by a possibly zero prefix factor, that

    p(k)^2<=p_i(k)^2*(1-r)^2/(1+t)^2.                (4)

For 0<=r<=1, t>=0 and 0<k<=1 the exact difference

    (1+k-r)*(1+t)^2-(1+k+r+2t)*(1-r)^2
      =r*[2k+(1-k)r-r^2]
         +2*(k+r-r^2)*t+(1+k-r)*t^2                 (5)

is nonnegative. The bracket in the first term is a concave quadratic
in r; its endpoint values are 2k and k, so it is positive throughout
[0,1]. The other coefficients are also nonnegative. Combining (4)--(5)
therefore gives

    [1+k*(1-h_i)]*p_i^2
      >=[1+k+k*h_i+2k*sum_(i<l<=j)h_l]*p^2.

Multiplying by h_i and summing, the identity
sum h_i*(h_i+2sum_(i<l<=j)h_l)=H^2 gives

    B(k)>=H*[1+k*(H+1)]*p^2.                        (6)

Finally

    [1+k*(H+1)]*E^2-[1+k*(H+2R+1)]
      =2k^2*R*(H+1)+k^2*R^2*[1+k*(H+1)]>=0.

This and (6) prove Pcut>=0, hence (3). Every zero product is already
covered by (4); no continuity assumption hides a denominator zero.

For any schedule that also passes the actual base cut, V0>=0. Thus
beta*V0+C0*V_k>=0 automatically throughout

    0<k<=1/max_(i<=j)h_i.                            (7)

This removes a whole interval with a sign proof; checking a finite grid
or one endpoint was not used as a proxy for that interval.

## 3. The first cut adds no curvature constraint beyond its base cut

Take j=0 and write x=h0, R=sum_(i>0)h_i, s=x+R, C=1+s. Direct expansion
of (1) gives the affine polynomial

    Qcut(k)=x*s*(s+2)
        +x*[R^2*(1-x)-x^2*(x+2R+1)]*k.              (8)

Its value at zero is positive. At k=1 it is

    Qcut(1)=(1+R)^2-C^2*(x-1)^2.

When x>1, the first base-cut inequality is C*(x-1)<=1+R, so this
second endpoint is nonnegative as well. An affine polynomial whose
two endpoint values are nonnegative is nonnegative on [0,1]. Therefore
V_k>=0 for every curvature, and the full j=0 C83 family is redundant
once that cut has passed. In the compact positive domain h0>1, so
this argument covers its whole first-cut branch.

## 1. Domain and exact statement

Keep h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) and C=1+sum h. The full compact-domain
derivation in the joint appendix supplies the following coarse consequences:

    1<h0,h3<9/5, 1<h1,h2<49/20,
    193/25<=C<87/10, b*c<2+d.                         (1)

The sharper bounds remain valid but are unnecessary for the estimates here.
Write m=49/20 and m0=9/5. For each middle cut j=1,2 use the C85
polynomials Qcut,T_i and the exact energy V_k, all recomputed for the
SAME original h. The actual base-cut inequality gives V0>=0.

Then every curvature up to

    k_hi=1/min_(0<=i<=j)h_i                          (2)

has V_k>0. Thus all middle-cut tests on (0,k_hi] pass automatically.
On (k_hi,1] every prefix factor 1-k*h_i is negative. There the only
possibly positive T_i is T_0 for j=1 and T_1 for j=2.

Moreover V_k has at most one sign change on (k_hi,1], from positive
to negative. Therefore, if V_1>=0, the ENTIRE cut-curvature family is
automatically satisfied. If V_1<0 its unique zero on this interval is
the unique root of Qcut there. These assertions concern the actual
auxiliary energy, before any repair credit is divided out.

Sections 2--5 prove all the sign claims. Section 6 then replaces the
whole remaining curvature interval by finitely many exact algebraic
evaluations, including every zero-credit and stationary boundary.

## 2. A bound used in the mixed-sign intervals

For a prefix of mass H and a suffix of mass R put

    F_HR(k)=[1+k*(H+2R+1)]/(1+k*R)^2.

It decreases as R increases, since its derivative with respect to R is
-2k^2*(H+R+1)/(1+k*R)^3. When R>=2 and H<=17/4,

    F_HR(k)<3/2,

because after using R=2 the numerator of the positive difference is
(24k^2-13k+2)/4; its discriminant is 169-192<0 and leading
coefficient is positive. When R>=1 and H<=67/10,

    F_HR(k)<3,

because after using R=1 the corresponding numerator is bounded below
by 3k^2-(37/10)k+2, whose discriminant is 1369/100-24<0.

In all cases the auxiliary energy has the exact form

    V_k/k=B(k)-H*F_HR(k)*p_(j+1)(k)^2.               (3)

The C85 no-overshoot proof already handles k<=1/max_(i<=j)h_i.
Only the remaining mixed-sign intervals need estimates.

## 3. The second cut: both possible step orders

Write x=h0,y=h1,H=x+y,R=h2+h3. Here H<17/4 and R>2.

First suppose y>=x and 1/y<=k<=1/x. Put t=1-k*x>=0. Then

    B(k)=1+(x-1)*t+(y/x)*[1+x-y+(y-1)*t]*t^2.

Since t<=1-x/y, x>1 and y<m, this gives

    B(k)>=1-(y-x-1)_+*(y-x)^2/(x*y)
          >1-(9/20)*(841/980)>3/5.                  (4)

The ratio (y-x)^2/(x*y) increases with y/x>=1 and is less than
(m-1)^2/m=841/980, which justifies the bound in (4). The possible
negative lobe of p_2 has absolute maximum (y-x)^2/(4*x*y), hence

    |p_2|<841/3920, p_2^2<1/20.

The last comparison follows from 20*841^2<3920^2. Equation (3) now
gives V_k/k>3/5-(17/4)*(3/2)*(1/20)=9/32>0.

Next suppose x>y and 1/x<=k<=1/y. Both x,y<m0. Each coefficient
1-k*(h_i-1) is positive and exceeds 1/5, so B(k)>1/5 from its
first term alone. The negative lobe now satisfies

    |p_2|<(m0-1)^2/(4*m0)=4/45, p_2^2<1/100.

Also H<18/5. Thus V_k/k>1/5-(18/5)*(3/2)/100=73/500>0.
These two order cases include the reciprocal boundaries. Together
with C85 they prove positivity through k_hi for j=1.

## 4. The third cut: all mixed-sign patterns

Write x=h0,y=h1,z=h2,H=x+y+z,R=h3. Thus H<67/10,R>1,
and F_HR<3. The following cases cover every curvature for which at
least one of the three prefix factors is nonnegative.

(a) Suppose k*x<=1. If all three factors are nonnegative, C85 applies.
Otherwise at least one of the last two factors is negative. Any positive
factor is at most 1-k and the absolute value of a negative factor is
at most m*k-1. The two possible sign patterns therefore yield

    |p_3| <= max((1-k)^2*(m*k-1),(1-k)*(m*k-1)^2)
           <=4*(m-1)^3/(27*m)=24389/132300<3/16.     (5)

The two cubic maxima follow by differentiating in u=1-k: the
expressions are u^2*(m-1-m*u) and u*(m-1-m*u)^2. Their maxima on
0<=u<=1-1/m are respectively 4*(m-1)^3/(27*m^2) and
4*(m-1)^3/(27*m); m>1 selects the latter. Thus (5) is a proof over
the whole interval, not an endpoint estimate.

The first term of B is 1+(x-1)*(1-k*x)>=1. If k<=20/29 then every
coefficient 1-k*(h_i-1) is nonnegative and B>=1. If k>20/29,

    p_1^2<(9/29)^2<1/10, |p_2|<1/4.

For the second bound, a positive p_2 is at most (1-k)^2<81/841<1/4;
a negative p_2 has magnitude at most the maximum of (1-k)*(m*k-1),
namely (m-1)^2/(4*m)=841/3920<1/4. Each possible negative coefficient
h_i*[1-k*(h_i-1)] is greater than -m*(m-2)=-441/400. Consequently

    B>1-(441/400)*(1/10+1/16)
       =1-5733/32000>4/5.

Using (3) and (5), V_k/k>4/5-(67/10)*3*(9/256)=239/2560>0.

(b) Suppose k*x>1 but k*y<=1. Then y<x<m0 and k lies between
1/x and 1/y. The bound from Section 3 gives |p_2|<4/45 and
p_2^2<1/100. The first two coefficients in B are positive, and
its first term exceeds x*(2-x)>9/25. The only potentially negative
third term is greater than -(441/400)*p_2^2. Therefore

    B>9/25-441/40000=13959/40000.

Also |p_3|<(4/45)*(29/20)=29/225, so p_3^2<1/60 because
60*29^2<225^2. Here H<2*m0+m=121/20. Hence

    V_k/k>13959/40000-(121/20)*3/60
           =1859/40000>0.

(c) Suppose k*x>1,k*y>1 but k*z<=1. Necessarily z<x<m0, so
H<121/20. Put u=k*y-1>0 and v=k*z in [k,1]. The contribution
of the last two prefix steps to B, after removing the nonnegative
factor p_1^2, is

    Btail=y*[1-k*(y-1)]+z*[1-k*(z-1)]*u^2.

Since v*(1+k-v)>=k for v in [k,1], expansion gives

    k*Btail=(u+1)*(k-u)+v*(1+k-v)*u^2
          >=k-(1-k)*u*(u+1)
          >=k*[1-m*(1-k)*(m*k-1)]
          >=k*[1-(m-1)^2/4]=759*k/1600>0.

Thus B>x*(2-x)>9/25. In this case

    |p_3|< (m0*k-1)*(m*k-1)*(1-k).

On k>=1/m0 the ratio (m0*k-1)/(m*k-1) increases with k and is
at most (m0-1)/(m-1)=16/29. By the same cubic maximum as in (5),

    |p_3|<(16/29)*(24389/132300)=3364/33075<1/9.

Therefore V_k/k>9/25-(121/20)*3/81=9/25-121/540>0.

Cases (a)--(c), including equality at any reciprocal root, exhaust
all curvatures through k_hi. This proves the claimed uniform
positivity for the entire j=2 mixed-sign range.

## 5. Unique auxiliary-energy sign change at high curvature

Now every prefix factor is negative. Let

    f_x(k)=x*[1-k*(x-1)]/(x*k-1)^2.

Its derivative is

    f_x'(k)=x*[x*(x-1)*k-x-1]/(x*k-1)^3.             (6)

For x=h0<m0 this is strictly negative, and f_x>0.

For j=1, divide V_k/k by (x*k-1)^2. The result is

    L1(k)=f_x(k)+y*[1-k*(y-1)]
                   -H*F_HR(k)*(y*k-1)^2.           (7)

All three terms together have strictly negative derivative. The first
two decrease. For the final term, the logarithmic derivative of the
positive product F_HR(k)*(y*k-1)^2 is

    (H+2R+1)/(1+k*(H+2R+1))
       +2y/(y*k-1)-2R/(1+k*R)>0,

because y/(y*k-1)>1/k>R/(1+k*R). Thus L1 strictly decreases.

For j=2, dividing V_k/k by (x*k-1)^2*(y*k-1)^2 gives

    L2(k)=f_x(k)/(y*k-1)^2+f_y(k)
                 +z*[1-k*(z-1)]
                 -H*F_HR(k)*(z*k-1)^2.             (8)

The last two terms strictly decrease. To control the first two, (6)
and x<m0 imply

    f_x'(k)<-(34/25)/(64/125)=-85/32,
    d/dk[f_x/(y*k-1)^2]
          <-(85/32)*(400/841)<-1.                  (9)

Indeed 1+2x-x^2>34/25, x>1, and 0<x*k-1<4/5. The extra term in
the derivative in (9) is negative because f_x>0. If f_y'<=0 there
is nothing further to bound. If f_y'>0, (6) gives

    y*k-1>2/(y-1)>1,
    y*[y*(y-1)*k-y-1]
       <=y*(y^2-2y-1)<(49/20)*(41/400)=2009/8000<1.

The cubic denominator in (6) is then greater than one, so f_y'<1.
Combining with (9) proves strict decrease of their sum, hence of L2.

Sections 3--4 give positive V_k at k_hi. The positive factors used
in (7)--(8) preserve signs on the open high-curvature interval.
Strict decrease therefore proves the one-crossing assertions in
Section 1, including V_1=0, for which V_k>0 at every k<1.



# Appendix C. Reproduced FULL2948 original-row proof

The following is reproduced proof text, not an accepted predecessor. Local equation labels and local notation restart within each historical transcript. Its scope is the one stated in the main inventory; no historical allocation or handoff instruction is renewed.

## 1. Statement and notation

Write a positive-excess four-step word as

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d),    a,b,c,d>0,
    C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d.

Suppose it admits the full primitive certificate of accepted fact
04219554e41d9750. Suppose additionally that a>sqrt(2)-1. Define

    u=1+a,             delta=u^2-2,
    H=3+c+d,           eta=b/H,
    R=a+3+1/a,         k=u*delta/a,
    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=R-k*eta-C^2*eta^2,
    Qenergy=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*d^2,
    Wrow=max(1+b,1+b*c*d),
    Krow=max(u,a*Wrow).

Full feasibility forces a<1, a*eta<1, nu>0, and
0<=Qenergy<1. The following quantitative necessary condition holds:

    u*delta <= Krow*Erow/nu + 2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).          (A)

In particular,

    Erow >= nu * [u*delta /
          (sqrt(Qenergy+Krow*u*delta)+sqrt(Qenergy))]^2 > 0.  (B)

Thus equality in the previously used lifted middle-cut condition Erow>=0
is impossible throughout this initial-step regime. A version avoiding
square roots is

    Erow>=0,
    [max(u*delta*nu-Krow*Erow,0)]^2 <= 4*Qenergy*Erow*nu.     (C)

If a*b<=1 and c*d<=1, the simpler strict necessary inequality is

    Erow > nu*delta^2/u^2,                                 (D)

equivalently

    b < (a+3)*(C-a-2) / ((a+1)*(C+a+2)).                (E)

The last two product assumptions are explicit hypotheses of this
simplification. They are not silently declared lossless for the
original problem. Conditions(A)--(C) do not require them. The separate
assumption a,b,c,d>0 is also retained explicitly; no assertion about a
zero step or a step at most one is being imported from an unverified
compactness draft.

## 2. Original formal certificate and its constant kernel

Put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_4=C-1, and let e be the five-entry all-ones
column. Write e_i for the i-th original standard coordinate of R^5,
with indices0 through4. Let T have diagonal1, entry h_t at(i,t) for t<i, and zero
entries above the diagonal. The original twenty multipliers satisfy

    Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, Lambda_ii=0,
    L=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L^T e=omega,
    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on ALL of R^5.               (1)

The identity in accepted04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                      =sum_(i,j) M_ij <g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)
          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i.                                (2)

Since Te=e+P and sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,

    e^TDe=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    omega^TTe=s^2+sum h_i^2,
    e^TMe=0.

For every real v, the nonnegative quadratic(e+t*v)^T M(e+t*v)
has zero constant coefficient. Considering both signs of t forces
v^TMe=0, hence Me=0. The initial row of this identity is

    sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j=2h0.                        (3)

To check(3) without any suppressed flow condition, direct expansion
gives(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 for i<4. At i=0,
P0=0 and(LP)_0=-sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j.

Let Aout=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j and m=M00. Its original diagonal is

    m=1-a^2-a*Aout.                                    (4)

Indeed the first column of T is(1,u,u,u,u)^T, so(LT)_00
=(1-u)Aout=-a*Aout. Since m,Aout>=0 and a>0, (4) gives
a<=1. Equality would give m=Aout=0, contradicting(3). Thus a<1.

For any positive semidefinite real matrix, the two-vector inequality

    |v^T M w|^2 <= (v^T M v)*(w^T M w)                 (5)

follows by applying nonnegativity to(v+t*w)^T M(v+t*w).
If w^T M w=0, the linear coefficient must vanish; otherwise minimizing
in t proves(5). This argument does not assume positive definiteness.

## 3. Two exact scalar energies

Here clamp(x,[-eta,1]) means min(1,max(-eta,x)).
The scalar function with this derivative and minimum0
has the actual trajectory

    x=(2+a,1,-b,-(2+d)*eta,-eta),
    g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).

Here b=(3+c+d)*eta. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and
1-Lipschitz. At every displayed sample its value is
f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2. The first step gives x1=1; the other three GD
updates follow directly from the displayed relation for b.

For any GD data with saturated values
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the squared distances gives

    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2.                        (6)

In detail, the first two sums in(2) reduce to
2sum h_i<x_i,g_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2-2s*f4;
the first two terms telescope to||x0||^2-||x4||^2.
Substitution of f4 then gives(6). The scalar trajectory has x4=g4,
so its energy is(2+a)^2-C^2*eta^2. By(1)--(2) it is nonnegative.
Consequently

    C*eta<=2+a,
    a*eta<=a*(2+a)/C<3/5<1,                           (7)

because 0<a<1 and C>5. This proves nu>0.

Next define the actual unit-quadratic gradient column

    p=(1,-a,a*b,-a*b*c,a*b*c*d)^T.

It satisfies Tp=e. Since Le=0,

    p^T M p=p^T D p=1-C^2*p4^2=Qenergy.               (8)

For the telescoping equality, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i gives
h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2; the terminal diagonal
then supplies-s(s+2)p4^2. Full PSD gives Qenergy>=0.
All a,b,c,d are positive, so Qenergy<1.

## 4. Complete lifted-cut data and its exact multiplier cost

Let U=(1,0), V=(0,1) be orthonormal physical vectors. Set

    z=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V.

Then

    ||z||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a,
    <U,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*eta.

Use the five two-dimensional data

    X=(U+u*z, U, -b*U, -(2+d)*eta*U, -eta*U),
    G=(z, U, -eta*U, -eta*U, -eta*U),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (9)

Every GD update has the original step length. For these saturated
values the original ordered interpolation residual is exactly

    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.

Here is a check of all twenty ordered finite pairs, grouped by their
target j. Targets1 and4 have zero normal. At target0 the normal is
U+a*z. The incoming pair(1,0) has residual0, whereas each incoming
pair(i,0), i=2,3,4, has residual-a*nu. At target2 the normal is
-(b-eta)U. The only nonzero finite residual there is
Q_12=-(1+eta)(b-eta). At target3 the normal is
-(1+d)*eta*U. The only nonzero finite residual there is
Q_13=-(1+eta)(1+d)*eta. Thus all twenty residuals are nonpositive.
The zero optimum jet can also be added: Q_(i,star)=0 by the chosen
values; the opposite inequalities follow from the same normals and
a*eta<1. These optimum pairs are not additional multipliers in(1).

For completeness these are actual jets of one global smooth convex
function. Let Kphys=conv{0,z,U,-eta*U} and define

    F(X)=max_(G in Kphys)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).

The comparisons just made say that X_i-G_i is an outward normal
to Kphys at G_i. Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer
as the Euclidean projection of X onto Kphys. If projections at X,Y
are G,H, adding their normal inequalities gives
||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>, and hence ||G-H||<=||X-Y||.
To see differentiability directly, optimality at X and X+v bounds
F(X+v)-F(X)-<G,v> between0 and<G(X+v)-G,v>, which is at most||v||^2.
Thus the gradient is the projection and is1-Lipschitz. The function
is convex as a maximum of affine functions of X; it has minimum0 at0
because0 belongs to Kphys. It realizes all of(9).

By(6), its primitive energy is

    ||U+u*z||^2-C^2*eta^2
      =a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2=Erow.            (10)

Let

    Bin=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40.

In the U coordinate the gradient column is-eta*e+(1+eta)*e1;
in the V coordinate it is sqrt(nu)*e0. The constant kernel proved
in Section2 therefore gives the full matrix remainder
(1+eta)^2*M11+nu*m. Substituting every nonzero residual above into
the original identity(2) yields the exact accounting identity

    Erow=nu*(m+a*Bin)+(1+eta)^2*M11
       +(1+eta)*eta*((2+c+d)*Lambda12+(1+d)*Lambda13). (11)

Every term on the right is nonnegative. In particular

    0<=m<=m+a*Bin<=Erow/nu.                              (12)

No incoming coefficient has been set to zero. Identity(11) prices the
original incoming coefficients explicitly.

## 5. The original first row couples the two energies

Define the weighted incoming combination

    Bweight=(1+b)*Lambda20+(1-b*c)*Lambda30
                                    +(1+b*c*d)*Lambda40.

The exact first-row identity is

    2*e0^T M p = u*(m-delta)+a^2*Bweight.             (13)

Here e0 denotes the original first coordinate column of R5. A direct
derivation avoids any coordinate reduction. Since Tp=e and Le=0,
Mp=Dp+T^T L^T p/2. Also Te0=u*e-a*e0, giving

    2*e0^T M p=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0.

The flow at node0 gives Lambda10=Aout+2u-Bin. Hence

    p^T L e0
      =Aout+a*Lambda10-a*b*Lambda20
                        +a*b*c*Lambda30-a*b*c*d*Lambda40
      =u*Aout+2a*u-a*Bweight.

Finally substitute a*Aout=1-a^2-m from(4). The constant term becomes

    (2-u)(1-a^2)-2a^2*u
      =u*((1-a)^2-2a^2)=-u*delta,

which proves(13), with all row and flow contributions retained.

All three incoming multipliers are nonnegative. Since b,c,d>0,
each coefficient in Bweight is at most Wrow. This remains true even
if 1-b*c is negative. Thus Bweight<=Wrow*Bin. Applying(5) with e0,p,
and using(8), gives from(13)

    u*delta <= u*m+a^2*Wrow*Bin+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)
             <= Krow*(m+a*Bin)+2*sqrt(m*Qenergy)
             <= Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Qenergy*Erow/nu).        (14)

This is(A). Put t=sqrt(Erow/nu). Because Krow,u,delta are positive,
(14) says

    Krow*t^2+2*sqrt(Qenergy)*t-u*delta>=0.

Solving this increasing quadratic for t>=0 and rationalizing its
positive root gives(B). Its numerator is strictly positive, so Erow>0.
Multiplying(A) by nu and squaring only when its left side after
subtraction is positive gives exactly(C). There is no sign-losing
squaring in this formulation.

## 6. A rational strengthened middle-cut boundary

If c*d<=1, then Wrow=1+b. If also a*b<=1, then
a*Wrow=a+a*b<=1+a=u, so Krow=u. Since Qenergy<1 and Erow>0,
(14) implies the strict inequality

    u*delta < u*t^2+2t,       t=sqrt(Erow/nu)>0.

The right side is strictly increasing for t>=0. Its value at
t=delta/u is

    delta^2/u+2delta/u=delta*(delta+2)/u=u*delta,

because delta+2=u^2. It follows that t>delta/u, which is(D).
The exact algebraic identity

    Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2
      =[(a+3-delta*eta)^2-u^2*C^2*eta^2]/u^2          (15)

can be checked by expanding the constant, linear, and quadratic
coefficients in eta. Those coefficients in the numerator are
(a+3)^2, -2(a+3)*delta, and delta^2-u^2*C^2.

The first factor a+3-delta*eta is positive: by a*eta<1,

    a+3-delta*eta > a+3-delta/a = 1+1/a >0.

Thus(D) and(15) give

    a+3 > (u*C+delta)*eta.

Substitute eta=b/(C-2-a-b), whose denominator is H>0. Since
delta+a+3=(a+1)(a+2), the result is exactly(E). Every denominator
in(E) is positive.

In particular this is a necessary bound on an entire step region,
not a condition on a sparse coefficient family. The exact stronger
condition(A) still retains the actual Qenergy rather than discarding
it in favor of the upper bound1.



# Appendix D. Reproduced original cut-slack and universal-prefix proof

The following is reproduced proof text, not an accepted predecessor. Local equation labels and local notation restart within each historical transcript. Its scope is the one stated in the main inventory; no historical allocation or handoff instruction is renewed.

## 1. Full formal interface and the gradient-difference matrix

Let r>=1, h_0,...,h_(r-1)>=0, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for0<=i<=r,
s=P_r, and C=1+s. Set h_r=0 only for formulas. Indices of the formal
gradients range over0,...,r. Let e be the all-ones column, and define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,  M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

Assume Lambda_ii=0, all off-diagonal Lambda_ij>=0, L^T e=omega,
and M>=0 on the entire original space R^(r+1). Thus this is precisely
the full finite certificate in fact04219554e41d9750, with arbitrary r.
No multiplier support, rank, or equality between formal gradients is imposed.

For x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)+sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                               -s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

The full coefficient identity from fact04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = <M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.       (1)

The residuals Q_ij are nonpositive on every actual convex differentiable
1-smooth trajectory by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Hence this interface implies
V_h>=0 on all such trajectories with a minimizer.

Here is an algebraic proof of the constant-gradient kernel, needed below.
Since Te=e+(P_0,...,P_r)^T and
2sum_(i<r)h_i P_i=s^2-sum_(i<r)h_i^2, we have

    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    e^T LT e=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.

Thus e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0: otherwise for
some y the linear term in (e+t y)^T M(e+t y) would be negative for one
sign of sufficiently small t.

For0<=j<r let u^j be the indicator of indices0,...,j. Let U have these
r columns and put K_jump=U^T M U. For any scalar formal vector g,

    g=e*g_r+sum_(j<r)u^j(g_j-g_(j+1)),
    g^T M g=sum_(i,j<r)(K_jump)_ij(g_i-g_(i+1))(g_j-g_(j+1)). (2)

In particular K_jump>=0. Conversely, under Me=0, its PSD property is
equivalent to full M>=0 by(2). This is a congruence identity retaining
all formal gradients, not a deletion of zero-step coordinates.

## 2. A legal cut trajectory for every positive step

Fix j with b=h_j>0. In this section define

    P=P_j, R=s-P_(j+1), A=1+P, B=1+R, d=b-1,
    v=1-b                 if 0<b<=1,
    v=(1-b)/B             if b>=1,
    w=1-v>0.

The two formulas agree at b=1. There is one legal scalar convex1-smooth
function with a minimizer whose GD trajectory has gradients1 at all
indices i<=j and v at all indices k>j, starting from x_0=A. Its positions
are

    x_i=1+P_j-P_i                         (i<=j),
    x_k=1-b-v(P_k-P_(j+1))                (k>j).             (3)

All sampled values can be chosen as f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.

For b>1, use the derivative clip(x,[v,1]) and f(0)=0. The bridge lands
at -d=vB, the suffix remains at or below v, and its terminal point is v.
The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and0 is a
minimizer. On each plateau the stated values follow by integration.

For0<b<=1, v=1-b>=0. Put zeta=v(1-R)-1, strictly below every suffix
sample and below v. Define a derivative on the whole real line by

    f'(x)=0                  for x<=zeta-v,
          x-zeta+v           for zeta-v<=x<=zeta,
          v                  for zeta<=x<=v,
          x                  for v<=x<=1,
          1                  for x>=1.

Choose the integration constant by f(v)=v^2/2. This derivative is again
continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and its zero region contains
minimizers. The bridge lands at v. The suffix stays in[zeta,v], so its
gradient is v. Integration gives the stated sampled values on both
plateaus. Degenerate intervals when v=0 cause no difficulty. The minimum
value need not be zero; V_h and every finite Q are invariant under adding
a constant to f.

For any scalar data satisfying f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, telescoping the GD
squares gives

    V_h=x_0^2-(x_r+s g_r)^2.

On(3), x_r=-d-vR, hence the exact energy is

    E_j=A^2-[(A+d)v-d]^2.                                (4)

The same sampled-value identity gives Q_ik=(g_i-g_k)(x_k-g_k).
Define, for k>j,

    t_jk=d+v(1+P_k-P_(j+1)).

These numbers are nonnegative, with the more transparent forms

    t_jk=v(P_k-P_(j+1))          if0<b<=1,
    t_jk=(-v)(s-P_k)             ifb>=1.

Every within-block residual is zero. Every cross residual is exactly

    -Q_(i,k)=w*t_jk,
    -Q_(k,i)=w*(P_j-P_i),              i<=j<k.             (5)

Since g=v e+w u^j and Me=0, substitution into the complete identity(1)
gives the exact slack budget

    E_j=w^2(K_jump)_jj
       +w sum_(i<=j<k)[t_jk Lambda_(i,k)
                                      +(P_j-P_i)Lambda_(k,i)]. (6)

Every term on the right is nonnegative. Equation(6) keeps every original
cross multiplier, including both directions and both endpoints.

## 3. Step-only matrix bounds and a quadratic compatibility test

For each positive h_j define beta_j=E_j/w^2 using the preceding formulas.
A feasible full certificate necessarily has beta_j>=0 and

    0<=(K_jump)_jj<=beta_j.                              (7)

When b>=1, the expression simplifies to

    beta_j=[A^2 B^2-C^2 d^2]/(B+d)^2,
    C=A+B+d.                                             (8)

For0<b<=1 it is

    beta_j=[A^2-(1-b)^2(A+b)^2]/b^2.                     (9)

No negative-v trajectory is used in deriving(9). In particular the
small-step case is not obtained by applying a wrong-sign Huber residual.

For this section suppose all steps are positive. Set beta_(-1)=beta_r=0
and Drow_i=sqrt(beta_(i-1))+sqrt(beta_i) for0<=i<=r.
For a PSD matrix, its2-by-2 principal minors give
|(K_jump)_ik|<=sqrt(beta_i beta_k). Equation(2), applied to a standard
coordinate vector, therefore gives

    M_ii<=Drow_i^2,
    |(M u^j)_i|<=Drow_i sqrt(beta_j).                    (10)

The second inequality is the PSD Cauchy--Schwarz inequality; it also
follows by requiring (e_i+t u^j)^T M(e_i+t u^j)>=0 for every real t.
For every real vector z of length r,

    0<=z^T K_jump z<=(sum_(i<r)|z_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.      (11)

An additional necessary condition involves no unknown multipliers.
Put p_0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. The actual quadratic f(x)=x^2/2,
started at1, has x_i=g_i=p_i and every finite Q_ik=0. Consequently

    V_h=1-C^2 p_r^2,
    0<=1-C^2 p_r^2
          <=(sum_(i<r)|h_i p_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.           (12)

The upper inequality follows from(2),(11), since p_i-p_(i+1)=h_i p_i.
Equation(12) is only a necessary compatibility test. Neither it nor the
scalar inequalities beta_j>=0 are asserted sufficient.

## 4. Explicit stability at a cut with b>1

Assume all h_i>0 and fix a cut j with b=h_j>1. Continue using the local
notation P,R,A,B,d above, and set

    a=d/B, w=1+a, delta=beta_j,
    m=min_(i<r)h_i>0, Delta_cut=AB-Cd.

Equation(8) implies Delta_cut>=0 and d<B: if d>=B, then Cd>=CB>AB.
Write u=Lambda_(j,r), v_k=Lambda_(k,j) for k>j, and set
c_k=h_k for j<k<r, c_r=1; thus sum_(k>j)c_k=B.

The ideal cross array has only the entries

    Lambda^0_(j,r)=d q, Lambda^0_(k,j)=q c_k (k>j),
    q=2(A+d)/(B-d),                                     (13)

with every other cross entry zero. At exact cut equality this is the
usual primitive-join array. Away from equality it is just an explicitly
defined comparison array; it is not claimed to be a certificate.

Here are step-only error bounds, with empty sums interpreted as zero:

    Fbd=w delta/(a m) if j<r-1, and Fbd=0 otherwise,
    Bbd=w delta/m     if j>0,   and Bbd=0 otherwise,
    Ubd=[2 sqrt(delta)sum_(i<j)Drow_i+P Bbd]/B,
    eta=Fbd+Bbd+Ubd,
    Ebd=2 sqrt(delta)sum_(k>j)Drow_k+2C(r-j)eta,
    Lbd=[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d].                         (14)

Then the sum of the absolute differences of ALL crossing coefficients
in the supplied full array and(13) is at most Lbd:

    sum_(i<=j<k)(|Lambda_(i,k)-Lambda^0_(i,k)|
                      +|Lambda_(k,i)-Lambda^0_(k,i)|)<=Lbd. (15)

Here is a derivation, keeping track of the off-support terms.
First (6) with t_jk=a(s-P_k) yields

    Factual=sum_(i<=j,j<k<r)Lambda_(i,k)<=Fbd,
    Bactual=sum_(k>j,i<j)Lambda_(k,i)<=Bbd.              (16)

The weights s-P_k and P_j-P_i in these sums are at least m. The
remaining potentially unwanted cross terms are u_i=Lambda_(i,r),i<j.
For i<j, summing the complete mixed matrix entries gives

    2(M 1_right)_i
      =-B u_i-sum_(j<l<r)(1+P_l-P_(j+1))Lambda_(i,l)
       -sum_(k>j)Lambda_(k,i)
       +h_i sum_(k>j,l<=i)Lambda_(k,l).                 (17)

In(17), 1_right=e-u^j. By Me=0 and(10), the absolute value of its left
side is at most2Drow_i sqrt(delta). The two subtracted sums are
nonnegative. Thus

    B u_i<=2Drow_i sqrt(delta)+h_i Bactual.

Summing gives Uactual=sum_(i<j)u_i<=Ubd. Hence the total mass of all
cross entries absent from(13) is at most eta.

For completeness, the full off-diagonal formula for i<k is

    2M_ik=-(Lambda_ik+Lambda_ki)
          -h_k sum_(l>k)Lambda_il+h_i sum_(l<=i)Lambda_kl. (18)

It follows directly by multiplying L and T; set h_r=0 in this formula.
For fixed k>j, summing absolute values in(18) over i<j gives

    sum_(i<j)|2M_ik|
       <=(1+h_k)(Factual+Uactual)+(1+P)Bactual<=C eta.

Since (M u^j)_k=M_kj+sum_(i<j)M_ki, equation(10) gives
|2M_jk|<=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+C eta.
Formula(18) at i=j gives, for k<r,

    2M_jk=d v_k-h_k u
        -Lambda_jk-h_k sum_(k<l<r)Lambda_jl
                                  +b sum_(l<j)Lambda_kl,

and at k=r gives 2M_jr=d v_r-u+b sum_(l<j)Lambda_rl.
The terms after d v_k-c_k u have total absolute value at most C eta.
Therefore, for every k>j,

    |d v_k-c_k u|<=e_k,
    e_k=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+2C eta,
    sum_(k>j)e_k=Ebd.                                  (19)

Sum the original value-flow equations over the left block. Its outward
flow is u+Uactual+Factual and its inward flow is sum v_k+Bactual. Thus

    |sum_(k>j)v_k-u-2(A+d)|<=eta.

Together with(19) this implies

    |u-d q|<=[d eta+Ebd]/(B-d)=:Uerr,
    |v_k-q c_k|<=[c_k Uerr+e_k]/d.

Adding these bounds and the off-support mass eta gives

    eta+(1+B/d)Uerr+Ebd/d
        =[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d]=Lbd,

which proves(15). In particular this controls the entire cross array,
not a selected sparse ansatz. No assertion about the internal arrays'
signs after tail restriction has entered the calculation.

## 5. Quantitative primitive-energy bound for the actual prefix

Let h_left=(h_0,...,h_(j-1)), of sum P and capacity A. Under the
hypotheses of Section4, EVERY actual convex1-smooth trajectory of this
prefix obeys

    V_(h_left)>=-[Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd] Gmax^2,             (20)
    Rbd=(A+d)Delta_cut/(B-d),
    Gmax=max_(0<=i<=j)||g_i||.

The energy of an empty prefix is0. For a nonempty prefix, no initial
distance normalization or bounded minimizer distance is assumed in(20).

To prove the claim, take any actual prefix data. Translate positions
and shift values so x_j=0 and f_j=0. Append formal tail data by setting
every g_k=f_k=0 for k>j and x_k=-b g_j for k>j. These tail data are NOT
asserted to arise from an actual function. They are permitted for
evaluating the full formal PSD identity(1). All tail-internal Q vanish.

Using the ideal cross array(13), direct substitution yields

    V_h+sum_cross Lambda^0_ik Q_ik
                      =V_(h_left)+Rbd||g_j||^2.         (21)

For an explicit coefficient check before the f_j=0 shift, V_h-V_left
has value coefficient2(P+b)f_j and gradient coefficient
b(1-b)+P(P+1) on ||g_j||^2. The ideal cross sum contributes
q(d-B)f_j+q[B/2-d(b-1/2)]||g_j||^2. The value coefficients cancel
because q(B-d)=2(A+d)=2(P+b). The remaining coefficient is

    A^2-(1+q)d^2
      =[(A+d)(AB-d(A+B+d))]/(B-d)=Rbd.

The actual prefix inequalities Q_ik<=0 and Lambda_ik>=0 make their
contribution in(1) nonpositive. Every cross residual on these formal
data has absolute value at most(2P+b+1/2)Gmax^2: actual convexity gives
|f_i-f_j|<=Gmax||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax^2, GD gives
||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax, and the formulas are

    Q_(i,k)=f_i+<g_i,-b g_j-x_i>+||g_i||^2/2,
    Q_(k,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2,                    i<=j<k.

Equations(1),(15),(21) and full M>=0 now give(20).
This proof uses the unobserved formal extension only inside a PSD
identity; it never claims those data constitute a legal GD witness.

The error Rbd is linear in the scalar cut gap:

    delta=Delta_cut(AB+Cd)/(B+d)^2,
    Rbd=delta (A+d)(B+d)^2/[(B-d)(AB+Cd)].              (22)

Consequently, along a bounded positive-step family with m and d bounded
away from0, B-d bounded away from0, and all beta_i bounded, the right
side's coefficient in(20) is O(sqrt(delta)). This asymptotic notation
is only a consequence of the explicit bounds(14),(22).

An actual prefix witness with V_left=-nu Gmax^2, nu>0, therefore forces

    nu<=Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd.                              (23)

This is an explicit necessary condition on the parent steps. It supplies
a quantitative route from a prefix obstruction to a positive gap at the
parent cut, rather than assuming an exactly saturated cut.

For a concrete symbolic instance, take a parent whose first step is3/2
and select j=1, assuming its selected bridge h_1>1 and all steps positive.
For its one-step prefix, f(x)=x^2/2 started at1 has gradients1,-1/2,
Gmax=1 and V_left=1-(5/2)^2(1/2)^2=-9/16. Therefore every such parent
full certificate must satisfy

    Rbd+(b+7/2)Lbd>=9/16.                              (24)

In any bounded family meeting the positivity conditions after(22),
this prohibits approaching delta=0 with that fixed first step. It does
not exclude a parent with a positive cut gap or any unrelated seed.

## 6. Equality, zero steps, and exact symbolic checks

At delta=0, every quantity in(14) vanishes and Delta_cut=0. Thus all
cross entries are exactly(13). Their mixed gradient block is zero by
(18),(19). The left internal multipliers are nonnegative and give a
full certificate for V_left, since(21) now has Rbd=0 and the full left
matrix is the corresponding principal PSD block. This recovers the
old saturated-prefix restriction by a new limiting-compatible estimate.


# Appendix E. Exact retained original-row parameter families and their derivations

The following is reproduced proof text, not an accepted predecessor. Local equation labels and local notation restart within each historical transcript. Its scope is the one stated in the main inventory; no historical allocation or handoff instruction is renewed.

## 1. Original matrix, notation and exact identities

Let

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, v>=0, bc<=1,
    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,
    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, H=2+a+b, H0=1+a+b,
    C=1+sum h=4+a+b+c+v,
    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t, gamma=w/mu,
    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t), K=b^2*gamma^2,
    Rcut=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,
    Dprefix=N^2*b^2-A^2,
    Gap=K*Rcut-ell*Dprefix,
    z=a*b*(w-mu)=a*b*v*t.

Here ell>=0 and gamma>=1. In this stated domain ell=0 if and only if
v=0 and bc=1. Every formula below that divides by ell is explicitly
restricted to ell>0. The case ell=0 is handled separately in Section6.

Write P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j, s=P_4=C-1, and let e_i be the five original
standard coordinate columns, indexed0 through4, and e=sum e_i. Put

    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j if j<i, T_ij=0 if j>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    Dmat=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=Dmat+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

The hypotheses are the original twenty signs Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j,
zero diagonal, L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on ALL of R^5. No coordinate,
multiplier, rank or support is removed. In particular v=0 does not
authorize identification of the formal gradients at indices3 and4.

The accepted polynomial identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                                 -s(s+1)||g4||^2.       (1)

It is valid on formal data following x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i; formal
substitutions below are not asserted to be actual trajectories unless
their interpolation inequalities are checked.

The flow implies e^T M e=0: Te=e+P,
e^T Dmat e=-s^2-sum h_i^2, and
omega^T(e+P)=s^2+sum h_i^2. Nonnegativity of
(e+r*q)^T M(e+r*q) for both signs of the real number r then gives Me=0.
The same argument gives

    |x^T M y|^2 <= (x^T M x)(y^T M y),
    x^T M x=0 implies Mx=0.                           (2)

Use the shorthand <x,y>_M=x^T M y and ||x||_M^2=<x,x>_M. Set

    E=e0, J=e0+e1, B=e3+e4,
    S=||J||_M^2, Tcut=||B||_M^2, r=<J,B>_M,
    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,
    Avec=u*E-a*beta*J-a*b*mu*B,
    c03=a*b*u*mu*v*t=u*mu*z,
    c23=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.

The next three identities hold for every array satisfying the original
flow and Me=0:

    Rcut=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*||Avec||_M^2
          +ell*mu*(H*B0+beta*B1)+c03*Lambda03+c23*Lambda23,              (3)

    Dprefix=beta*((b*w*H0-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*r-beta*S
                    -u*Lambda20-b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)),            (4)

    2<E,B>_M=a*B0-Lambda03-(1+v)*Lambda04.                             (5)

Here are full derivations. For (3), take saturated values
f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 in the two scalar arrays

    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    x_aux =(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).

Both follow the original h. Their normals x_i-g_i are respectively
(H,beta,0,-vt,0) and (0,0,0,-abvt,0). For saturated values
Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). In the weighted sum ell times the base array
plus mu times the auxiliary array, the complete nonzero residual list is

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*H, Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-c03, Q23=-c23.

All other ordered residuals vanish. In particular Q13 cancels because
ell=a^2*b*(1-bt). At Q03 use
ell+ab*(1+abt)=ab*u, and at Q23 use
ell+a^2*b^2*mu=a^2*b*beta. The position/gradient square telescope in
(1) for saturated values is V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2. Since x4=g4,
the weighted energy is Rcut. Me=0 gives g_base=-mu*B+e and
g_aux=Avec+ab*e, proving (3). No interpolation inequality is omitted.

For (4), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients
(1,1,-b), then extend it FORMALLY with constant gradient -b through
the last two steps and affine function values. The full energy is
A^2-N^2*b^2=-Dprefix. The gradient column is beta*J-b*e.
The only negative residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta. The positive
ones are Q03=Q13=w*b*beta and Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta.
The other ordered residuals vanish. Thus (1) gives

 Dprefix=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))
                  -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).

The two original cross rows, obtained by multiplying the displayed T,
are (5) and

    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.

Their sum eliminates Lambda03+Lambda13+(1+v)(Lambda04+Lambda14).
Because (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, the result is exactly (4).
These identities use the original rows; no reversal is invoked.

Finally define the original unit-quadratic column and its energy by

    p=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,
    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,
    m=||E||_M^2, delta=u^2-2,
    W0=1+bc*(v-1).

We have Tp=e and Le=0. Telescoping
h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives

    ||p||_M^2=Q>=0,
    2<E,p>_M=u*(m-delta)
                +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).     (6)

For completeness, put O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and
I0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. The diagonal gives
m=1-a^2-a*O0. Node0 flow gives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0.
Using Te0=u*e-a*e0 in Mp=Dmat*p+T^T L^T p/2 yields
2<E,p>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*p^T L e0. Substitute

 p^T L e0=u*O0+2a*u
             -a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).

The remaining constant is -u*delta, proving (6). On bc<=1,v>=0,
0<=1-bc<=W0. Consequently every coefficient in the incoming
combination in (6) is nonnegative. This sign assertion includes v<1.

## 2. A family of exact three-vector positive-gap remainders

For ell>0 define

    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu)=b^2*gamma/ell,
    kold=K/ell,
    U=2*beta*z/(a*u).

Choose a positive number k and a real number theta satisfying

    k>=k0,
    0<=theta<=min(U,2*k*mu*z),
    F(k,theta):=b^2*w^2-z*theta
                  +(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4-k*ell*mu^2 <=0.          (7)

Put

    Btheta=b*w-a*theta/2,
    Atheta=Avec-theta*B/(2*k*mu),
    cB=k*ell*mu*H-beta*(b*w*H0-u)+a*u*theta/2,
    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w),
    c04=beta*b*v*c-u*(1+v)*theta/2,
    c03new=k*c03-u*theta/2.

There is the EXACT full-array identity

 k*Rcut-Dprefix
   =||beta*J+Btheta*B||_M^2+k*mu*||Atheta||_M^2-F(k,theta)*Tcut
      +cB*B0+c1*B1+beta*u*Lambda20
      +c04*Lambda04+beta*b*v*c*Lambda14
      +c03new*Lambda03+k*c23*Lambda23.                              (8)

All displayed coefficients on the right are nonnegative. For cB,
at k=k0,theta=0 its value is

    b^2*w*H-beta*(b*w*H0-u)=u*(1-bc)>=0;

increasing k or theta adds nonnegative terms. The other coefficients
have exactly the signs in (7).

To check (8), expand its two squares. Their J-B cross coefficient is
2*beta*b*w because -a*theta*beta from the first square cancels
+a*theta*beta from -theta<Avec,B>_M in the second. Their E-B
cross contribution is -u*theta<E,B>_M. The Tcut coefficient is

    (b*w-a*theta/2)^2+a*b*mu*theta+theta^2/(4*k*mu)
                      =k*ell*mu^2+F(k,theta).

Now use (5) in the remaining u*theta<E,B>_M. Its contributions are
+a*u*theta*B0/2,-u*theta*Lambda03/2, and
-u*(1+v)*theta*Lambda04/2. Combining (3) and (4) gives precisely
every coefficient in (8). This proof divides by no matrix energy.

In particular, every full original certificate satisfies

                         Dprefix<=k*Rcut.                            (9)

## 3. The optimal constant in this three-vector relaxation

The minimizer of F(k,theta) before the upper bounds in (7) is

    theta_free(k)=2*z/(a^2+1/(k*mu)).

It always obeys theta_free<=2*k*mu*z. Thus the optimum allowed value
is
