<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001138 -->
# The fixed common-prefix law fails for every positive amplitude history

Status: **complete joint candidate, UNVERIFIED**. This continues the eighth
max4 assignment. No solver, parameter sweep, installation, or fact submission
is used. The only correctness predecessors are accepted facts
`1252b8823bb5a290` (original smooth-convex interpolation) and
`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark and its elementary bound). All other
arguments used here are supplied within this document. In particular the
earlier named-amplitude obstruction is not a predecessor.

The amplitudes below are arbitrary positive numbers. They may depend on the
entire known chronological history, the enclosing horizon, and the full word.
No two copies of a subword are assumed to have matching amplitude profiles.

## 1. Statement, unchanged word, and every probability atom

Fix an integer m >= 1024. Define

    sigma = 1+sqrt(2), delta = 1-1/m,
    c_chr = sigma(1+1/m), a_chr = delta/c_chr,
    N = 2^m, n = N-1, epsilon = 1/N, eta = 1/N^2,
    l = 1+a_chr, k = 1+delta.

The actual word is H_chr,0 empty and

    H_chr,j+1 = (H_chr,j, 1+a_chr*c_chr^j, H_chr,j), 0 <= j < m.

Thus h_i = 1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0 <= i < n, where nu_2(q)
is the exponent of 2 dividing the positive integer q. Every subword retains
the same enclosing-m parameters. There are n physical steps and N samples.

Give sample i its standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For
s in {0,1}^m define

    chi_s(i) = (-1)^(sum_j s_j i_j),
    B(s) = (-1)^s_0,
    C_j(s) = (-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1 <= j < m.

These are real signs. The map from s to (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective:
(-1)^s_1=C_1 and (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Put

    mu_+ = -7/10, mu_- = 9/10, p_chr = 1-epsilon,

    A_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1))
      = (1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=1)
          + (1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all c_j=-1),
    P_b(c_1,...,c_(m-1)) = product_j (1+mu_b*c_j)/2,

    w_s = (1+p_chr*B(s))/2
             * [(1-eta) A_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))
                     + eta P_(B(s))(C_1(s),...,C_(m-1)(s))].       (1)

Each conditional law sums to one. Every atom is strictly positive because
epsilon,eta>0 and both conditional means lie strictly between -1 and 1.
This is exactly the assigned finite probability law.

For ANY positive amplitudes Gamma_0,...,Gamma_n, prescribe

    g_i = (Gamma_i*sqrt(w_s)*chi_s(i))_s, 0 <= i <= n.              (2)

Suppose these are the actual gradients of ONE finite-dimensional convex
differentiable 1-smooth function F with a minimizer, along its actual GD:

    X_(i+1) = X_i-h_i*g_i, g_i = grad F(X_i), 0 <= i <= n,

where only i<n is used in the recursion. The same assertion covers any
Euclidean realization of the Gram matrix in (2). The anchor, function, and
values are unrestricted apart from these requirements. Define

    R = dist(X_0,argmin F), F_star = min F,
    V_chr = 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R^2,
    T_path = sum_(i<n) h_i*Gamma_i.

Then R>0, and the claimed bounds are

    T_path < 800 R,                                         (3)
    ||X_i-X_star|| <= 129 R for every i,                     (4)
    Gamma_n <= R[(3/5)^(N/8)+2^23/N^2],                     (5)
    V_chr <= 512(3/5)^(N/8)+2^32/N^2.                       (6)

Here X_star is a true closest minimizer. Since the exact original benchmark
obeys B_n <= 1+4*n^p, p=log_2(3), by fact `b98bb807d514e80d`,

    B_n V_chr <= (1+4*n^p)[512(3/5)^((n+1)/8)
                                      +2^32/(n+1)^2] -> 0.     (7)

The bounds are uniform over ALL positive amplitudes and all actual legal
realizations of (2). They concern this fixed witness class, not unrestricted
W_n(h). Failure on these original dyadic words prevents this class from
establishing the all-schedule matching lower bound. It does not decide the
project's minimax question.

For L-smooth functions, take F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L. The same proof then
uses the required normalization 2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R^2).

## 2. Necessary original inequalities and short-step consequences

The set of minimizers is nonempty and closed. A bounded minimizing sequence
for distance from X_0 has a convergent subsequence, so a closest minimizer
X_star exists. Positive Gamma_0 implies X_0 is not a minimizer, hence R>0.

Write f_i=F(X_i)-F_star. Accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies all
original ordered inequalities, including the optimum:

    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,X_j-X_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.

Adding the two orientations gives

    ||g_i-g_j||^2 <= <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>.                         (8)

In particular <g_i,X_i-X_star> >= ||g_i||^2. Norms in (2) are exactly
Gamma_i. Smoothness and grad F(X_star)=0 also give
Gamma_i <= ||X_i-X_star||. For a physical step 0<h<=2, (8) gives

    ||X_(i+1)-X_star||^2
       <= ||X_i-X_star||^2-h(2-h)||g_i||^2,
    ||g_(i+1)|| <= ||g_i||.                                  (9)

For the second assertion, put d=g_(i+1)-g_i. Then
-h<d,g_i> >= ||d||^2, and expansion of ||g_i+d||^2 gives an increment
at most (1-2/h)||d||^2 <= 0. These facts will only be applied to the
explicit short physical steps l,k,l, all strictly between 0 and 2.

We will use the following rational bounds:

    7/5 < l < 3/2, 1 < k < 2, delta*c_chr < 5/2,
    epsilon < 1/65536, eta < 1/65536,
    p_chr >= 999/1000.                                      (10)

For example sqrt(2)<99/70 by squaring, so
c_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<49/20. Also delta>99/100, giving
delta/c_chr>(99/100)/(49/20)>2/5; c_chr>2 gives the upper bound on l.
Finally delta*c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<sigma<5/2. The bounds on epsilon,eta
follow from m>=1024. No decimal approximation is needed.

## 3. A uniform movement bound before taking any small-mixture estimate

Select in (1) the component B=1 and all prefix signs common. Its mass is

    lambda = (1-epsilon/2)(1-eta), omega = 1-lambda.

Conditional on it, the only variable sign is C_1, with mean -7/10; all
character bits except s_1 are zero. Thus its unit sample directions are
two unit vectors a_0,b_0, in the exact period a_0,a_0,b_0,b_0, with
<a_0,b_0>=-beta_0, beta_0=7/10. The remaining measure has mass omega.
In a direct-sum Euclidean realization of the SAME Gram matrix,

    g_i = Gamma_i (sqrt(lambda)*u_i, sqrt(omega)*z_i),
    u_i in {a_0,b_0}, ||z_i||=1.                             (11)

Splitting a probability mixture proves this representation directly. An
isometry of gradient spans, extended to the initial displacement and any
orthogonal component, preserves every scalar product and the initial
distance. Thus it is legitimate for every realization in the statement.

Fix a block start t=4r, 0<=r<N/4. Before t put

    P_a = sum_(j<t,u_j=a_0) h_j*Gamma_j,
    P_b = sum_(j<t,u_j=b_0) h_j*Gamma_j, T=P_a+P_b.

Translate X_star to zero. If X_0=(x^c,x^r), then

    X_t = (x^c-sqrt(lambda)(P_a*a_0+P_b*b_0),
                         x^r-sqrt(omega)*W), ||W||<=T.

Define normalized coarse coordinates

    A_x = <a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_a+beta_0*P_b,
    B_x = <b_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)-P_b+beta_0*P_a.

The first two steps in the block have sizes l,k and both coarse directions
a_0. Their total amplitude-weighted mass is

    D_a = l*Gamma_t+k*Gamma_(t+1)>0.

Their displacement is (sqrt(lambda)*D_a*a_0,sqrt(omega)*Z), ||Z||<=D_a.
Both are short steps. The two distance inequalities in (9), added or
applied successively, therefore give

    lambda*D_a^2
       <= 2 lambda*D_a*A_x
            +2 sqrt(omega)(R+sqrt(omega)*T)*D_a.

With e=omega/lambda and r_0=sqrt(omega)*R/lambda, division gives

    D_a <= 2 A_x+2(r_0+eT).                                 (12)

At sample t+2 the coarse gradient direction is b_0. Its optimum
inequality <g_(t+2),X_(t+2)> >= ||g_(t+2)||^2 > 0 implies, after
bounding the remainder component by its norm,

    B_x+(beta_0+e)D_a+r_0+eT >= 0.

Substitution of (12), with positive coefficient beta_0+e, yields

    B_x+2(beta_0+e)A_x+[1+2(beta_0+e)](r_0+eT) >= 0.          (13)

Here omega<=epsilon/2+eta<1/65536, so

    e<1/1000, 1/sqrt(lambda)<1001/1000,
    sqrt(omega)/lambda<1/255.

Expanding (13) gives a bound on the nonnegative masses:

    c_a P_a+c_b P_b
       <= <b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0,x^c>/sqrt(lambda)
                           +[1+2(beta_0+e)]r_0,

    c_a = beta_0+(1-2beta_0)e-2e^2,
    c_b = 1-2beta_0^2-(1+4beta_0)e-2e^2.                    (14)

Both coefficients exceed 1/64: their lower bounds at 0<=e<=1/1000
are 699598/1000000 and 16198/1000000. Also

    ||b_0+2(beta_0+e)a_0||^2 = 1+4e(beta_0+e)
                                      < (1002/1000)^2,
    1+2(beta_0+e)<5/2.

The right side of (14) is less than
[(1002/1000)(1001/1000)+(5/2)/255]R < 2R. Therefore

    T < 128 R at EVERY block start.                         (15)

This bound has been derived with arbitrary amplitudes still present; no
bounded-amplitude hypothesis or conditioning assumption has been imposed.
At a block start ||X_t||<=R+T<=129R. Its three short steps l,k,l preserve
this bound by (9). These blocks cover every sample 0,...,n, proving (4).
Every Gamma_i<=129R as well. At the last block the mass added after its
start is at most (2l+k)129R<5*129R. Thus

    T_path < (128+645)R < 800R,
    K_path := R+T_path < 1024R.                             (16)

In particular every gradient norm and every subinterval's total mass is
bounded by K_path. This estimate precedes the perturbation argument below.

## 4. Keep the finite epsilon component exactly: four coarse directions

Now split (1) in a DIFFERENT way: keep the whole common-prefix component,
of mass 1-eta, and regard only the conditional-product component of mass
eta as the remainder. In the common component write C_j=V for every j.
The four unit directions for indices modulo 4 are

    a_e=1, a_o=B, b_e=V, b_o=B V                              (17)

in the finite real probability space of (B,V). Their correlations are

    p = E B = 1-epsilon,
    z = E V = -7/10+4epsilon/5,
    w = E B V = -7/10-epsilon/10,

    <a_e,a_o>=<b_e,b_o>=p,
    <a_o,b_o>=<a_e,b_e>=z,
    <a_e,b_o>=<a_o,b_e>=w.                                 (18)

These formulas average the stated conditional means in (1). In particular
finite epsilon has NOT been replaced by zero. Put beta=-z. We have

    69/100 <= beta <= 7/10, p>0, p-z*w>0,
    1-k*z^2 >= 1-2(7/10)^2 = 1/50.                         (19)

The positivity p-zw follows already from p>=999/1000,
|z|<=7/10 and |w|<701/1000.

Let v_i denote the coarse direction (17) at sample i, and let u_i denote
its unit direction under the FULL measure (1). Splitting the probability
mixture gives, for ALL original i,j,

    |<u_i,u_j>-<v_i,v_j>| <= 2 eta.                         (20)

For a pair with amplitudes A,B and intervening total mass T_ij, replacing
all its directions by their coarse versions changes the cocoercivity
expression <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2 by at most

    2 eta(A+B)T_ij +4 eta A B
           <= 4 eta K_path(A+B).                           (21)

The first term comes from the actual displacement sum, one scalar product
at a time; the second comes from the cross term in the squared gradient
difference. Individual squared norms are unchanged. The final inequality
uses (16) and 2AB<=K_path(A+B). Thus (21) accounts explicitly for the
whole amplitude-weighted trajectory, rather than merely the Gram entries.

## 5. Every consecutive odd-b return can grow only by O(eta K_path)

Let j=4r+3 and suppose j+4<=n. These are consecutive odd-b samples.
Their four intervening steps and directions are

    j:     H on b_o, amplitude B_0=Gamma_j,
    j+1:   l on a_e, amplitude A_0=Gamma_(j+1),
    j+2:   k on a_o, amplitude C_0=Gamma_(j+2),
    j+3:   l on b_e, amplitude E_0=Gamma_(j+3),
    j+4:            b_o, amplitude D_0=Gamma_(j+4).

The physical H may be arbitrarily large. No amplitude ratios are imposed.
Temporarily set a=a_o, b=b_o, tau=<a,b>=z=-beta, and

    Z = -H B_0 b-l A_0 a_e,
    X = <b,Z>, Y=<a-tau*b,Z>.

By (18),(19),

    Y = -l A_0(p-z*w) <= 0.                                (22)

The ORIGINAL pair between j and j+2, together with (21), therefore gives

    C_0^2+B_0^2-2tau B_0 C_0
       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+C_0 Y+e_0(C_0+B_0)
       <= (tau C_0-B_0)X+e_0(C_0+B_0),                     (23)

where e_0=4 eta K_path. Suppose Delta=D_0-B_0>0. The ORIGINAL pair
between j and j+4 similarly gives

    Delta[X-k C_0 tau-l E_0 p]
       >= Delta^2-e_0(D_0+B_0).

Consequently

    X >= Delta+k C_0 tau+l E_0 p-e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.         (24)

Substitute (24) into (23). Its coefficient tau C_0-B_0 is negative.
Putting M=B_0+beta C_0>0 gives exactly

    (1-k*tau^2)C_0^2 + tau(k-2)B_0 C_0+B_0^2
           +M Delta+M l E_0 p
       <= e_0(C_0+B_0)+M e_0(D_0+B_0)/Delta.                (25)

Every discarded term on the left is nonnegative: tau<0, k<2, p>0.
It follows from (19) that the left side is at least

    (C_0^2+B_0^2)/100 + M Delta.

On the right, D_0+B_0=Delta+2B_0, so it is at most

    e_0[(1+beta)C_0+2B_0] + (2e_0/Delta)B_0 M
       <= 4e_0 M + (3e_0/Delta)(C_0^2+B_0^2).             (26)

Here beta>=69/100 proves the first bound, and
B_0 M <= (3/2)(B_0^2+C_0^2) proves the second.
If Delta>=1024e_0, (26) is at most

    M Delta/256 + (3/1024)(C_0^2+B_0^2),

strictly less than the lower bound for (25). This is a contradiction.
Thus every such return obeys

    Gamma_(j+4) <= Gamma_j + d_amp,
    d_amp = 4096 eta K_path <= 2^22 eta R.                  (27)

This uses two pairs of ORIGINAL samples and their actual displacement
sums. The profiles on either side need not match in any way. If eta=0,
the same argument with zero error directly rules out Delta>0, so the
exact four-ray boundary has nonincreasing odd-b amplitudes.

## 6. Every other return contracts by 3/5, for the full finite measure

For any original bridge of level q>=1 and its following leaf, let the
three amplitudes be A,M,D and the two steps 1+u,l. The relative character
signs of the three samples are 1,B C_q,C_q. This follows because adding
one to an odd binary label with q trailing ones flips bits 0,...,q; the
following leaf flips bit 0 once more. Therefore the FULL law, including
its conditional-product part, gives correlations w,p,z in that order.
Both mixture components have the same individual conditional prefix mean.

The two original inequalities between the endpoints give the exact sum

    0 >= -u A^2+D^2+(u-1)z A D+l M(pD-wA).                 (28)

The following leaf is short, so (9) gives M>=D. Since p>0 and w<0,
pD-wA>0. Hence the ratio q_amp=D/A satisfies

    F_u(q_amp) <= 0,
    F_u(q) = (1+lp)q^2+[(u-1)z-lw]q-u.                    (29)

This is a necessary inequality with arbitrary adjacent slack. Its positive
leading coefficient and negative constant term give exactly one positive
root; F_u(q)<=0 for positive q places q below that root.

At level 1, u=delta<1. Bounds (10),(18) give

    1+lp >= 11993/5000,
    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)l >= 49/50,
    F_delta(1/2) >= 1793/20000 > 0.

Thus every low bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 1/2.

At level 2, u=delta*c_chr lies between 1 and 5/2. Since z>=-7/10,
w<=-7/10 and l>7/5,

    (u-1)z-lw >= (7/10)(1+l-u) >= -7/100,
    F_u(6/5) >= 108748/125000 > 0.

Thus every level-2 bridge followed by its leaf has gain less than 6/5.
If the H in section 5 has level 2, its return is exactly the concatenation
of this compound step and a level-1 compound step. Therefore

    Gamma_(j+4) <= (3/5) Gamma_j at every level-2 return.    (30)

No value, anchor, copy profile, or equality assumption was used in this
calculation. The even samples and their physical leaves are retained.

## 7. The exact block count, terminal objective, and benchmark

There are K=N/4 odd-b samples. Put B_r=Gamma_(4r+3), 0<=r<K.
For 0<=r<K-1 the bridge at index 4r+3 has level

    nu_2(4r+4)=2+nu_2(r+1).

It has level 2 precisely when r is even. Since K is even, (27),(30) give

    B_(2s+1) <= theta B_(2s), theta=3/5,
    B_(2s+2) <= B_(2s+1)+d_amp,

whenever the indicated indices exist. The final return is a contraction.
Iteration of these scalar inequalities, keeping the actual K, yields

    B_(K-1) <= theta^(K/2) B_0
                   + [theta/(1-theta)] d_amp
             = theta^(N/8) B_0+(3/2)d_amp.                 (31)

The first three steps are l,k,l<2, so B_0=Gamma_3<=Gamma_0<=R.
Equations (16),(27),(31) prove (5), since
(3/2)*2^22 < 2^23 and eta=N^-2. The terminal sample is exactly
n=4(K-1)+3. Neither a discarded suffix nor a free extra terminal sample
is present.

Convexity at X_n gives

    F(X_n)-F_star <= <g_n,X_n-X_star>
                     <= 129R Gamma_n.

After division by the TRUE distance squared, this gives
V_chr<=258[(3/5)^(N/8)+2^23/N^2], which implies (6).
There is no replacement of distance by an inverse-Gram anchor norm.

For the original B_n, accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` gives (7). Its first
term tends to zero because a geometric exponential in N dominates N^p;
its second does because p=log_2(3)<2. Both limits hold uniformly over the
entire amplitude/anchor/function class. The horizon remains n=2^m-1.

## 8. The fixed finite class is nonempty

This is a nonvacuity construction, not a competitive lower witness. It also
shows that the obstruction does not mean the Gram itself is illegal.
For the SAME full-support weights (1) and the SAME actual word, put

    H_max=max_i h_i,
    kappa_chr=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0,
    q_decay=kappa_chr/[8(H_max+kappa_chr+1)],
    Gamma_i=q_decay^i, f_i=(kappa_chr+1/2)Gamma_i^2.

All N characters are present and have positive weight, so the character
matrix is invertible. Indeed
sum_s chi_s(i)chi_s(j)=N for i=j and 0 otherwise, by pairing the sign
of a differing bit. Hence the Gram K_ij=<g_i,g_j> is positive definite.
Define

    b_i=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,
    X_0=sum_i (K^(-1)b)_i g_i,
    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.

These finite formulas fix one anchor before GD is run. They give
<X_i,g_i>=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2. For i<j direct expansion gives

    Q_ij=(kappa_chr+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa_chr Gamma_j^2
                          -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,
    Q_ji=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2
                          +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt.

Using |K_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t, q_decay<1/2 and h_i-1>=4kappa_chr,
the first residual divided by Gamma_i^2 is at most

    -3kappa_chr+H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)+q_decay
       <= -3kappa_chr+2(H_max+1)q_decay
       <= -11kappa_chr/4 < 0.

The second is at most

    -kappa_chr+(kappa_chr+1)q_decay^2
                             +H_max*q_decay/(1-q_decay)
       <= -kappa_chr+(2H_max+kappa_chr+1)q_decay
       <= -3kappa_chr/4 < 0.

For the optimum jet (0,0,0), Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus ALL original ordered pairs,
including the optimum and terminal, have been checked.

For an explicit one-function realization, let

    C_poly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),
    F_poly(X)=max_(v in C_poly) [<X,v>-||v||^2/2].

The unique maximizer is the nearest-point projection P_(C_poly)(X).
Its normal condition is <X-P_(C_poly)(X),v-P_(C_poly)(X)><=0 for every
v in C_poly. Adding this condition at X and Y proves that the projection
is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing expressions bounds the
first-order remainder for F_poly between 0 and ||X-Y||^2, so F_poly is
differentiable with gradient P_(C_poly), convex, finite, and 1-smooth.

At each prescribed sample the displayed residuals say
<X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_ji<=0, and
<X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0. Thus the normal condition holds
on every vertex and their convex hull: grad F_poly(X_i)=g_i and
F_poly(X_i)=f_i. The fixed function therefore follows the stated GD.
It is nonnegative and minimized at zero. Its full minimizer set is
{Z:<Z,g_i><=0 for all i}, by the same normal condition at the zero vertex.
The true distance to this polar cone is positive and is the R used in
(3)--(7); no claim equates it with ||X_0||.

## 9. Scope and unresolved changes

The exclusion covers EVERY positive amplitude choice for (1),(2), including
unbounded, history-dependent, nonsaturating, and nonmatching choices, all
anchors and values that actually interpolate, and every m>=1024. It keeps
the B=-1 mass exactly in the return argument and pays only the product
mixture eta=N^-2 there. The separate initial mass proof pays the full
epsilon/2+eta remainder before bounding ANY amplitudes.

The same derivation also covers the exact eta=0 boundary, and epsilon=0
when considered with the same physical word: zero perturbation yields
nonincreasing returns directly. It does not assume positive definiteness
for the upper bound. Positive definiteness is used only for the separate
full-support nonvacuity example.

Different measures and different binary labels remain outside. In
particular the coercive margins 1-2*(7/10)^2 and 1-k*z^2 are essential;
a geometry at or beyond absolute ray correlation 1/sqrt(2) is not covered
by this proof. Nor does a larger arbitrary full-support component come
with a vanishing exact-B_n comparison: the estimate retains its eta term.
No claim is made about arbitrary schedules, free unprescribed terminal
gradients, or the accepted unrestricted finite H6 witness.

Matlas and web searches for long-step cone/length bounds and gradient-norm
monotonicity did not supply an applicable external theorem. The returned
short-step theorems cannot be applied to this whole word. Every short-step
fact actually used is derived above from accepted interpolation. This
remains the already audited finite interpolation regime; no additional
unread body of external theory is a premise.

The result is a full scoped OBSTRUCTION CANDIDATE, not an accepted fact.
The current assignment prohibits fact_submit. The original all-h minimax
target remains unresolved and is not finalized.

# Global clipping-branch exclusion and exact endpoint reduction

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Recovery round authorized by
TASK.md and master_guidance 6c702a3eacae802d. No numerical/model evaluation,
word scan, solver, replay, verifier, installation, or subordinate agent was
used. Ordinary symbolic analysis only. The accepted inputs are
04219554e41d9750 and 1252b8823bb5a290. Appendices reproduce the draft
prerequisites as proof text to be checked jointly, not as accepted premises.

The global four-step seed decision is still OPEN. This document excludes
the WHOLE bc>=1 branch, derives a stronger endpoint bound with a complete
new witness, and reduces a precisely specified necessary subsystem on
bc<1 to explicit interval intersections. It does not assert a survivor of
every scalar test, full coefficient feasibility, or full-domain emptiness.

## 1. Interface and the prerequisite package

Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=5+a+b+c+d,
    P=2+a, Q=3+a+b, D=2+d, H=3+c+d,
    k=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), R=P^2-k,
    eta=b/H, J=R-k*eta.

Appendix A derives, directly from the FULL original seed interface, the
following prerequisite package for every C>=193/25:

    193/25<=C<87/10,
    0<a,d<77/97<4/5,
    0<b,c<1552559/1081453<3/2,
    Ca<=C-1-a, Cb<=PH, Cc<=QD, Cd<=C-1-d,             (1)
    Cabcd<=1, Ccd<=Q,
    ab[1+a(C+1)]<=H,
    bc[R+b(P+C)]<=D*J,
    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]<=1.                              (2)

In particular cd<1. The last inequality has a strictly positive factor
greater than one. Appendix A gives every legal cut function, all three
adjacent triangles, the backward middle lift, the mixed cut, and the
boundary arguments that force all four steps above one. It retains all
twenty original interpolation multipliers and all five formal gradients.

There is a useful exact rewriting of the already-derived mixed cut:

                       C^2*eta^2<=J.                 (3)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1, k=0 and (3) is the second cut in (1).
For a>sqrt(2)-1 put u=1+a in Appendix A equation (20). Dividing that
inequality by a gives

    (u^2+u-1)/a+u(2-u^2)*eta/a-C^2*eta^2
        =a+3+1/a-k*eta-C^2*eta^2=J-C^2*eta^2.

The domain a*eta<1 follows from eta<=P/C and a<4/5,P<14/5,C>7.
In particular J>0, because C,eta>0. Thus no denominator involving J
is being assigned a sign from an unverified numerical observation.

Appendix B derives the exact elimination of ALL lower clipping parameters
for the family f'_alpha(x)=clamp(x,[-alpha,1]), x0=P. It gives

    Cb/H<=P,
    Cbd/(2+c)<=P,
    C|bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
    Cd<=P if bc>=1.                                  (4)

These are necessary, not sufficient for the full seed. Their derivation
uses a single static smooth convex function for each parameter and all
four actual steps. Both terminal clipping branches are included.

## 2. The entire bc>=1 branch is impossible

First observe the uniform identity and bound

    R=(2+a)^2                     if a<=sqrt(2)-1,
    R=a+3+1/a                     if a>=sqrt(2)-1,
    0<R<=3+2sqrt(2)<6.                                  (5)

The first expression increases up to the joining point. The second has
derivative 1-1/a^2<0 for sqrt(2)-1<a<1. Both have value
3+2sqrt(2) at the joining point. The final strict bound follows by
squaring sqrt(2)<3/2.

Suppose bc>=1. The middle inequality in (2) and J<=R imply

    R+b(P+C)<=D*J<=D*R,
    b(P+C)<=(1+d)R<=(1+P/C)R,
    b<=R/C<6/C.                                          (6)

Here the second line uses Cd<=P from (4), valid on this entire branch.
Since bc>=1, (6) forces c>C/6.

On the other hand, P<14/5 and C>7 give d<=P/C<2/5. Thus D<12/5.
Since C>15/2, the ratio z=D/C is strictly less than 8/25. The third
cut in (1), using Q=C-D-c, rearranges to

    c/C<=z(1-z)/(1+z).

The derivative of z(1-z)/(1+z) has numerator 1-2z-z^2, positive
on [0,8/25]. Consequently

    c/C<136/825<1/6,
    1/6-136/825=1/550>0.                                 (7)

This contradicts c>C/6. Therefore EVERY full seed in the entire original
capacity range must satisfy

                              bc<1.                     (8)

No ordering, equality, fixed capacity, symmetry, prefix heredity, or
support restriction was used. The boundary bc=1 is excluded as well.
Only the prerequisites reproduced in Appendices A and B were needed.

## 3. A lifted return witness and its global consequences

The next result is new to this recovery round. It modifies the first
gradient of the partial-tail return witness, rather than changing its
curvature. Thus it is distinct from max3's assigned curvature family.
Throughout the compact domain, every full seed must satisfy

    C^2*b^2*d^2<=R*(2+c)^2-k*b*(2+c).                    (9)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1 this is exactly the second condition in (4).
For a>sqrt(2)-1 the new right side is strictly smaller than
P^2*(2+c)^2. The entire witness and every ordered interpolation pair
are checked next.

Put beta=b/(2+c), let u=(1,0), and suppose a>sqrt(2)-1. The compact
bounds give beta<3/4, a*beta<3/5, and d*beta<3/5. Define

    q=(1+(1-a)*beta)/a,
    z=-beta*u+sqrt(q-beta^2)*(0,1),
    Kproj=conv{u,-beta*u,z}.

The radicand is (1+beta)(1-a*beta)/a>0. The optimum gradient zero
lies in the segment joining u and -beta*u. Use the five samples

    x=(u+(1+a)*z, u, -b*u, -beta*u, d*beta*u),
    g=(z, u, -beta*u, -beta*u, d*beta*u),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and the zero optimum jet. All four GD identities have exactly their
original step lengths: in particular b=(2+c)*beta. Every listed g_i
belongs to Kproj, since 0<d*beta<1.

For these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>. To check every
finite and optimum ordered pair it suffices to check the three vertex
normal inequalities at every target sample. Targets 1,3,4 have x_j=g_j,
so their normal is zero. At target 2 the normal is -(b-beta)u, with
b-beta>0. The comparison with z is zero since <z,u>=-beta; comparison
with u is negative. At target 0 the normal is u+a*z. Since

    <u,z>+a||z||^2=1-a*beta,

the comparison with any t*u, -beta<=t<=1, is exactly

    <t*u-z,u+a*z>=(1-a*beta)(t-1)<=0.

The comparison with z itself is zero. These comparisons include the
zero optimum and the terminal gradient. Hence all original ordered Q
are nonpositive. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes these complete
finite data by one global convex differentiable 1-smooth function with
a minimizer at zero. No pre-existing Gram or omitted pair is used.

The saturated-value telescoping identity from Appendix A and accepted
04219554e41d9750 gives

    V=||x0||^2-C^2*d^2*beta^2,
    ||x0||^2=1-2(1+a)*beta+(1+a)^2*q
             =P^2-k*(1+beta)=R-k*beta.

Thus V>=0 implies (9). When a<=sqrt(2)-1, the original scalar witness
has x0=P and proves the same formula with k=0. This proves (9) on
the whole compact domain, including the joining point.

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). Equations (5) and (9) imply the uniform return
bound Cbd<=lambda*(2+c). On the remaining branch bc<1, (4) also gives
Cbcd<=P, while the full quadratic gives Cbcd<=1/a. The elementary
function min(2+a,1/a) is at most lambda: its increasing and decreasing
branches meet only at a=sqrt(2)-1. Therefore

    Cbcd<=lambda,
    Cbd<=lambda*min(2+c,1/c)<=lambda^2.                 (10)

The last inequality follows from the same increasing/decreasing
comparison in c. In addition, (3) gives

                             Cb<=lambda*H.             (11)

These are globally valid necessary bounds on the full threshold seed
domain. They are weaker than retaining (9), (3), and the actual a,c.
They do not prove that the total capacity is below 193/25.

## 4. Exact last-endpoint elimination for a specified necessary core

Keep C,a,b free. Put

    Ksum=C-5-a-b, H=3+Ksum, Q=3+a+b, P=2+a,
    eta=b/H, J=R-k*eta,
    A=b*(R+b*(P+C))/J,
    y=Cb/P, m=max(1,a*P), r=(C-1)/(C+1),
    Bprod=min(1/(m*y),Q/C).

Assume the compact C,a,b bounds from Section 1, Ksum>0, and the
constraints independent of d:

    a<=r, Cb<=PH, ab[1+a(C+1)]<=H,
    C^2*eta^2<=J.                                      (12)

Then J>0 and every displayed denominator is positive. Define

    Lcut=(C*Ksum-2Q)/(C+Q),
    Ltri=(A*Ksum-2)/(A+1),
    Uret=(Ksum+2)/(1+y),
    L=max(0,Ksum-1/b,Lcut,Ltri),
    U=min(Ksum,r,Uret),
    I={d: L<=d<=U, 0<d<Ksum, d>Ksum-1/b}.             (13)

The following EXACT equivalence concerns the stated necessary core:
with c=Ksum-d, the positivity conditions c,d>0, bc<1, all four
pointwise cuts, the first and lifted-middle triangles, the mixed first
cut (3), the suffix product, full unit quadratic, and the entire lower
clipping family (4) hold if and only if (12) holds and

                      d in I, d*(Ksum-d)<=Bprod.        (14)

The compact uniform bounds on c,d also follow from these pointwise cuts
and the fixed capacity range, as in Appendix A. Equivalence (14) does
NOT include the last adjacent triangle, C82/C83, or the other repaired
triangles. Those are retained below as additional conditions.

For clarity, each step of the elimination is an equality-preserving
rearrangement. The third cut Cc<=Q(2+d) is d>=Lcut. The lifted middle
triangle is A*c<=2+d, namely d>=Ltri. The last endpoint cut is d<=r.
The second return test Cbd<=P(2+c) is d<=Uret. Positivity gives 0<d<Ksum,
and bc<1 gives d>Ksum-1/b. The suffix test is cd<=Q/C. The full
quadratic and the third condition in (4), now with bc<1, are respectively
cd<=1/(Cab) and cd<=P/(Cb); their minimum is 1/(m*y).
The first clipping condition is exactly the second cut. Since Q<C,
Bprod<1; thus (14) itself supplies cd<1, the prerequisite needed for
the complete lower-clipping elimination in Appendix B. No clipping
regime is silently assumed in the converse direction.

This gives an exact endpoint set without evaluating an inadmissible
endpoint. Let Delta=Ksum^2-4Bprod. If Delta<=0, every d in I satisfies
the product condition. If Delta>0 define

    d_minus=(Ksum-sqrt(Delta))/2,
    d_plus =(Ksum+sqrt(Delta))/2.

Then the complete set in (14) is

    [I intersect (0,d_minus]] union [I intersect [d_plus,Ksum)]. (15)

All open and closed boundaries in (13) and (15) are intentional.
The excluded equality bc=1 is not reintroduced by taking a closure.

To retain the last adjacent triangle as well, put

    v=(Q+C)/Q^2,
    Tlast(d)=d*(Ksum-d)*(1+v*(Ksum-d)),
    d_peak=(1+2v*Ksum-sqrt(1+v*Ksum+v^2*Ksum^2))/(3v).

The polynomial Tlast increases strictly from zero to its unique maximum
at d_peak in (0,Ksum/2), then decreases strictly to zero at Ksum.
Indeed its derivative is

    Ksum-2d+v*(Ksum^2-4Ksum*d+3d^2).

This quadratic has the displayed smaller root and a larger root greater
than Ksum: for the latter assertion,
1+sqrt(1+v*Ksum+v^2*Ksum^2)>v*Ksum. Its derivative at zero is positive
and its value at Ksum/2 is -v*Ksum^2/4<0. These facts prove all the
stated locations and monotonicity.

If Tlast(d_peak)<=1, this triangle removes no endpoint. Otherwise let
t_minus and t_plus be the unique two roots of Tlast(d)=1 in
(0,d_peak) and (d_peak,Ksum), respectively. Intersect (15) with

                       (0,t_minus] union [t_plus,Ksum). (16)

This is a finite exact algebraic interval description of the full
specified core INCLUDING all three adjacent triangles. It can have more
than one component; no component may be discarded on ordering grounds.

## 5. The new return bound also eliminates to one explicit endpoint

Equation (9) strengthens only the upper endpoint in Section 4. Put

    vret=k*b/(Ksum+2),
    zret=(sqrt(vret^2+4C^2*b^2*(R-vret))-vret)/(2C^2*b^2),
    Ulift=(Ksum+2)*zret/(1+zret).                     (17)

Here R-vret>0. For k=0 this is immediate. For k>0, repeat the explicit
Section 3 vector construction with beta=b/(Ksum+2)<3/4. Its initial
norm squared is R-vret, and its fresh coordinate is strictly positive
because a*beta<3/5. This argument establishes the algebraic sign; it
does not suppose that a different schedule is primitive.

For 0<d<Ksum set z=d/(Ksum+2-d), a strictly increasing positive
function of d. Divide (9) by (Ksum+2-d)^2. It becomes exactly

                C^2*b^2*z^2+vret*z+(vret-R)<=0.

This polynomial is strictly increasing for z>0, has negative constant
term, and has the single positive root zret. Thus (9) is equivalent
to d<=Ulift. In (13), replace Uret by Ulift. If k=0, this is the
old bound Uret. If k>0, Ulift<Uret because at every positive d the new
return test is strictly stronger, and the old equality gives a strict
violation of the new test. All other interval components remain.

## 6. What the two global attempts do and do not settle

Direct attempt one tried to make the entire interval (13) empty from
the middle lift and return bounds. The exact comparison is

    Ksum*(A*y-1)<=2*(A+y+2),

but it is only one necessary comparison. In particular it is not an
equivalence after Lcut, the positivity boundaries, and r are restored.
No global sign proof over C,a,b has been found for this comparison or
for the stronger Ulift comparison.

Direct attempt two retained product concavity and the last triangle.
It yields the exact interval unions (15)--(16), but not a proof that
both components are empty for every C,a,b. Keeping just one component,
or replacing its open endpoint by an attained minimizer, is invalid.
An exact one-variable counterexample to that shortcut is

    Ksum=2, d in (1/2,1], Bprod=3/4.

The product equals Bprod at the excluded left endpoint, whereas for
every actual d in the interval,

    d(2-d)-3/4=(d-1/2)(3/2-d)>0.

Thus checking a closed-interval endpoint equality would falsely report
feasibility. This is a counterexample to a proof shortcut, not a seed
proposal or a candidate schedule evaluation.

The old h(t) family is already excluded by the base return test. It is
not a counterexample to emptiness of the current system. No new word
has been scanned or asserted to satisfy every necessary test.

The unresolved exact interface is still the following: for some free
C,a,b in the compact domain, a d in EVERY required surviving interval,
c=Ksum-d, obeying all the earlier repaired-cut, lifted-last-triangle,
C82/C83, and all-curvature conditions (and any new independent max3
conditions), construct twenty nonnegative Lambda_ij, i!=j in {0,...,4},
with diagonal zero, exact value flow and the full ambient matrix PSD:

    s=C-1, e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    L^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    M=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2))
        +(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0 on all of R^5.             (18)

Alternatively exclude that ENTIRE remaining parameter set by a joint
proof. Neither (18) nor a global exclusion is supplied here. Even a
complete four-step no-seed result would close this sufficient negative
route only, and would not establish the original positive exact-profile
minimax assertion. The original W=2R quantifiers remain unchanged.

## Appendix A. Complete earlier joint compact-domain and triangle proofs

The following prior proof text is included in full so that none of its
new mathematical claims is adopted as an accepted fact. Its historical
round/status remarks are historical only. Local section and equation
numbers belong to that appendix, and the current unresolved interface
is Section 6 above.

# Full four-step seed: a compact domain from legal cut functions

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, not verifier-accepted. This round has no
numerical or fact_submit allowance. No unverified finding is used as a premise.
The accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the interpolation convention Q<=0;
the particular functions and all algebra below are derived explicitly.

## 1. Interface and energy

Let h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3)>=0, s=sum h_i, C=1+s>=193/25.
Indices i,j range over0,...,4. Set p_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t, so p_0=1,p_4=C.
Let e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T. Define T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,T_it=0 for t>i,
Lambda_ii=0,Lambda_ij>=0,L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T, and
D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).
The full interface is

    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)

No multiplier support, rank, remainder, symmetry, or step order is fixed.
For arbitrary formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      -s(s+1)||g_4||^2.

Put delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 and y_i=x_i-g_i=x_0-(Tg)_i.
Then Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>, while
V=-omega^T delta+sum_i D_ii||g_i||^2. Value flow in(1) therefore gives

    V+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>. (2)

In particular every feasible full certificate implies V>=0 on every legal
convex differentiable1-smooth trajectory with a minimizer. This direction
requires neither certificate necessity nor any duality assertion.

## 2. Legal cut witnesses at every position

Fix j<4, put b=h_j,P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i, and first assume b>1.
Set a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0. Define the scalar function

    f(x)=x-1/2                     if x>=1,
         x^2/2                     if -a<=x<=1,
         -a*x-a^2/2                if x<=-a.

Its derivative is clamp(x,[-a,1]), which is continuous, nondecreasing,
and1-Lipschitz. The function is convex1-smooth and has a minimizer at0.
Start at x_0=P+1. Before and at j all gradients are1, and x_j=1.
After step j, x_(j+1)=1-b=-a(R+1). All subsequent gradients equal-a,
since the remaining nonnegative steps move monotonically to x_4=-a.
Every sampled value obeys f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and x_4=g_4.

For any vector-valued data obeying these two identities, GD telescoping gives

    2sum_(i<4)h_i f_i+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      =||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2,
    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                         (3)

Applied to this scalar function, nonnegative V implies

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+P)(1+R), for every j=0,...,3.      (4)

If h_j<=1 the same inequality is automatic. Thus(4) holds on every
zero-step boundary too. Failure of any inequality is a specific legal
negative-V function, hence rules out the full interface(1).

## 3. Prefix bounds and compact capacity

For fixed C>1 define the increasing rational map

    R_C(z)=1+2Cz/(C+z), z>=0,
    r_k=R_C iterated k times at1, k=0,1,2,3.

Rearrangement of(4) gives p_(i+1)<=R_C(p_i). Conversely this inequality
is equivalent to(4) on the nonnegative schedule domain. Induction yields
p_i<=r_i. The cut constraints are invariant under reversing the four
steps, regardless of whether the original certificate admits reversal.
Applying the same induction to the reversed cut inequalities gives

    p_i >= C+1-r_(4-i).                               (5)

Only the cases i=1,2,3 are needed; r_0=1. Explicitly,

    r_1=(3C+1)/(C+1),
    r_2=(7C^2+6C+1)/(C^2+4C+1),
    r_3=(15C^3+23C^2+9C+1)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1).          (6)

Taking i=2 in(5) and p_2<=r_2 gives C+1<=2r_2, hence

    C^3-9C^2-7C-1<=0.                                (7)

This polynomial is strictly increasing for C>=7: its derivative
3C^2-18C-7 is positive there and increasing. At C=39/4 its value is
131/64>0. Since C>=193/25>7, we conclude

    193/25 <= C < 39/4.                               (8)

In particular this argument covers all larger candidate sums; it did not
replace the target by the slice sum h=168/25.

## 4. All four steps are uniformly positive and bounded

The first and last cut inequalities give

    h_0,h_3 <= 2C/(C+1) < 78/43.                      (9)

For the lower bounds, subtract the upper prefix bound from the lower
bound for the next prefix in(5). This gives

    h_0,h_3 >= C-r_3 = N(C)/(C^3+11C^2+7C+1),
    h_1,h_2 >= C+1-r_1-r_2 = N(C)/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)), (10)
    N(C)=C^4-4C^3-16C^2-8C-1.

The endpoint lower bound exceeds1/2 exactly when

    A(C)=2C^4-9C^3-43C^2-23C-3>0.

The middle lower bound exceeds4/5 exactly when

    B(C)=5C^4-24C^3-100C^2-60C-9>0.

At C=77/10 these polynomials respectively equal1922412/10000 and
2197285/10000. They are strictly increasing for C>=7: their derivatives
are bounded below respectively by29C^2-86C-23 and68C^2-200C-60,
both positive and increasing on that interval. Since193/25>77/10,

    h_0,h_3>1/2, h_1,h_2>4/5.                         (11)

For completeness, the middle upper bound uses the same cuts, without
assuming either middle step exceeds1. Write

    G_C(z)=R_C(z)-z=1+z(C-z)/(C+z).

For1<=z<=r_1<3 its derivative is
(C^2-2Cz-z^2)/(C+z)^2>0: its numerator is at least C^2-6C-9,
which is positive for C>=15/2. Thus h_1<=G_C(p_1)<=G_C(r_1), and
the reversed cut constraints give the same bound for h_2. Calculation yields

    G_C(r_1)=4C^3/((C+1)(C^2+4C+1)).                  (12)

The right side is strictly increasing in C>0: its logarithmic derivative
has positive numerator5C^2+10C+3 after multiplication by positive
denominators. At39/4 its value is237276/92923<13/5, with the difference
21619/464615. Consequently

    1/2<h_0,h_3<78/43, 4/5<h_1,h_2<13/5.             (13)

This excludes every zero-step boundary for this threshold by actual
witnesses. It does not assert h_i>1, ordering, or reversal of certificates.

## 5. Full multiplier compactness from the common-gradient kernel

This step bounds all20 original multipliers, including every cycle.
The vector p=Te has entries p_i. Since

    sum_i D_ii=-s^2-sum_(i<4)h_i^2,
    omega^T p=s^2+sum_(i<4)h_i^2,

equations(1) give e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0.
For clarity this implication follows by inserting e+t v into the quadratic
form: a nonzero linear coefficient would violate nonnegativity for one
sign of sufficiently small t. Calculation of each component gives

    2De+T^T omega=-diag(p)omega,
    Lp=diag(p)omega.                                  (14)

Define A_ij=Lambda_ij p_j for i!=j and A_ii=0. Since omega_i=out_i-in_i,
equation(14) says sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i in_i. Therefore

    sum_j A_ij=sum_j A_ji for every i.                 (15)

Thus A is a nonnegative circulation. Write c_i=1/p_i. Substituting
Lambda_ij=A_ij c_j into the original value flow and using(15) gives

    sum_i c_i omega_i
      =sum_(i,j) A_ij(c_i c_j-c_i^2)
      =-1/2 sum_(i,j)A_ij(c_i-c_j)^2.

Equivalently, the entire circulation has the fixed Dirichlet energy

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij (p_i-p_j)^2/(p_i^2 p_j)
       =4[sum_(i<4)h_i/p_i-s/C] <4s<35.              (16)

All steps exceed1/2 by(13). Consequently |p_i-p_j|>1/2 for i!=j.
Also every p_i<10 by(8), so p_i^2 p_j<1000. Every coefficient of
Lambda_ij on the left of(16) exceeds1/4000. Hence

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij < 140000.                      (17)

This is a bound on the complete multiplier vector, not a choice of sparse
support. No skew freedom or PSD remainder is fixed. In particular all
possible solutions of(1) at the requested threshold belong to the closed
bounded box obtained by replacing the strict inequalities in(8),(13),(17)
by non-strict ones. The equations and PSD conditions are continuous, so
their solution set in this box is compact (possibly empty).

## 6. A suffix witness and a full two-coordinate mixed witness

The first new constraint is

    C |(1-h_2)(1-h_3)| <= 1+h_0+h_1.                  (18)

To prove it, take the1-smooth convex function with derivative min(x,1),
whose values are x^2/2 for x<=1 and x-1/2 for x>=1. Start at
x_0=1+h_0+h_1. The first two gradients are1, x_2=1, x_3=1-h_2<=1,
and x_4=(1-h_2)(1-h_3). Every sample satisfies f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.
Without imposing x_4=g_4, the same telescoping calculation as(3) gives

    V=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                     (19)

If x_4<=1, then g_4=x_4 and(19) is precisely(18). If x_4>1,
g_4=1 and x_4+s>C>x_0, since h_2+h_3>0 by(13), giving V<0.
This latter case is excluded, proving(18) for every candidate.

Here is a second necessary inequality with its complete interpolation
proof. Suppose u=h_0>1, b=h_1>1, put a=u-1,A=1+u,
R=h_2+h_3,D_tail=1+R, eta=(b-1)/D_tail, and assume a*eta<1.
Then every feasible seed satisfies

    H_mix(u,C,eta)=u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta
                   -(u-1)C^2 eta^2 >=0.             (20)

Take the base cut data from Section2 with j=1. Its gradients are1
at i=0,1 and-eta at i=2,3,4; its initial point is A and its final
point and gradient are-eta. Its proximal points y_i=x_i-g_i are
y_0=u,y_1=0 and y_i=-eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Its energy is F_base=A^2-C^2 eta^2.

Define auxiliary scalar data by x_0=g_0=1, x_1=g_1=-a,
g_i=a*eta for i>=2, and actual GD recursion for x_i. Thus
x_2=a(b-1)=a*eta*D_tail, x_4=g_4=a*eta; all earlier auxiliary
proximal points are0 and y_i=a*eta*sum_(t=i)^3 h_t for i>=2.
Assign f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, and include the zero optimum jet.

For any data with this value assignment, direct expansion gives
Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)y_j. Optimum residuals are Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-g_i y_i. All auxiliary optimum residuals are nonpositive.
The only positive auxiliary finite residuals are, for j=2,3,

    Q_aux,(0,j)=(1-a*eta)a*eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

Every other finite residual is nonpositive: target j=0,1,4 has y_j=0;
two post-cut gradients agree; the pre-cut gradient-a is smaller than
a*eta. The corresponding base residuals are

    Q_base,(0,j)=-(1+eta)eta*sum_(t=j)^3 h_t.

With epsilon=(1+eta)/(a(1-a*eta))>0, take the orthogonal sum of the
base and sqrt(epsilon) times the auxiliary positions and gradients,
and the sum of their function values with weight epsilon. All Q add;
these two possible positive residuals cancel, and every other one remains
nonpositive. The full finite array, including the optimum, is realized
by one globally convex1-smooth function by fact1252b8823bb5a290.
Its energy is

    F_base+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 eta^2)
       = u*H_mix(u,C,eta)/(a(1-a*eta)).                (21)

This equality follows by expanding both sides and using
1-a^2 A^2=u^2(2-u^2). All denominators are positive, proving(20).
Every original directed interpolation pair is included in this argument.

## 7. Both middle steps are strictly greater than one

For this section write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1.
The previously proved bounds imply

    a,d<9/11; b,c<14/9; a+b+c+d=C-5>=68/25.           (22)

Indeed78/43<20/11 and237276/92923<23/9; the latter difference is
1745/836307. These bounds allow negative a,b,c,d initially.

First suppose h_1<=1, so b<=0. Then

    c+d >=68/25-a-b >68/25-9/11=523/275.

As c,d<14/9 and523/275>14/9, both c and d are positive.
The identity (14/9-c)(14/9-d)>0 implies

    cd > (14/9)(523/275-14/9)=11998/22275.

But(18) requires Ccd<=3+a+b<42/11. This is impossible, since

    (193/25)(11998/22275)-42/11=189364/556875>0.

Hence h_1>1.

Next suppose h_2<=1, so c<=0. From(22),
b>=68/25-a-d>68/25-18/11>0, so the second-step cut is available.
Its inequality is

    Cb <= (2+a)(3+c+d).

If a<=3/5, its left side is at least
(193/25)(68/25-3/5-9/11), while its right side is at most
(2+3/5)(3+9/11). The first exceeds the second by844/6875>0.
Thus a>3/5 and u=h_0 lies in(8/5,20/11).

Let C_0=193/25,D_0=42/11 and

    eta=b/(3+c+d)=(C-1-u)/(3+c+d)-1,
    eta_0(u)=(C_0-1-u)/D_0-1.

The denominator3+c+d is positive and at most D_0, while C-1-u>0.
Therefore eta>=eta_0(u)>0 on this interval. The cut itself yields

    a*eta <= a(1+u)/C=(u^2-1)/C<1.

Consequently the mixed necessary inequality(20) applies. Since
u>=8/5>sqrt(2), its coefficient u(2-u^2) is negative and u-1>0.
H_mix decreases as eta>0 increases and as C>0 increases. Thus

    0 <= H_mix(u,C,eta) <= H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u)).       (23)

The final expression is a rational quartic in u. Put
u=8/5+(20/11-8/5)z for0<=z<=1, and define
B_(4,k)(z)=binomial(4,k)z^k(1-z)^(4-k). Direct multiplication of the
displayed definition of H_mix gives

    H_mix(u,C_0,eta_0(u))=sum_(k=0)^4 beta_k B_(4,k)(z),

where the exact coefficients in increasing k are

    -124646528/95703125,
    -3776352043/3158203125,
    -1682896352/1654296875,
    -98764097577/127380859375,
    -36497765912/76428515625.

All are strictly negative. The Bernstein basis terms are nonnegative
and sum to1; hence this quartic is strictly negative throughout the
whole interval, contradicting(23). This proves

    h_1>1 and h_2>1.                                  (24)

No conclusion h_0>1 or h_3>1 has been assumed or obtained here.
The fixed quartic expansion is small exact algebra, not a numerical
parameter scan or a solver certificate.

## 8. General cut/quadratic repair and two additional tests

The mixed construction has a useful full-pair version at either middle
cut, retaining the entire quadratic prefix. Fix j in{1,2}, let h_j>1,
P=sum_(i<j)h_i,R=sum_(i>j)h_i,eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R), and define

    z_0=1,z_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for1<=i<=j,
    q=-eta*z_j,
    beta=max(0,max_(0<=i<=j) q(z_i-q)).

Then another necessary inequality is

    beta*((1+P)^2-C^2 eta^2)
       +(1+eta)eta*(1-C^2 q^2) >=0.                  (25)

Here is a joint derivation, including beta=0. Use the cut function of
Section2 as the base. For auxiliary data set x_i=g_i=z_i for i<=j,
set g_i=q for i>j, and follow GD. The first post-cut point is
x_(j+1)=q(1+R), and x_4=q. All auxiliary proximal points before or
at j are0; later they equal q times the remaining step sum. Assign
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 and include the zero optimum. The optimum residuals
are nonpositive. A possible positive finite residual has source i<=j
and target k>j and equals q(z_i-q) times that remaining step sum.
The corresponding base residual is-(1+eta)eta times that sum.

If beta>0, multiply the auxiliary Gram/values by
epsilon=(1+eta)eta/beta and orthogonally add to the base. Every original
residual is nonpositive; fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives one legal function.
Its energy is the left side of(25) divided by beta, proving the inequality.
If beta=0 the auxiliary data alone are legal, and their energy is
1-C^2 q^2. This proves(25) in this case too, without division by zero.
No Gram from a different schedule was reused.

Full-curvature quadratic data give the additional necessary condition

    C*|product_(i=0)^3(1-h_i)| <=1.                   (26)

This is the actual function f(x)=x^2/2 started at1; equation(3) applies.

There is also a nonlinear scalar condition that is stronger than merely
imposing(26). Since both middle steps exceed1 by Section7, put
q_mid=(h_1-1)(h_2-1)>0. Then

    C*|q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1)| <=1+h_0.              (27)

Use again the function with derivative min(x,1), this time started at
x_0=1+h_0. Its first step reaches1; the next two points are
x_2=1-h_1 and x_3=q_mid. Thus x_4=q_mid-h_3*min(q_mid,1).
All values have the saturated form, so(19) holds. If x_4>1 then
x_4+s>C>1+h_0 and V<0. Otherwise g_4=x_4 and(19) gives(27).
The inequality in(27) itself forces |x_4|<1, because 1+h_0<C.

The cuts yield a quantitative endpoint-sum constraint as well. Put
A=1+h_0,D_end=1+h_3,E_end=A+D_end and b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1.
Rearranging the cuts at1 and2 gives

    b<=A(C-A)/(C+A), c<=D_end(C-D_end)/(C+D_end).

The function z(C-z)/(C+z) has second derivative-4C^2/(C+z)^3<0.
Adding these two inequalities and using concavity at their midpoint yields

    C-E_end-1=b+c<=E_end*(2C-E_end)/(2C+E_end),
    E_end >= 2C(C-1)/(3C+1).                         (28)

In particular at least one endpoint exceeds1. Indeed if both were at
most1 then E_end<=4, and(28) would imply C^2-7C-2<=0. That polynomial
is positive at193/25 (value2224/625) and strictly increasing thereafter.
Thus a remaining candidate has three or four steps>1; the possible step
at most1 can only be one endpoint. No endpoint has been deleted.

## 9. A sharper capacity cap from cut products and the actual quadratic

The preceding constraints improve the capacity bound further to

    193/25<=C<87/10.                                 (29)

For a contradiction suppose C>=87/10. Define the following scalar
functions (distinct from the matrix and interpolation notation):

    d_cap(C)=(C+1)(C^2+4C+1),
    r_cap(C)=(C-1)/(C+1),
    t_cap(C)=4C^3/d_cap(C)-1,
    ell_cap(C)=2C(C-1)/(3C+1)-4,
    k_cap(C)=C-5-2r_cap(C).

Write a=h_0-1,b=h_1-1,c=h_2-1,d=h_3-1 as before. Equations(9),(12),(28)
give a,d<=r_cap, b,c<=t_cap, a+d>=ell_cap, and b+c>=k_cap.
Multiplication of the nonnegative factors r_cap-a,r_cap-d and
t_cap-b,t_cap-c yields

    ad>=r_cap*(ell_cap-r_cap),
    bc>=t_cap*(k_cap-t_cap).                         (30)

All four factors on the right are positive and increasing for C>=87/10.
Here are detailed sign bounds. Clearly r_cap>0,r_cap'>0 there, and
t_cap'>0 follows from

    t_cap'=4C^2(5C^2+10C+3)/d_cap(C)^2.

For C>=8, d_cap(C)>C^3 gives t_cap'<403/1024, while
k_cap'=1-4/(C+1)^2>=77/81; hence k_cap-t_cap is strictly increasing.
Also

    ell_cap'=(6C^2+4C-2)/(3C+1)^2>384/625,
    r_cap'=2/(C+1)^2<=2/81

for C>=8, proving ell_cap-r_cap is strictly increasing. The first lower
bound uses3C+1<=(25/8)C and6C^2+4C-2>6C^2. At C=87/10 the exact
values of r_cap,t_cap,ell_cap-r_cap,k_cap-t_cap are respectively

    77/97,
    1552559/1081453,
    19728/131435,
    7318711/10814530,

which are all positive. Positivity in(30) also forces a,d,b,c positive:
their pairwise sums are positive, and their pairwise products are positive.
Consequently the full-curvature constraint(26) requires C*ad*bc<=1.
But the increasing lower bound from(30), evaluated at87/10, exceeds1 by

    9782467331765167/1375525927835685125>0.

This proves(29) for every larger sum, without assuming saturation in(26).

The monotonic formulas(9),(12) now refine the step upper bounds to

    1/2<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (31)

Finally p_i<9 and4s<154/5 in the exact circulation identity(16). Its
coefficients still exceed1/(4*9^3)=1/2916, so

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<449064/5<90000.               (32)

Thus(29),(31),(32) supersede the earlier coarse compact box; the sharper
pointwise rational cut bounds remain available inside that box.

## 10. Why the clipped three-step test cannot be omitted

An exact scalar example separates(27) from the preceding cuts, the
full-curvature test, the suffix pair test, and the two middle-cut repairs.
It is an explicitly constructed counterexample to the sufficiency of
that smaller necessary-condition list, not a candidate seed.

Take

    h=(1,217/100,41/20,3/2), s=168/25, C=193/25.

All four cuts(4) hold. At j=0 the left side is0. At j=1 the two sides
are22581/2500 and91/10; at j=2 they are4053/500 and417/40;
at j=3 they are193/50 and311/50. The suffix pair constraint(18)
has left side4053/1000 and right side417/100. The full-curvature
product in(26) vanishes. At both middle cuts the auxiliary prefix
product z_j is zero since h_0=1, so q=beta=0 and(25) is positive.
The first mixed test(20) requires h_0>1 and does not apply here.

Nevertheless the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at2,
has the exact trajectory

    x=(2,1,-117/100,2457/2000,-543/2000),
    g=(1,1,-117/100,1,-543/2000).

Its energy equals

    V=4-(104799/50000)^2<0,

because104799>100000. This is precisely a violation of(27), arising
from the return to the affine part at x_3>1. It demonstrates the need
to retain nonlinear chronological transitions even after compactness,
original coefficient signs, and full-curvature screening are imposed.

## 11. Adjacent triangle witness, rederived in this joint argument

The shared UNVERIFIED C80 finding7b447329aac48edd suggested this construction.
It is awareness only: the geometry, every interpolation normal, and the
energy identity needed here are rederived in this section. The only fact
used for realization is1252b8823bb5a290.

Write h=(a,b,c,d), where b,c>1 and a,d>=0. Define

    P=a+1, B=b-1, E=c-1, D=d+1,
    U=b+c+d, C=P+U.

First suppose D>B*E and set

    Kappa=B^2*E+U*(D-B*E), t=B^2*E/Kappa,
    u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B*u+D*w)/c.

Kappa>0 and0<t<1. The convex hull of u,v,w contains0, since
B*u+c*v+D*w=0 with all three coefficients positive. Set

    x=(P*u,u,-B*u,D*w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and include the zero optimum jet. The four GD identities follow from
c*v=-B*u-D*w and D=d+1. Direct inner products give

    ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
    <u,v>=-(B+D*t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)*t/c.

All sampled interpolation inequalities follow by checking that x_i-g_i
is an outward normal to this triangle at g_i. At i=0 that normal is
a*u, with nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u. At i=1,4 it is zero.
At i=2 put y=-B*u-v. Expanding the displayed inner products gives

    <w-v,y>=(-B^2*E+Kappa*t)/c^2=0,
    <u-v,y>=-B*(1-t)*E/c<0.

For the first expansion, the coefficient of t is
D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B*c^2, which equals Kappa after substituting
E=c-1,U=B+c+D. For the second expansion the difference between the
two sides, multiplied by c^2, is-B^2*E+Kappa*t=0. At i=3 the normal
d*w pairs with u-w to zero and with v-w to
-d*t*(1+(B+D)/c)<=0. This exhausts the vertex comparisons. As0 is in
their convex hull, it also exhausts the optimum comparisons.

Indeed for these assigned values Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>, so all the
original ordered Q are nonpositive, Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)<=0.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes one global convex1-smooth function with
a minimizer at0 and precisely these GD data. The terminal x_4=g_4=w,
so(3) gives V=P^2-C^2*t. A direct expansion gives

    C^2 B^2 E-P^2 Kappa
       =U*{B*E[P^2+B(P+C)]-P^2 D}.

Consequently feasible full certificates must satisfy

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2] <= D.                         (34)

This condition applies without imposing D>B*E in advance. If instead
D<=B*E, the legal function with derivative min(x,1), started at P,
has x=(P,1,-B,B*E,B*E-D+1) and g=(1,1,-B,1,1). Its final point
is at least1, and(19) gives

    V=P^2-[P+(B+1)(E+1)]^2<0.

Thus the complementary regime is impossible, proving(34) for every
candidate with b,c>1. The inequality itself implies D>B*E. No previous
word's Gram, an assumed primitive subword, or a numerical proposal has
been adopted in this argument.

The same construction proves(34) at any adjacent pair of steps>1, with
P=1+the sum of all earlier steps, D=1+the sum of all later steps,
B=the first selected step minus1, E=the second selected step minus1,
and C=1+the sum of the whole word. Before the selected pair every
gradient is u and each iterate is(1+the remaining prefix sum)u; its
proximal point is a nonnegative multiple of u, so the same two vertex
normal comparisons hold. After the selected pair every gradient is w
and each iterate is(1+the remaining suffix sum)w, with the same
nonnegative-multiple normal argument. All original intermediate samples
and all directed Q are thus retained. The initial point is P*u and the
terminal point is w, giving the same energy and(34). This paragraph
justifies applying the construction to the first pair as well as the
middle pair of the four-step word, without assuming either unselected
block is primitive.

## 12. The first endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose for a contradiction h_0<=1. The endpoint-sum consequence(28)
forces h_3>1. Since h_0-1<=0 and h_3-1<=r_cap(C), the same consequence
forces ell_cap(C)<=r_cap(C), or2C^3-15C^2-16C-3<=0. This polynomial
equals11/2 at17/2 and its derivative is positive for C>=7. Therefore
C<17/2. In the notation of Section11,

    1<P<=2, 2<D<3, B=h_1-1>0, E=h_2-1>0.

Rearranging the second-step cut and using monotonicity in P<=2 gives

    B<=P(C-P)/(C+P)<=2(C-2)/(C+2)<26/21.             (35)

The derivative with respect to P of P(C-P)/(C+P) has numerator
C^2-2CP-P^2>0 on C>=193/25,P<=2; the last expression increases in C.
Also(31) gives E<1552559/1081453<23/16, with the latter positive
difference32475/17303248. Thus both B and E are less than23/16.

We first show D<13/5. The suffix-pair inequality(18) becomes

    E <= (C-D)/(1+C(D-2)),                            (36)

since P+1+B=C-D-E and D>2. By the capacity identity and(35),

    E=C-P-D-1-B >= H(C)-D-1,
    H(C)=C(C-2)/(C+2).

Therefore feasibility requires

    F_sep(C,D)=H(C)-D-1-(C-D)/(1+C(D-2))<=0.           (37)

For C>=193/25 and13/5<=D<=3, this separation expression strictly
increases with C. Indeed H'=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81, while the derivative
of the final rational term with respect to C is
(D-1)^2/[1+C(D-2)]^2<=25/169; their difference is positive.
For C_0=193/25 the expression is concave in D: its second derivative
is-2C_0(C_0-1)^2/[1+C_0(D-2)]^3<0. Its endpoint values are

    F_sep(C_0,13/5)=2269/66825>0,
    F_sep(C_0,3)=1166/662175>0.

Concavity puts its value above the chord joining these positive values.
It is therefore positive throughout this interval, contradicting(37).
This proves D<13/5.

Now B+E=C-P-D-1>53/25. Since B,E<23/16, multiplication of
(23/16-B)(23/16-E)>0 yields

    B*E>6279/6400, B>273/400.

For P<=2 and C>=193/25, (P+C)/P^2>=243/100; the expression decreases
with P>0 and increases with C. Consequently

    B*E*[1+B(P+C)/P^2]
       >(6279/6400)*(106339/40000)
       =13/5+2102581/256000000 >D,

contradicting the full triangle condition(34). Hence

    h_0>1.                                           (38)

Together with Section7, the first THREE steps exceed1. No reversal of
the schedule or symmetry of the certificate was used.

## 13. The last endpoint also exceeds one

Suppose now h_3<=1. Set alpha=h_0-1>0,b=h_1-1>0,c=h_2-1>0,
delta=h_3-1<=0. As in Section12, the endpoint-sum bound gives C<17/2.
The last-middle cut and the capacity identity imply

    c<=2(C-2)/(C+2),
    b=C-5-alpha-delta-c>=L(C,alpha),
    L(C,alpha)=C-7-alpha+8/(C+2).

Apply the adjacent triangle(34) to the first two steps, with prefix

    b<=J(C,alpha),
    J(C,alpha)=(C-2-alpha)/(1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1)).       (39)

We claim alpha<7/10. If7/10<=alpha<77/97<4/5, put
R=1+alpha+alpha^2(C+1) and N=C-2-alpha. On the rectangle
193/25<=C<=17/2,7/10<=alpha<=4/5 one has R>5 and

    J_C=[1+alpha+alpha^2(3+alpha)]/R^2<1/5,
    L_C=1-8/(C+2)^2>73/81.

Thus L-J increases with C. It also increases with alpha. Indeed
J_alpha=-(R+N R_alpha)/R^2, while L_alpha=-1. Here

    R<=197/25, N>=123/25, R_alpha>=1651/125,
    N R_alpha-R^2>=9028/3125>0.

Hence-J_alpha>1 and(L-J)_alpha>0. Finally the exact lower corner value is

    L(193/25,7/10)-J(193/25,7/10)=58247/22677975>0.

This contradicts(39), proving alpha<7/10.

For the middle adjacent pair now put P=1+h_0=2+alpha<27/10,
D=1+h_3<=2, and x=b+c=C-P-D-1. Then

    x>101/50, c<26/21,
    821/1050<b<783/560,
    (P+C)/P^2>=1042/729.                             (40)

The lower bound on b is x-c. For the upper bound use the cut
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P); this function increases in P<=27/10 and C>=193/25
on the current rectangle, and its value at(P,C)=(27/10,17/2) is783/560.
For its P derivative the numerator C^2-2CP-P^2 is positive at the
worst corner(C,P)=(193/25,27/10) and increases with C and decreases
with P; the C derivative is2P^2/(C+P)^2>0. The final inequality in(40)
uses monotonic decrease in P and increase in C of(P+C)/P^2.

Define the fixed cubic

    F_pair(b)=b*(101/50-b)*(1+(1042/729)b).

Since b<783/560<101/50, (40) implies

    b*c*[1+b(P+C)/P^2]>=F_pair(b).

The second derivative of F_pair is strictly decreasing in b and at
b=821/1050 equals-124646/42525<0. It is therefore concave on the
entire interval in(40), so its minimum is at an endpoint. The two
exact endpoint margins above2 are

    F_pair(821/1050)-2=211017368/4219543125>0,
    F_pair(783/560)-2=266455141/439040000>0.

Thus the middle-pair left side in(34) is greater than2>=D, a contradiction.
This proves h_3>1. All four original steps therefore satisfy

    1<h_0,h_3<174/97,
    1<h_1,h_2<2634012/1081453.                        (41)

This conclusion is derived; it was not assumed during the search-domain
reduction. It uses actual legal functions with all interpolation pairs.
All prefix differences |p_i-p_j| now exceed1. Since p_i<9 and4s<154/5,
the exact multiplier-energy identity(16) further gives

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij <112266/5<22500.              (42)

One explicit way to preserve every remaining step degree of freedom is

    h=(1+alpha,1+b,1+c,1+delta),
    c=C-5-alpha-delta-b.

The surviving region is contained in

    193/25<=C<87/10,
    0<alpha,delta<77/97,

    C*|b*c-(1+delta)*min(b*c,1)|<=2+alpha,

together with both repaired-cut inequalities(25), the triangle
condition(34) at ALL THREE adjacent pairs, and the original full matrix
interface. The listed cuts are pointwise, not merely box bounds. All

For each remaining schedule all20 multipliers remain nonnegative with

Neither a feasible seed nor a global separator has been produced.
