## 15. A backward extension strengthens the middle triangle

This is a new joint DRAFT calculation in the seventh launch's final round.
It is not a fact and consumes no verifier or numerical allowance. All four
steps retain their order. Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0,
    C=5+a+b+c+d, P=2+a, D=2+d, U=C-P,
    H=U-b=1+c+D, eta=b/H,
    kappa(a)=(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,
    kappa_plus=max(kappa(a),0),
    R=P^2-kappa_plus, J=P^2-kappa_plus*(1+eta).

The preceding full-domain derivation gives a<1 and the b-cut gives
eta<=P/C, hence a*eta<1 (indeed a*P/C<3/7). Then a stronger necessary
inequality than the middle adjacent triangle is

    b*c*[R+b(P+C)] <= D*J.                           (43)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1 this is exactly(34). For a>sqrt(2)-1 the positive
term subtracted from the old triangle slack is strictly positive.
No relation between c and b, or saturation assumption, is imposed.

Here is the entire new interpolation check. The case D<=b*c is already
excluded by the explicit capped function in Section11. Thus D>b*c. Set

    K=b^2*c+U*(D-b*c)=U*D-b*c*H,
    r=b^2*c/K,
    u=(1,0,0), w=(r,sqrt(r*(1-r)),0),
    v=-(b*u+D*w)/(1+c).

We have 0<r<1, <u,w>=||w||^2=r, and 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}.
These are exactly the middle triangle vectors, but its initial prefix
will now change. Choose t in[-eta,1] and put

    q=(1+(a-1)*t)/a,
    z=t*u+sqrt(q-t^2)*(0,0,1).

The radicand is (1-t)(1+a*t)/a>=0 because a*eta<1. Define the actual
positions and gradients by

    x=(u+(1+a)*z, u, -b*u, D*w, w),
    g=(z,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The four GD equalities hold. Add the optimum jet(0,0,0). Each proximal
point y_i=x_i-g_i must support conv{z,u,v,w} at g_i. This suffices for
all ordered Q because Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

At i=0, y_0=u+a*z. Since t+a*q=1+a*t and every old vertex y in
{0,u,v,w} has <u,y><=1,

    <y-z,y_0>=(1+a*t)(<u,y>-1)<=0.

At i=1 and i=4 the proximal point is zero. At i=3 it equals(1+d)*w.
Its pairing with z-w is (1+d)*r*(t-1)<=0; pairings with u-w and v-w
are respectively zero and negative, as explicitly computed in Section11.

At i=2 let n=-b*u-v. The two old vertex comparisons are

    <w-v,n>=0, <u-v,n>=-b*(1-r)*c/(1+c)<0.

The remaining comparison with z is exact. Put A_num=D*r-b*c. Then

    <u,n>=A_num/(1+c),
    <v,n>=r*(D-b*c)/(1+c),
    A_num=-b*c*H*(D-b*c)/K<0,
    r*(D-b*c)=-eta*A_num.

The fresh coordinate of z is orthogonal to n. Consequently

    <z-v,n>=(t+eta)*A_num/(1+c)<=0.

The optimum comparisons follow as 0 is already a convex combination
of u,v,w. This checks every original ordered pair, including those
involving the modified initial gradient. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes
the complete array by one global convex1-smooth function with minimizer0.
No gradient Gram from any other schedule has been reused.

The energy is ||x_0||^2-C^2*r by the directly derived identity(3), and

    ||x_0||^2
      =1+2(1+a)t+(1+a)^2*q
      =P^2+kappa(a)*(t-1).

Choose t=1 if kappa(a)<=0 and t=-eta otherwise. The initial squared
norm is exactly J. Nonnegative energy therefore requires

    C^2*b^2*c <= J*K.

Using K=U*D-b*c*H, C-P=U, and J*H=P^2*H-kappa_plus*U gives

    C^2*b^2*c-J*K
      =U*{b*c*[P^2-kappa_plus+b(P+C)]-D*J},

which proves(43). Compared to the original triangle's forbidden-side
expression, the new left-minus-right expression increases by

    kappa_plus*[D*(1+eta)-b*c]
      =kappa_plus*K/H.

This is strictly positive whenever a>sqrt(2)-1. Thus(43) is a genuine
strengthening throughout that region, rather than a relabeling of the
old test. This last algebraic comparison is a strength comparison only;
it does not by itself prove there exists a word passing every old test
and failing the new one.

For rational elimination on the a>sqrt(2)-1 branch, use

    R=a+3+1/a,
    J=R-kappa(a)*b/(C-2-a-b),
    c <= J*(C-3-a-b)/(J+b*[R+b(C+2+a)]),              (44)

where c+d=C-5-a-b and D=2+d. All denominators are positive: H>0,
J=||x_0||^2>0, b>0, R>0. The strict positivity of J follows also
because its fresh coordinate is nonzero at t=-eta. Equations(43),(44)
retain the independent variables C,a,b,d (with c recovered by the sum).

## 16. A second extension retains both initial gradients and their normals

Continue the same joint draft, with h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) and the same
positive a,b,c,d. This section constructs a different legal witness,
with its last triangle at steps2 and3. Its symbols are local to this
section and do not change the definitions in Section15.

The old adjacent triangle at the middle pair implies b*c<2+d, and the
last adjacent triangle implies c*d<1. Set

    U_tail=c+d+2,
    K_tail=c^2*d+U_tail*(1-c*d),
    r_tail=c^2*d/K_tail,
    eta_tail=c/(d+2),
    A_pre=1+a+b, H_pre=A_pre+1=2+a+b,
    Z_tail=(c+r_tail)/(1+d),
    q_late=(1+(1-b)*eta_tail)/b,
    q_early=[eta_tail+(A_pre*eta_tail-1)*Z_tail]/A_pre,
    q_pre=max(q_late,q_early),
    J_pre=1-2H_pre*eta_tail+H_pre^2*q_pre.

Every denominator is positive. We have 0<r_tail<1, b*eta_tail<1, and

    q_late-eta_tail^2
      =(1+eta_tail)(1-b*eta_tail)/b>0.

The resulting new necessary inequality is

    C^2*c^2*d <= J_pre*K_tail.                       (45)

The old last-pair triangle is retained too. Equivalently the right
side can be replaced by min(J_pre,(3+a+b)^2)*K_tail. This use of a
minimum means choosing the stronger of two independently legal witnesses.
It imposes no equality between formal gradients in the full certificate.

For the complete construction take

    u=(1,0,0),
    w=(r_tail,sqrt(r_tail*(1-r_tail)),0),
    v=-(c*u+w)/(1+d),
    z=-eta_tail*u+sqrt(q_pre-eta_tail^2)*(0,0,1).

As before 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}. Define all five original samples by

    x=(u+H_pre*z, u+(1+b)*z, u, -c*u, w),
    g=(z,z,u,v,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,

and add the zero optimum jet. Each GD step has exactly its original
length. We check the complete normal list for conv{z,u,v,w}.

At indices2 and4 the normal is zero. At index3 it is n=-c*u-v.
The triangle calculation gives

    <w-v,n>=0,
    <u-v,n>=-c*(1-r_tail)*d/(1+d)<0,
    <z-v,n>=0.

For the last equality the Section15 calculation uses first-pair
shift c, second-pair shift d, suffix capacity1, and t=-eta_tail.
It is independent of q_pre because the fresh coordinate is orthogonal
to n. Explicitly, with B_num=r_tail-c*d<0,

    <u,n>=B_num/(1+d),
    <v,n>=r_tail*(1-c*d)/(1+d),
    r_tail*(1-c*d)=-eta_tail*B_num.

At index1 the normal is u+b*z. For an old vertex y in{0,u,v,w},

    <y-z,u+b*z>
      =(1-b*eta_tail)<u,y>+eta_tail-b*q_pre.

The coefficient of <u,y> is positive and its maximum over the old
vertices is at u. The choice q_pre>=q_late makes that maximum<=0.

At index0 the normal is u+A_pre*z. Its comparison with y is

    (1-A_pre*eta_tail)<u,y>+eta_tail-A_pre*q_pre.

The old values <u,y> lie between -Z_tail (at v) and1 (at u).
The v comparison is nonpositive by q_pre>=q_early. At u, even the
smaller q_late gives the exact value

    (1+eta_tail)*(1-A_pre/b)<0,

since A_pre>b. These two endpoints control the entire interval,
including0 andw, for either sign of1-A_pre*eta_tail. The pairs
between indices0 and1 have the same gradient and hence Q=0. This
exhausts all original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities.
Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the single global convex1-smooth function.

The saturated-value energy identity gives

    V=||x_0||^2-C^2||w||^2=J_pre-C^2*r_tail.

This proves(45) without a numerical Gram or a loss of any original
coefficient freedom. If it is violated, the displayed data constitute
an explicit full-pair legal negative-V witness in three dimensions.

There is a useful closed subregion where the max disappears. If
A_pre*eta_tail<=1, the earliest normal has a nonnegative u coefficient,
so the calculation above proves q_early<=q_late. In that subregion put

    kappa_pre=H_pre*[2b+H_pre*(b-1)]/b.

Then

    J_pre=(3+a+b)^2-kappa_pre*(1+eta_tail).            (46)

Thus the old last-pair triangle is strictly strengthened whenever
A_pre*eta_tail<=1 and b>H_pre/(H_pre+2).

In particular this happens throughout the branch

    0<a<=sqrt(2)-1, b>=1                            (47)

of any remaining candidate. To check this assertion without numerical
constants, write P=2+a, L_mid=(P+C)/P^2. The old middle triangle says
b*eta_tail<=1/(1+L_mid*b). On(47), a<1/2 and P<5/2, so

    L_mid>(2+193/25)/(25/4)=972/625>3/2>1+a.

Consequently b+L_mid*b^2> b+1+a=A_pre, proving A_pre*eta_tail<1.
Also b>=1>H_pre/(H_pre+2). This is a full-domain implication on the
stated branch, not an assumption about the relative order of the steps.

The two extensions therefore give explicit strict improvements in two
complementary useful regimes: Section15 covers h0>sqrt(2), and this
section covers h0<=sqrt(2) with h1>=2. Neither result eliminates its
whole regime; they strengthen necessary tests there. Outside(47), the
full max(q_late,q_early) in(45) must be retained unless separately proved
inactive. A positive remaining value of one witness is not a certificate.

## 17. Repairing a quadratic prefix by the last triangle

There is a second full-pair way to use the same last triangle, which
does not identify the first two auxiliary gradients. Retain the local
symbols r_tail,eta_tail,K_tail,u,v,w from Section16 and put

    Q_pre=3+a+b,
    J_quad=[(1-b*eta_tail)*Q_pre^2
             +(1+eta_tail)/(a^2*b)]/(1+b).

Then every full feasible seed must satisfy

    C^2*r_tail <= J_quad.                            (48)

Here is its direct construction. Base data have

    x_base=(Q_pre*u,(2+b)*u,u,-c*u,w),
    g_base=(u,u,u,v,w).

Auxiliary data have

    x_aux=(u,-a*u,a*b*u,-a*b*c*u,a*b*w),
    g_aux=(u,-a*u,a*b*u,a*b*v,a*b*w).

Both arrays follow the actual h, and values are assigned in each array
by f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. The auxiliary normals vanish except at
index3, where the normal is a*b*n with n=-c*u-v. Write

    k=<u,n>=(r_tail-c*d)/(1+d)<0.

The triangle gives <v,n>=-eta_tail*k>0 and <w,n>=<v,n>.
All nonzero auxiliary finite interpolation residuals have target3.
For sources0,1,2,4 respectively they are

    a*b*k*(1+a*b*eta_tail) <0,
    -a^2*b*k*(1-b*eta_tail) >0,
    a^2*b^2*k*(1+eta_tail) <0,
    0.

Thus the ONLY positive auxiliary pair is(1,3), since b*eta_tail<1.
The incoming optimum residual at3 is -a^2*b^2*<v,n><0 and the other
optimum residuals are zero. The base array is legal by the triangle
normal check with all prefix nodes retained. Its pair(1,3) equals
(1+eta_tail)*k<0.

Choose

    epsilon=(1+eta_tail)/(a^2*b*(1-b*eta_tail))>0.

Take the orthogonal sum of base data and sqrt(epsilon) times auxiliary
data, with values added with weight epsilon. The pair(1,3) cancels
exactly. Every other original finite or optimum residual is nonpositive.
The GD identities are preserved in both coordinates, so the complete
array comes from one legal function by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Its energy is

    Q_pre^2-C^2*r_tail
       +epsilon*(1-C^2*a^2*b^2*r_tail).

Multiplication by the positive a^2*b*(1-b*eta_tail) yields

    a^2*b*(1-b*eta_tail)*Q_pre^2+(1+eta_tail)
       -C^2*a^2*b*(1+b)*r_tail.

Its nonnegativity is precisely(48). All auxiliaries are derived for the
same current schedule, with the actual first gradients u,-a*u,a*b*u.

The same repair can be made with the scalar last-cut base. In that
case take w=v=-eta_tail*u, x_3=(2+d)*w, x_4=w, and keep the same
quadratic prefix in the auxiliary. The same residual calculation holds:
its n=-(c-eta_tail)*u has k<0 and <v,n>=-eta_tail*k. This gives

    C^2*eta_tail^2 <= J_quad,                        (49)

which is the full j=2 cut repair in explicit rational coordinates,
rederived here rather than adopted from an unverified finding.

The two independently legal functions can therefore be combined into
the stronger necessary scalar inequality

    C^2*max(eta_tail^2,r_tail) <= J_quad.             (50)

The active branch has an exact schedule-independent description. Since
K_tail=(c+d+2)-c*d*(d+2), direct multiplication gives

    d*(d+2)^2-K_tail=(c+d+2)*(d*(d+2)-1).

Consequently r_tail>eta_tail^2 exactly when d>sqrt(2)-1, with equality
at that boundary. Thus(48) strictly strengthens the old scalar j=2
repair throughout h3>sqrt(2). On the complementary branch(49) is
stronger. This is a strict comparison of necessary inequalities, not a
claim that either test is sufficient. The displayed functions themselves
prove infeasibility whenever the relevant inequality is violated.

## 18. A concrete warning about dropping the earliest normal

The max in q_pre from Section16 cannot be removed using just the
construction hypotheses c*d<1 and b*eta_tail<1, even inside the coarse
four-step positive box. Consider the single exact example

    a=1/10, b=c=7/5, d=1/100, C=791/100.

It lies within the step and capacity box of Section14 and satisfies
c*d=7/500<1 and b*eta_tail=196/201<1. Its exact quantities are

    r_tail=980/169093,
    eta_tail=140/201,
    q_late=725/1407,
    q_early=791047600/1144252331,
    q_early-q_late=4230164675/24029298951>0.

If q_pre is incorrectly replaced by q_late, the original comparison
at index0 with vertex v is positive:

    <v-z,x_0-z>
      =A_pre*(q_early-q_late)
      =21150823375/48058597902>0.

This is the actual ordered interpolation residual Q_(3,0)>0. The
claimed global interpolant would not exist for that uncorrected array.
Using q_pre=max(q_late,q_early) repairs it exactly, as already proved.

This example refutes only that proof shortcut. It is NOT a proposed
seed or a point satisfying every older necessary condition; in fact its
middle triangle condition fails. One fixed rational calculation checked
these displayed fractions. No parameter scan or solver was used.

## 19. Exact remaining interface and final-round handoff

Status: still a joint proof DRAFT. No claim in this file has been
promoted to a fact. Accepted04219554e41d9750 remains a conditional
seed-to-asymptotic theorem; no seed or original-target solution has
been obtained. The seventh launch's second/final allocated round
(cumulative17) added no optimizer, parameter grid, installation,
subordinate agent, or fact_submit call. The one machine arithmetic
check was the predeclared rational counterexample in Section18.

The remaining step variables are C,a,b,d, with

    c=C-5-a-b-d,
    193/25<=C<87/10,
    0<a,d<77/97,
    0<b,c<1552559/1081453.

Keep EVERY pointwise condition from Section14, including all cuts,
both original cut/quadratic repairs, the clipped three-step test,
the actual quadratic and suffix pair tests, and all three adjacent
triangles. The three new independent necessary conditions are:

 * Middle triangle with its first-gradient extension: equation(43),
   with all quantities defined in Section15.
 * Last triangle with two actual prefix queries: equation(45), using
   q_pre=max(q_late,q_early), and retaining the old last-pair test.
 * Last triangle with the independently repaired quadratic prefix:
   equation(50), with the active max branch determined exactly by
   whether d is above or below sqrt(2)-1.

This is a genuinely stronger necessary region. The strict improvement
mechanisms cover, respectively, a>sqrt(2)-1, the branch
a<=sqrt(2)-1 with b>=1, and d>sqrt(2)-1. No statement that an entire
one of these regions is empty has been proved. In particular the
set where a,d<=sqrt(2)-1 and b<1 has not been discarded; equations
(45),(49) and every earlier test still apply there. The other branches
also remain unless the full joint inequalities exclude them.

For every surviving word retain all20 original Lambda_ij>=0 with
sum Lambda_ij<22500, the exact original L^T e=omega, and the complete
five-gradient PSD matrix. All four step degrees of freedom, all
coefficient signs, all cycles, and all PSD remainder directions remain.
There is no justified lossless reduction to a fixed C slice, fixed
step order, saturated cut, saturated quadratic, symmetric word, or
sparse multiplier support.

Two global elimination attempts remain incomplete:

1. Bounding b*c and c*d from the cuts, old triangles, quadratic, and
   nonlinear clipped-tail tests gives competing upper bounds, but has
   not supplied one signed polynomial inequality covering the complete
   C,a,b,d region. Endpoint upper bounds cannot be substituted as if
   they were attained together.
2. The new backward extensions reduce actual initial energy and the
   repaired terminal triangle increases actual terminal energy. Their
   negative cases are exact full-pair legal witnesses. Their joint
   nonnegative region is not proved empty. The activation of the earliest
   normal in Section16 is a real extra constraint; Section18 refutes
   deleting it from the construction without additional hypotheses.

Neither failure establishes existence or nonexistence of a full seed.
The next precise missing result is either a global polynomial/radical
exclusion of these jointly necessary tests for all C>=193/25 (using
the already derived C<87/10), or an actual full nonnegative multiplier
and PSD certificate for a surviving word. A certificate-free point
passing some finite witness tests would not settle the task.

Shared C82 finding6d992d34120a0c16 and C83 finding32380fa0da39d710 were
read during this round. They respectively eliminate a DIFFERENT

They remain unverified shared awareness and are not premises of any

Their current handoff also reports no seed and no global exclusion.

Latest main guidance read:5e2fd47b60222794. It continues this four-step
route and adds no numerical or verifier allowance to max2. The older

## Appendix B. Complete lower-clipping proof and earlier endpoint reduction

The following proof reproduces the full lower-clipping construction and
parameter elimination, including all bc>=1 branches. Section 2 above
subsequently rules those branches out for full threshold seeds. The
historical old-system survivor remains explicitly rejected.

## 10. A missing partial-tail clipped-return test rejects this entire family

The previous sections prove survival of the PRE-EXISTING scalar system.
They do not imply a seed, and the following new actual function now
proves that every displayed word is INFEASIBLE in the full original cone.
The original global decision remains open.

Here is a new necessary inequality for every four-step full seed with
h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), all a,b,c,d>0:

                 C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                 (9)

No ordering, fixed capacity or cut saturation is assumed. Define
alpha=b/(2+c)>0 and the scalar function

 f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,
 f(x)=x^2/2 for -alpha<=x<=1,
 f(x)=-alpha*x-alpha^2/2 for x<=-alpha.

Its derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]) is continuous, nondecreasing and
1-Lipschitz, so f is one globally convex differentiable1-smooth
function with unique minimizer0. Start at x0=P=2+a. The first four
queries and gradients are exactly

 x0=P,      g0=1,
 x1=1,      g1=1,
 x2=-b,     g2=-alpha,
 x3=-alpha, g3=-alpha.

Indeed x2=-(2+c)alpha and the next step has length1+c.
The last point is x4=d*alpha>0, with g4=min(d*alpha,1).
Every sample of this function satisfies f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2.
Telescoping the actual GD recursion therefore gives the exact primitive
energy V=x0^2-(x4+s*g4)^2, s=C-1.

If d*alpha<=1, then x4=g4 and V=P^2-C^2 d^2 alpha^2.
If d*alpha>1, then g4=1 and x4+s>C>P, giving V<0 directly.
Thus a full original nonnegative flow/PSD certificate necessarily
forces (9). Conversely, if (9) is violated, this exact function has
negative V in either case. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10,
then contradicts the full certificate identity, retaining every formal
coordinate and every original multiplier. This argument requires no
unproved prefix or tail heredity.

For h(t) from Section1, d*alpha<15/32<1. The difference between the
two sides of (9) is

 C b d-P(2+c)=3339/2000-(117/80)t>0
                                         for0<=t<=1/100.

Hence the entire family passing the old scalar tests, including C82
and C83 on0<=t<=10^(-12), fails the ORIGINAL full certificate cone.
At t=0 all data are rational:

 alpha=125/232,
 x=(12/5,1,-5/4,-125/232,375/928),
 g=(1,1,-125/232,-125/232,375/928),
 V=(12/5)^2-(2895/928)^2
   =-(3339*25611)/(25*928^2)<0.

Every original interpolation pair is legal because these are the
actual samples of the single explicit smooth convex function above.
This is a concrete missing constraint, not a violation inferred from
independently chosen jets or a numerical Gram.

The old system's global-coverage implication is still refuted by
Sections1--8. The enlarged system including (9) is a new question:
its whole-domain emptiness is NOT proved, and no surviving full seed
has been constructed. In particular this witness must not be turned
into an unsupported statement that every four-step seed is impossible.

## 11. Exact elimination of the entire lower-clipping parameter

The new inequality is not an isolated parameter choice. The whole
one-parameter scalar family can be eliminated exactly. Let
h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), with all shifts positive, P=2+a,
H=3+c+d, C=P+b+H. Assume cd<1; the earlier legal last-triangle
condition forces this on any candidate. For every alpha>0, take the
same function with derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]), start at x0=P,
and perform all four original steps. Then its energy is nonnegative
for EVERY alpha>0 if and only if the following conditions all hold:

 C b/H <=P,
 C b d/(2+c)<=P,
 C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
 and, when bc>=1, C d<=P.                              (10)

This equivalence concerns only this particular one-dimensional function
family; it is not a characterization of full primitive feasibility.
Every strict violation gives a finite positive alpha and one actual
negative-energy function. Equality and zero limits are retained.

Proof. Every sample of every function in this family has saturated
value f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Thus V=P^2-Y(alpha)^2, where
Y(alpha)=x4+s g4, s=C-1. The first two steps reach x1=1 and x2=-b.
For alpha>=b the remainder is independent of alpha: x3=bc and
x4=bc-(1+d)min(bc,1). The assumption cd<1 guarantees x4>=-b:
if bc<=1 use x4=-bcd; if bc>=1, then b>=1/c>d and x4>=-d>-b.
Consequently the final lower clipping is inactive. If x4>1 then
Y=x4+s>C>P and the third condition in (10) fails too. Otherwise
Y=C x4, exactly the third condition.

For 0<alpha<=b, g2=-alpha and x3=-b+(1+c)alpha. Split at

 alpha_A=b/H, alpha_B=b/(2+c),
 alpha_C=(b+1)/(1+c) when bc>=1.

Their relevant order is 0<alpha_A<alpha_B<=b, and, when defined,
alpha_B<alpha_C<=b. For alpha<=alpha_B the third query remains
on the lower flat part, so g3=-alpha and x4=-b+(2+c+d)alpha.
For alpha<=alpha_A one has g4=-alpha and
Y=-b-(P+b)alpha, a decreasing function, reaching -C b/H.
From alpha_A to alpha_B, x4 increases and g4 follows x4 until
possibly reaching the upper cap1. Thus Y increases throughout that
interval. Its endpoint at alpha_B has x4=d b/(2+c), with
Y=C d b/(2+c) if x4<=1, and Y=x4+s otherwise. If x4>1 the
second condition in (10) and nonnegative energy both fail. The
limit |Y(0)|=b is smaller than C b/H since C>H.

For alpha between alpha_B and min(b,alpha_C), with alpha_C omitted
when bc<1, the third query is on the quadratic part: g3=x3 and
x4=-d x3 decreases with alpha. On each final clipping branch,
Y is one of x4+s, C x4, or x4-s alpha. Each is decreasing, and
they agree at their boundaries. Thus |Y| attains its maximum at
an endpoint of this interval. At its lower endpoint we already
have the second condition. If its upper endpoint is b, we already
have the third condition. Otherwise x3=1 at alpha_C, and cd<1
implies alpha_C>=1/c>d; hence x4=g4=-d and Y=-C d.

For alpha_C<=alpha<=b when bc>=1, g3=1, and x4=x3-(1+d)
increases with alpha. Also x4+alpha>0 at alpha_C and increases,
so the final lower cap stays inactive. Y increases, first as C x4
and then, if x4>1, as x4+s. Again its absolute maximum is at an
endpoint, already covered by the last and third conditions.
These regimes exhaust every alpha, and prove (10).

For the compact candidate box b<1552559/1081453<2<2+c, the second
condition is implied by Cd<=P when bc>=1. On bc<=1, the third
condition is Cbcd<=P, so the two new/old return constraints are
precisely

 C b d <= P min(2+c,1/c).                              (11)

The max over c>0 of min(2+c,1/c) is1+sqrt(2), attained only at
c=sqrt(2)-1. This follows from the increasing/decreasing branches
and their unique positive intersection. With the separate full
quadratic bound Cabcd<=1 one can strengthen (11) to

 C b d <= P min(2+c,1/(m c)), m=max(1,a P),
 C b d <= P [1+sqrt(1+1/m)].                           (12)

These are necessary inequalities only; no end of the capacity range
is fixed. The first inequality in (12) retains the actual c and is
stronger than replacing it by its maximum in the second.

## 12. Remaining global endpoint/slack problem

The actual enlarged necessary region consists of every prior scalar
condition, all C82/C83 branches, and now (10), with all four variables
C,a,b,d free and c=C-5-a-b-d. No interval of that enlarged region
has been discarded merely because an extra inequality is strict.
The scalar-system survivor from Sections1--8 is explicitly rejected by
(9), so it must not be submitted as an actual full seed.

For the branch bc<=1 one useful exact remaining reduction is as follows.
Put kappa=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), R=P^2-kappa,
J=P^2-kappa*(1+b/(3+c+d)), Ksum=c+d,
A=b[R+b(P+C)]/J, and y=Cb/P. The existing middle lift, and the
new partial-tail return, respectively say

 d >= (A Ksum-2)/(A+1),
 d <= (Ksum+2)/(y+1).                                 (13)

All denominators are positive: J is the squared initial norm of the
explicit backward-lift witness, and P,b>0. In particular interval
nonemptiness implies

 Ksum(A y-1)<=2(A+y+2).                               (14)

The clipped return and unit quadratic also give

 d(Ksum-d)<=1/(max(1,aP)*y).                           (15)

Equations(13)--(15) keep both interval endpoints, including the case
where the lower bound in(13) is negative. Product concavity may be used
only after intersecting(13) with the original positive endpoint bounds;
one cannot minimize at an inadmissible endpoint or assume saturation.
The branch bc>=1 retains Cd<=P and the other original inequalities.
No proof that this enlarged four-variable system is empty was obtained
in this round. No actual Lambda/M certificate was obtained either.

# C98: paid anchor innovations and arbitrary optimum slack

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, subject to the author audit recorded
in the handoff. This is actual runtime round 2 of the current allocation,
completed baseline 27, completed ceiling 29, attempted ceiling 30 including
one historical interruption. No computation or formal gate is authorized.
The original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile Goal remains OPEN.

The accepted mathematical inputs are `1252b8823bb5a290` (the original
directed interpolation inequalities and its explicit static interpolant)
and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the ORIGINAL benchmark and its crude upper bound).
Every other assertion used below is proved in this joint text. C97 and
the older Markov candidates are context, never mathematical premises.

## 1. Objects, scope, and the new distinction

Use smoothness 1. A supplied word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) generates

    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,  g_i=grad F(X_i),
    f_i=F(X_i)-min F,  q_i=||g_i||^2,
    V=2 f_n / R_init^2,  R_init=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0.

The included optimum is star, with X_star=g_star=f_star=0. The ORIGINAL
directed residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.       (1)

For a nonzero queried gradient put

    A_i=sqrt(q_i), u_i=g_i/A_i,
    eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i,
    delta_i=f_i-q_i/2,
    z_i=<X_i,g_i>-f_i-q_i/2.

The two optimum rows imply

    eta_i>=1,  delta_i>=0,  z_i>=0,
    delta_i+z_i=(eta_i-1)q_i.                         (2)

The affine value reserve is delta_i and the optimum upper-row slack is
z_i. They are different quantities. The constructions below make both
strictly positive. The class bounds allow either to be zero and do not
prescribe any queried value within the legal interval (2).

Three conclusions are proved here.

* Arbitrary slack does not rescue an aligned-anchor Markov trajectory
  satisfying 1<=eta_i<h_i. Its amplitude contraction is already forced
  by its support geometry, independently of all values and remote rows.
* A genuinely future-correlated anchor does escape that contraction.
  Sections 4--6 provide an exact finite family and a bounded rule for
  every word with min h>1, with all pairs, one static function, a true
  radius, a payoff and a paid continuation law.
* Sparse future correlations still cannot supply the original benchmark
  on the original enclosing-m recursive words. Section 8 gives an exact
  weighted bound for arbitrary locations and arbitrary values, a free
  terminal, and even arbitrary signs at the exceptional transitions.
  A nonvanishing benchmark payoff requires order n/m nonzero future
  anchor innovations under the stated support restriction.

The last conclusion does NOT exclude a dense bounded-rule anchor, a
different dependence law, or trajectories whose support ratios cross
the physical steps. It is not an upper bound on unrestricted W.

## 2. The finite sign law and the actual cost of a correlated anchor

For queried indices 0,...,n-1, suppose the unit directions have Markov
product inner products

    <u_i,u_j>=product_(k=i)^(j-1)rho_k, i<j, |rho_k|<=1. (3)

For strictly interior rho_k, these are the moments of a literal finite
two-state sign law. Let S_0 be fair and independently let the bond signs
J_sign_k have P(J_sign_k=1)=(1+rho_k)/2. Set S_(k+1)=S_k J_sign_k. Then

    p(s_0,...,s_(n-1))=2^(-n) product_(k<n-1)
                             (1+rho_k s_k s_(k+1)).   (4)

Successive summation over the last sign normalizes (4); independence
of the bonds gives (3). Real coordinates are explicit:

    u_0=e_0,
    u_(i+1)=rho_i u_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)e_(i+1),            (5)

where the e_i are orthonormal. Hence PSD is not an assumption about a
formal Gram matrix. Formula (5) realizes it. The formula also allows
rho_i=+/-1, in which case no new direction is needed at that transition.

Conversely, from (3), when |rho_i|<1 the vector

    e_(i+1)=(u_(i+1)-rho_i u_i)/sqrt(1-rho_i^2)

has unit norm and is orthogonal to every u_j, j<=i. This follows by
substituting <u_j,u_(i+1)>=rho_i<u_j,u_i>. It is consequently orthogonal
to all earlier nondegenerate innovations. Thus (5) describes the
canonical real innovations of any such product-correlation data.

Write w_(i+1)=<X_0,e_(i+1)>. Earlier gradients are orthogonal to
e_(i+1), so actual GD, not an assigned support matrix, gives

    eta_(i+1) A_(i+1)
       =rho_i(eta_i-h_i) A_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)w_(i+1).    (6)

The initial e_0 coefficient is eta_0 A_0. Orthogonal decomposition gives
the EXACT identity

    ||X_0||^2 = eta_0^2 q_0
       +sum_(i:|rho_i|<1)
          [eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)-rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i]^2
                                  /(1-rho_i^2)
       +||X_perp||^2,                              (7)

where X_perp is the component perpendicular to all queried gradients.
For a degenerate transition the numerator must be zero and there is
no summand. For prescribed A_i, eta_i and rho_i, (7) is the minimal
squared norm realizing those support data when X_perp=0. This is only
a geometric realization statement; it is NOT interpolation sufficiency.

If 0 is not the closest minimizer, (7) is not the true initial radius.
The explicit constructions below have unique minimizer 0. For the
whole-class bounds we instead use

    A_0<=R_init,                                       (8)

which follows from 1-Lipschitzness of grad F between X_0 and ANY
minimizer, followed by taking the infimum of the distances. Thus no
nonclosest minimizer is silently used as the normalization.

## 3. Slack cannot repair the unforced support contraction

Assume every queried eta_i lies in [1,h_i). If w_(i+1)=0, (6) forces
rho_i<0 for nonzero consecutive gradients, and

    A_(i+1)=|rho_i|(h_i-eta_i)A_i/eta_(i+1)
            <=(h_i-1)A_i.                          (9)

For a radial initial point X_0=eta_0 g_0 all future coefficients w_i
are zero. The sharper product before the last inequality in (9) is

    A_j/A_i <= product_(k=i)^(j-1)
                                  (h_k-eta_k)/eta_(k+1). (10)

No f_i or z_i appears. In particular arbitrary optimum slack, even
horizon-dependent and approaching the whole allowed reserve, cannot
change this support obstruction. A zero queried gradient means its
point minimizes F and every later GD iterate stays there; its terminal
error is zero and is already covered separately.

For later use there is also a universal one-step bound, without (3),
any support restriction, or any condition on the initial anchor. Adding
the two adjacent rows (1) yields

    ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2 <= h_i<g_i-g_(i+1),g_i>.

Completing the square gives the entire necessary disk

    ||g_(i+1)-(1-h_i/2)g_i|| <= (h_i/2)||g_i||.

For h_i>1 this implies

    A_(i+1)<=max{1,h_i-1}A_i.                      (11)

If the adjacent inner product is nonpositive, a stronger bound holds.
Write d=h_i-1>0 and t=-<g_i,g_(i+1)>/q_i>=0.
The same inequality and Cauchy--Schwarz give

    q_(i+1)/q_i <= d+(d-1)t,  t^2<=q_(i+1)/q_i.

Thus (t+1)(t-d)<=0 and 0<=t<=d. Maximizing the affine expression over
that interval gives

    q_(i+1)<=d max{1,d} q_i.                       (12)

These are necessary consequences of the original rows, not claims
that adjacent rows suffice for a legal trajectory.

## 4. An actual correlated-anchor escape: all rows displayed

Take the supplied physical word h=(3,10). For

    8/5<=k<4,  0<epsilon<1/2,

define in R^3

    g_0=e_0,
    g_1=(-e_0+e_1)/2,
    g_2=(k/2)e_0-(k/2)e_1+sqrt(k(4-k)/2)e_2,
    X_0=2e_0+e_1,
    X_1=-e_0+e_1,
    X_2=4e_0-4e_1.

The points obey the supplied GD updates. Their squared gradient norms
are q=(1,1/2,2k), and <X_i,g_i>=2q_i. Prescribe

    f=(3/2,3/4,3k)-epsilon,
    delta_i=q_i-epsilon>0,  z_i=epsilon>0.          (13)

The normalized directions have product correlations with

    rho_0=-1/sqrt(2), rho_1=-sqrt(k)/2.

The projection coefficient of g_2 on g_1 is -k, and its e_2 component
is precisely the positive innovation needed for (5). Also
<u_0,u_2>=rho_0 rho_1, so this is the actual Markov law, not only its
adjacent correlations. Choosing k=8/5 makes rho_0 and rho_1 different.

All six non-optimum residuals are

    Q_01=-1,             Q_10=-1,
    Q_02=4-5k/2,         Q_20=-1,
    Q_12=-4-3k/2,        Q_21=-(1+k)/2.             (14)

For example <g_0,X_2-X_0>=2 and
||g_0-g_2||^2=1+k, giving Q_02=4-5k/2.
Also <g_2,X_0-X_2>=-7k/2, giving Q_20=-1.
The adjacent expansions use <g_0,g_1>=-1/2 and
<g_1,g_2>=-k/2, giving the remaining entries of (14).
Subtracting the same epsilon from all finite values changes none of
these six residuals. The optimum rows are exactly

    Q_(i,star)=-epsilon,  Q_(star,i)=epsilon-q_i<0. (15)

Equations (14)--(15) cover every directed pair. Section 6 constructs
one fixed smooth function realizing all these jets and proves its
unique minimizer is 0. Its true squared initial radius is 5 and its
payoff is

    V=(6k-2epsilon)/5.                             (16)

For the definite choice k=8/5, epsilon=1/8, the terminal value is
187/40 and V=187/100. The second physical displacement has e_1
component -5. Both the positive affine values and the transverse
action are retained.

This legal example falsifies any extension of the ALIGNED estimate
(10) to a free initial anchor: eta_0=eta_1=2, h_0=3 would give
q_1<=q_0/4 in the aligned case, but here q_1=q_0/2. The paid initial
innovation is w_1=1. It is not a translation of the minimizer: the
constructed function has unique minimizer 0 and true radius sqrt(5).

This finite example is not a target-scale improvement. In fact every
member of this displayed family has V<24/5<81/16, while the accepted
single-cut bound on the same word is at least 81/16 (the cut at 10).
It certifies a real escape from a scoped hypothesis, not a stronger
all-schedule adversary.

## 5. A bounded nonstationary rule with a paid initial pulse

Here is a complete construction for EVERY supplied word of length
n>=2 with min_i h_i>1. The function is planned for the full word before
GD begins. Choose

    1<eta<min_i h_i,  0<theta<1,

for example eta=(1+min_i h_i)/2 and theta=1/2. Set the virtual terminal
entry h_n=eta. For i=0,...,n-1 put

    t_i=theta min{1,(h_i-eta)/h_(i+1)}.

Let s=t_0, q_0=1, and set

    q_1=(h_0-1)s/eta,
    v=sqrt(q_1-s^2),
    w=(eta-1)s/v,
    g_0=e_0, g_1=-s e_0+v e_1,
    X_0=eta e_0+w e_1.                             (17)

The quantity v is strictly positive: s<(h_0-eta)/h_1<
(h_0-1)/eta. Thus all coordinates in (17) are real and w>0.
For i>=1 define

    b_i=(h_i-eta)/eta,
    rho_i=-sqrt(t_i/b_i),
    A_i=sqrt(q_i), u_i=g_i/A_i,
    u_(i+1)=rho_i u_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)e_(i+1),
    q_(i+1)=t_i b_i q_i, g_(i+1)=sqrt(q_(i+1))u_(i+1). (18)

The initial transition is rho_0=-s/sqrt(q_1), whose square is
s eta/(h_0-1)<1. For i>=1, t_i/b_i<=theta eta/h_(i+1)<=theta<1.
Thus all rho_i lie strictly between -1 and 0, and (4)--(5), now including terminal index n, give
the full law and literal real realization. Every parameter comes from
the supplied word and the two scalars; there is no free Gram matrix
or collection of independently adjustable modes.

Set X_i=X_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j. The first support is eta, and

    <X_1,g_1>=(h_0-eta)s+wv=(h_0-1)s=eta q_1.

For i>=1 all future innovations are orthogonal to X_i. Formula (18)
therefore gives <X_i,g_i>=eta q_i by induction using actual GD.

Choose one common slack epsilon with

    0<epsilon<(eta-1)min_(0<=i<=n)q_i,
    f_i=(eta-1/2)q_i-epsilon.                       (19)

For example half the right-hand minimum fixes epsilon from the full
word. Both delta_i=(eta-1)q_i-epsilon and z_i=epsilon are positive.
This slack is not set anew at each join.

We prove EVERY non-optimum pair next. Common epsilon cancels from
each such residual. For 1<=i<=j<=n put

    P_ij=product_(k=i)^(j-1)(-t_k),
    E_ij=product_(k=i)^(j-1)(t_k b_k)=q_j/q_i,
    B_ij=sum_(k=i)^(j-1)h_k P_ik+P_ij,
    P_ii=E_ii=B_ii=1.

The guards imply

    0<t_i<1,
    h_i-t_i h_(i+1)>=eta,
    h_i-t_i>=eta.                                  (20)

For i<j, pairing alternating terms in B_ij proves B_ij>=eta.
For even j-i, group physical terms as
P_(i,i+2r)[h_(i+2r)-t_(i+2r)h_(i+2r+1)], followed by the
positive P_ij. The first group alone is at least eta and all groups
are nonnegative. For odd j-i, the last term after the analogous
pairs is P_(i,j-1)[h_(j-1)-t_(j-1)]; if there are no earlier pairs
this is at least eta, and otherwise the first pair is already at
least eta. Also

    B_ij=h_i-t_i B_(i+1,j)<=h_i,                    (21)

because B_(i+1,j)>0, including B_jj=1. Thus
eta<=B_ij<=h_i for every i<j with i>=1.

Inner products from (18) give <g_i,g_j>=q_i P_ij and
<X_i,g_j>=eta<g_i,g_j> for i>=1. Expanding the ORIGINAL (1) gives

    Q_(j,i)=(eta-1)q_i(P_ij-1)<0,
    Q_(i,j)=q_i[eta-B_ij-(eta-1)E_ij]<0.            (22)

The first inequality uses the alternating sign and |P_ij|<1; the
second uses B_ij>=eta. No history is discarded in B_ij.

It remains to check every pair involving the original query 0.
Let y_0=X_0-g_0. Equation (17) gives <y_0,g_1>=0. Every later
g_j is a scalar multiple of g_1 plus directions e_2,...,e_j, so
<y_0,g_j>=0 for ALL j>=1. The exact original reverse rows are

    Q_(j,0)=-(eta-1), j>=1.                         (23)

For the other orientation, <g_0,g_j>=-s P_(1,j) and direct GD
substitution gives

    Q_(0,j)=eta-h_0+s B_(1,j)-(eta-1)q_j, j>=1.    (24)

For j=1 use B_11=1<=h_1. For j>1 use (21).
Since s h_1<=theta(h_0-eta)<h_0-eta, (24) is strictly negative.
The optimum rows are

    Q_(i,star)=-epsilon,
    Q_(star,i)=epsilon-(eta-1)q_i<0.                (25)

Equations (22)--(25) include the terminal and every chronological
cross pair, in both directions. In particular the argument does not
claim that local tests or a PSD correlation law suffice.

## 6. One static function, exact radius, payoff and continuation

For either construction in Sections 4--5 define, using ALL its jets,

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<=n)
                                 (<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i)},
    F(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                         (26)

This is exactly the full-data construction in accepted
`1252b8823bb5a290`: expanding its ith quadratic gives
||v||^2/2+<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i. The proved all-pair rows therefore
give one everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth F with
F(X_i)=f_i, grad F(X_i)=g_i and F(0)=0=min F. It is fixed before GD.

Because all delta_i>0, the maximum in (26) is zero in a neighborhood
of v=0. If X!=0, choose 0<a<1 sufficiently small that aX belongs
to that neighborhood. Then

    F(X)>=(a-a^2/2)||X||^2>0.

Thus argmin F={0}. Section 4 has exact squared radius 5. Section 5
has exact squared radius eta^2+w^2, and its exact payoff is

    q_n=[(h_0-1)s/eta] product_(i=1)^(n-1)(t_i b_i),
    V=[(2eta-1)q_n-2epsilon]/(eta^2+w^2).           (27)

In Section 5 the second GD displacement has nonzero e_1 component
-h_1 v. Subsequent queried innovations also have nonzero coefficients
in the actual displacement. Hence these are paid transverse changes,
including their full cost in the radius, the supports and the values.

The state transfer for every subsequent transition i>=1 is

    q_(i+1)=t_i b_i q_i,
    f_(i+1)+epsilon=(t_i b_i)(f_i+epsilon),
    delta_(i+1)+epsilon=(t_i b_i)(delta_i+epsilon),
    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,                            (28)

with u_(i+1) propagated by (18) in the SAME growing coordinate system.
For a literal append to already fixed jets through index k>=2, retain
eta, epsilon, w, all old jets and all old transitions. A sufficient
boundary condition for the first new physical step h_k is

    h_k>eta,  t_(k-1)h_k<=h_(k-1)-eta.              (29)

Choose later transitions by (18), and require
epsilon<(eta-1)q_i at every new sample. These conditions preserve
all old guards, add the missing boundary guard, and keep every affine
reserve positive. The SAME proofs (21)--(25) then cover all cross
pairs of the extended history. This is an existence statement for
one interpolant of the jointly planned complete word. It does not
change an already running objective at a join, or assert that a
shorter instance's particular F already has the desired extension.

Replacing a shorter word's virtual terminal step by a physical step
does not come free: (29) and the retained epsilon reserve can fail.
The pulse w is part of X_0, not energy added during the run.

For arbitrary L>0 multiply F by L and use steps h_i/L. Both the
trajectory and normalized W=2R payoff formulas remain the same.

## 7. Entire sparse-anchor class: exact weighted necessary bound

Consider ANY legal nonzero queried trajectory with (3) and

    1<=eta_i<h_i for every queried 0<=i<n.          (30)

Allow arbitrary initial anchors, arbitrary dimensions, all values
permitted by (2), and a completely FREE terminal gradient and value
generated by the same function. No construction guard from Section 5
is imposed. In the canonical innovations define

    I={i in {0,...,n-2}: |rho_i|<1 and w_(i+1)!=0},
    E_anchor=sum_(i in I) log_+(1/(h_i-1)),
    K_anchor=cardinality(I), log_+(x)=max{0,log x}.         (31)

These are the actual support and initial coefficients relative to the
included minimizer 0. Additional components X_perp are allowed.
No claim that 0 is closest is needed, by (8).

Outside I, (9) applies. Inside I, (11) applies. Multiplying the exact
per-step upper bounds gives

    q_(n-1)/q_0 <= [product_(i=0)^(n-2)(h_i-1)]^2
                                             exp(2 E_anchor). (32)

If EVERY adjacent queried inner product is nonpositive, use (12)
inside I instead; the stronger version is

    q_(n-1)/q_0 <= [product_(i=0)^(n-2)(h_i-1)]^2
                                                    exp(E_anchor).  (33)

In (32), nonzero anchor coefficients may cause positive transitions.
Thus (32) does not silently retain an all-negative sign restriction.
The weights in E_anchor show that correlations after steps h_i>=2 receive
no improvement factor at all in this estimate.

For a final physical step ell=h_(n-1) in (1,2), integrating the
1-Lipschitz gradient along that step gives

    2 f_n<=2 f_(n-1)-ell(2-ell)q_(n-1).

The actual optimum upper row and (30) imply
f_(n-1)<=(eta_(n-1)-1/2)q_(n-1), so

    2 f_n < [(ell-1)^2+2(ell-1)]q_(n-1).           (34)

This step leaves the terminal gradient entirely unrestricted. Combined
with (8), equations (32)--(34) are true-distance bounds, even for
functions with a nonunique minimizer and arbitrary optimum slack.

## 8. Uniform comparison on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m words

For m>=4 fix throughout the entire word

    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m,
    c=sigma(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Set h=H_m with these SAME enclosing-m parameters in every child.
The smallest and last step is ell=1+a, and

    1/5<a<1/2.

Indeed a=(m-1)/[(m+1)sigma]>=3/(5sigma)>1/5, since sigma<3,
and a<1/sigma<1/2, since sigma>2.

Let D be the product of ALL physical offsets h_i-1. The recursion
D_(j+1)=D_j^2 a c^j with D_0=1 proves by induction

    D=a^(N-1)c^(N-m-1)=beta^n c^(-m).              (35)

The product in (32) is D/a, since only the last physical step is
omitted. Use gamma=2 for the general-sign estimate (32), or gamma=1
under the all-nonpositive adjacent hypothesis of (33). Equations
(8), (32)--(35) prove the whole-class upper bound

    V <= (1+2/a) beta^(2n)c^(-2m) exp(gamma E_anchor).     (36)

This includes arbitrary horizon-dependent values, support ratios,
correlations, pulse positions and pulse magnitudes satisfying the
specified class hypotheses. The aligned class has E_anchor=0, so (36)
already closes arbitrary-slack repair and is stronger than the
previous tight-row estimate. Section 5 has only one possible queried
future coefficient, w_1, so it is covered as well.

For the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark, accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`
gives B_n<=1+4 n^(log_2 3)<=1+4*3^m. Therefore

    B_n V <= (1+2/a)(1+4*3^m)c^(-2m)
                             exp(-2n/m+gamma E_anchor).   (37)

We used log(1-1/m)<=-1/m, not an asymptotic replacement of the word
or benchmark. If E_anchor=o(n/m), (37) tends to zero. More quantitatively,
E_anchor<=2n/(gamma m) already forces a zero limit, because c>=sigma and
sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)>3, while 1+2/a<11.

The exact weights identify where an exception could help. Offset
level 0 is a, level 1 is beta<1, and every level >=2 has offset at
least beta c=sigma(1-1/m^2)>1. Consequently, if K_0 and K_1 count
indices in I following physical levels 0 and 1, then

    E_anchor=K_0 log(1/a)+K_1 log(1/beta)
       <=K_anchor log(1/a).                              (38)

Correlations at higher physical levels contribute zero to this bound.

Suppose, along this sequence of ORIGINAL words, a family from Section
7 has liminf B_n V>0. Take a constant v_0>0 with B_n V>=v_0 for all
sufficiently large m. From the sharper (36) one MUST have

    gamma E_anchor >= 2n log(1/beta)+2m log c
                  -log B_n-log(1+2/a)+log v_0.     (39)

Here m log(1/beta)->1, log(1/a)->log sigma, and all terms of order m
are negligible relative to n/m, since m^2/(2^m-1)->0. Equations
(38)--(39) therefore imply the precise necessary count condition

    liminf_(m->infinity) K_anchor m/n >= 2/(gamma log sigma). (40)

In particular K_anchor=o(n/m), including any fixed number of arbitrarily
located anchor pulses, is excluded. The stronger constant 2/log sigma
applies when all adjacent correlations are nonpositive; the constant
1/log sigma applies when the exceptional transitions have free signs.

Neither (36) nor (40) asserts sufficiency. They do not bound the
unrestricted W_n(H_m), and they do not exclude a finite-state rule
which prepares many nonzero anchor coordinates from the full word.

## 9. Remaining interface, failed extensions and literature audit

The slack-only escape is closed in the complete class (30) with a
radial anchor; it does not require tight optimum rows or a floor on
the positive reserve. Retuning eta_i inside [1,h_i) cannot evade it.
The initial-pulse construction proves that a future-correlated anchor
is a real legal freedom. The finite family (13)--(16) disproves an
unqualified extension of the aligned contraction to that freedom.
The weighted bound then closes sparse-pulse repair on the original
words, even allowing arbitrary pulse locations, values and terminal.

What remains is a bounded rule preparing sufficiently many effective
future correlations, or support ratios crossing the physical steps,
with every chronological interpolation row and the true radius paid.
The identity (7) alone is not such a rule and is not presented as one.
It prices any proposed rule. Likewise (39) is only necessary; enough
nonzero entries does not imply that their amplitudes, cross rows or
actual payoff work. No arbitrary-h matching lower bound follows.

Older UNVERIFIED `c035d2edbaffb2c0` and `fd7036b719ef9645` were read
for their full domains: consecutive first-occurrence ruler blocks,
constant queried gradients within ordinary blocks, and their stated
terminal/intercept restrictions. This draft changes gradients at
every physical sample and studies the initial future correlations.
It neither uses nor claims to subsume those older results.

Matlas queries about Markov gradient slack and innovation norm budgets
returned multi-task gradient surgery, mirror-prox bounds, Brownian
precision matrices and reciprocal Gaussian processes. None establishes
the needed GD inequalities. The built-in web fallback located the
already-local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper, *Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods*,
arXiv:1502.05666; its definitions, Theorem 4, proof and construction in
Sections 2.1 and 2.5 were read. Its terminology was checked at mu=0,
L=1. Here the interpolation premise is the accepted self-contained
fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, not a newly imported external theorem.

The already-local Ising-chain reading from C97 was recalled solely as
background for the finite sign language. Equations (4)--(7) are
proved directly, so no probability or covariance theorem is a proof
premise. A new covariance-paper download is unnecessary: this branch
has reduced to the same audited finite independent-bond law and
explicit orthogonal decomposition, not a new Gaussian-process result.

All calculations in this text are symbolic author arguments. No
numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, subagent,
consult, installation, fact_submit or newly accepted fact was used.

# All checkpoint selections collapse to Huber on a perturbed dyadic family

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, max2 round51.
Assignment: TASK.md and master_guidance4c8674478c3e4a53.
Plan:20537eac824926ce. No fact_submit is authorized or made.

The new claim is about the definite scalar functional in frozen formula(5),
including its entire parameter domain and every eligible checkpoint subset.
It is NOT an upper bound for actual gradient-descent error W. It says nothing
about the stronger true-radius formula(4), the separate source-format
formula(5a), or the sufficiency of the actual spiral functions. In particular
it does not resolve the original minimax problem.

All new lemmas below are proved together in this one draft; none is treated
as an independently accepted fact. The only accepted mathematical input is
fact04219554e41d9750, for the unchanged benchmark ray and its certified
enclosures. The spiral geometry and its sibling audit are not premises.

## 1. Complete scalar definition and result

For 0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2 set

    Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*Theta),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp*Theta/kappa)-1)/d_sp,
    H_sp=C*(1+(1+kappa)*T_sp)-1,
    A=A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon.

Here C and A are local abbreviations for the source's explicit constants;
C is not the benchmark sequence C_n. The source is frozen finding
cdd7f057606aeb18 at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/cdd7f057606aeb18.md,
SHA256366cd6a12c8e4f56cf3c875c104f3e9bfdc185bf26c955e4f7e83d5bef7b607c.
That hash was read back and matched. No extension of the parameter range
or change in the constant formula is made here.

For a finite nonnegative physical word h, a selected subset is written in
its actual chronological order as

    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k).

The ordinary sums s_i are sums of exactly those intervening unselected
steps. Each selected checkpoint must satisfy b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2).
Define Q(b)=max{epsilon*b,A} and

    Phi(h;epsilon,kappa,selection)
      =1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
          [(b_i-1)/(C*s_i+Q(b_i))]^2.                 (1)

Empty blocks are allowed. For k=0 this is exactly1/(1+2*sum h).
Let L_sp(h) be the supremum of(1) over all these subsets and all
0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2, including k=0. No geometry is required to define it.

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2). For a fixed real tau>0 and an integer m>tau set

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda*(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.       (2)

Every child in(2) keeps the SAME enclosing(m,tau). Write H_(m,tau)=H_m,
n_m=2^m-1, and S_j=sum H_j. These are exactly the words specified in
TASK.md. Only tau=1 is the original diagnostic word; the values used below
are new explicitly specified words in the permitted family.

CLAIM. For EVERY real0<tau<=1/4 and EVERY integer m>=1536,

    L_sp(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).                      (3)

More strongly, at EACH individual allowed parameter pair, every eligible
selection is bounded above by the right side of(3). Thus the full parameter
supremum, including parameters depending arbitrarily on the whole word and
horizon, is controlled by a finite statement, not an interchange of a limit
and a supremum.

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

All split indices are integers, and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 establishes the positive finite ray limit

    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1.

For every fixed0<tau<=1/4, the resulting exact comparison is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_sp(H_(m,tau))
                   =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).               (4)

In particular the single explicit choice tau=1/4 gives

    lim B_(n_m)*L_sp(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1.            (5)

Every fixed tau in[1/5,1/4] also gives a limit below79/80. These strict
constants use only the already certified benchmark enclosure, restated
and checked from its saved endpoints in Section7. No new benchmark value,
dynamic program, numerical experiment, parameter scan, solver or
mathematical program is evaluated.

## 2. Exact sums and the complete children next to a selected minimum

For the fixed enclosing(m,tau), let

    v=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],
    M_j=1+S_j,
    d_j=a*c^j,
    b_j=1+d_j,
    u_j=d_j/M_j,
    M_max=M_(m-1), r=c(c-2).

The subscript j here is TREE LEVEL, not the physical time of a particular
occurrence. Every occurrence of b_j has an immediate complete H_j child
on each side. The physical length of H_j is2^j-1.

Direct induction in(2), starting from M_0=1, gives

    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j.                             (6)

Indeed the sum recurrence is S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j, and therefore
M_(j+1)=2*M_j+a*c^j. Substituting(6) and using a=v(c-2) proves the next
case. This is a symbolic identity, not an executed recurrence.

With x=tau/m>0 and gamma=4+2*sqrt(2),

    r=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0.

Thus0<v<1 and c>lambda>2. Formula(6) yields

    1<=M_j<=c^j,
    d_j/M_j<=a/v=c-2,
    M_j<=M_max for0<=j<m.                            (7)

The last inequality also follows directly from the positive sum recurrence.

Now take ANY nonempty selected subset and choose one of its checkpoints
with globally minimum tree level j. Neither adjacent H_j child contains
a selected checkpoint, since every step in those children has level<j.
Consequently the ordinary sums s and t immediately before and after this
chosen checkpoint satisfy

    s>=S_j, t>=S_j.                                 (8)

If it is the first selected checkpoint, s means the actual initial block;
if it is the last, t means the actual terminal block. Both still contain
the complete adjacent child. Tied minimum levels cause no difficulty:
their complete children contain strictly smaller levels. This observation
uses no assumption about records, greedy selection, independence, or an
optimal split of a different recurrence.

## 3. An exact deletion lemma, retaining the next checkpoint cost

Here suppose2<c<=5/2, and suppose C>=1,A>=C are fixed for the whole word.
Keep Q(b)=max{epsilon*b,A}; for this lemma epsilon may be any nonnegative
number. The proof does not change any remaining checkpoint or its parameter.

For a selected minimum-level checkpoint b=b_j=1+d, put

    M=M_j, S=M-1, u=d/M.

By(7),0<u<=c-2<=1/2. The costs Q(b), and Q of any next checkpoint,
are at least A.

First suppose there IS a next selected checkpoint b', with R=Q(b').
All earlier and later factors in(1) cancel when b is deleted. The exact
local comparison of the positive, unsquared factors is

       d/(C*s+Q(b)) * (b'-1)/(C*t+R)
    <= (b'-1)/(C*(s+b+t)+R)                          (9)

if and only if

    d*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q(b))*(C*t+R).           (10)

This pays for the entire deleted physical step b, including its added1.
It also keeps the ACTUAL next checkpoint cost R, even if that step is huge.

To prove(10), subtract its left side from its right side. The difference
is increasing in Q(b). Its derivative with respect to R is C*s+Q(b)-d.
Its derivatives with respect to s and t are respectively
C*(C*t+R-d) and C*(C*s+Q(b)-d). All are positive on the required region:
by(8), C*s+Q(b)>=C*(M-1)+A>=C*M>=M>=2*d, and the same holds for C*t+R.
Therefore the smallest difference occurs at

    s=t=S, Q(b)=R=A.

Define z=(A-C)/M>=0. At these values the difference, divided by M^2, is

    E(C,u,z)=(C+z)^2-u*[C*(2+u)+z]
            =C^2-C*u*(2+u)+z*(2*C-u)+z^2.            (11)

This proves that either of the following WHOLE-WORD conditions suffices
for deleting every selected minimum-level interior checkpoint:

    (I)  C>=c(c-2);
    (II) A>=2*C*M_max.                               (12)

For(I), u*(2+u)<=c(c-2)<=C; the first two terms of(11) have nonnegative
sum, and the other terms are nonnegative because2*C-u>0.

For(II), M<=M_max and M_max>=1 imply

    z=(A-C)/M>=C*(2*M_max-1)/M>=C.

Using u<=1/2, C>=1, z>=C, and2*C-u>0 in(11) gives

    E(C,u,z)>=C^2-(5/4)*C+C*(2*C-1/2)+C^2
              =4*C^2-(7/4)*C>0.                     (13)

Thus(9) holds in either case. Squaring preserves the comparison, all
unchanged factors are nonnegative, and deleting b cannot lower Phi.

## 4. The last selected checkpoint has a different exact comparison

If the minimum-level checkpoint b is LAST, deletion changes the terminal
Huber denominator. Its exact comparison is

       d^2/[(C*s+Q(b))^2*(1+2*t)]
    <= 1/[1+2*(s+b+t)].                             (14)

Since C>=1 and A>=C, C*s+Q(b)>=s+1. It suffices to bound

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)]<=1.          (15)

For fixed s>=0, the fraction without d^2 equals

    [1+2*(s+b)/(1+2*t)]/(s+1)^2,

so it is decreasing in t. Its derivative with respect to s, with the
positive constant1+2*t suppressed, is

    -2*(s+2*b+2*t)/(s+1)^3<0.

It is therefore largest at s=t=S=M-1, by(8). At that endpoint(15) has
left side

    u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)
      <=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1,                             (16)

because M>=1 and0<u<=1/2. For the first inequality the difference after
multiplying by2*M-1 is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, u^2*(3+2*u)
is increasing on[0,1/2] and equals1 at1/2.

Unlike the interior case, this needs neither condition(I) nor(II).
It handles the sole selected checkpoint and the final stage of pruning.

Under either condition in(12), repeatedly choose a currently selected
minimum level and apply Section3 or Section4. The number of selections
strictly decreases, so after finitely many TEXTUAL deletion steps the
selection is empty and its payoff has not decreased. This is an argument
for an arbitrary finite subset, not an executed enumeration or algorithm.
In particular, for EACH fixed parameter pair satisfying(12),

    Phi<=1/(1+2*S_m).                               (17)

No eligibility is lost by deletion: all remaining checkpoints and both
parameters stay unchanged. Threshold equalities are retained. At level0
the children are empty, S_0=0 and M_0=1; all denominators and inequalities
above remain defined, with no division by S_j. The target words have no
zero physical steps, and no argument treats a zero step as positive.

## 5. The frozen spiral constants force the deletion dichotomy uniformly

We now prove(12) for EVERY allowed epsilon,kappa whenever
0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536.

First, c<5/2. Indeed sqrt(2)<10/7, so lambda<17/7, and for m>=9,

    c<=lambda*(1+1/(4*m))
      <(17/7)*(37/36)=629/252<5/2.                  (18)

Also gamma<7, lambda^2<6, and tau<=1/4 give, for m>=2,

    r-1=gamma*tau/m+lambda^2*tau^2/m^2
      <7/(4*m)+3/(8*m^2)
      <=31/(16*m)<2/m.                             (19)

The explicit source formula simplifies exactly. Put X=d_sp*Theta/kappa.
Since d_sp=(1-kappa)*(1+kappa),

    1+(1+kappa)*T_sp=(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa),
    A=epsilon+(C/epsilon)*(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa)
       >=(C/epsilon)*exp(X).                        (20)

The last inequality follows from
(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa)-exp(X)
=kappa*(exp(X)-1)/(1-kappa)>=0. In particular C>1 and A>=C/epsilon>=2*C,
so the baseline assumptions of Sections3--4 hold.

If C>=r, condition(I) already holds. It remains to consider EVERY
parameter pair with C<r, without choosing or optimizing one scaling.
Since C>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2), equations(19) and m>=2 give

    epsilon^2<=C^2-1<r^2-1<4/m+4/m^2<=6/m.

For m>=1536 this implies epsilon<1/16, hence kappa<=epsilon<1/16 and

    d_sp>255/256,
    Theta=pi/2-arctan(epsilon)>3/2-epsilon>23/16.    (21)

Here pi>3 follows, for example, from the strictly shorter inscribed
regular hexagon, and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon follows by integrating
1/(1+t^2)<1 for t>0. These are strict elementary inequalities.

The SAME assumption C<r gives

    kappa*Theta<=log C<log r<r-1<2/m.

Consequently

    X=d_sp*Theta/kappa
       >(d_sp*Theta^2/2)*m
       >(134895/131072)*m>m.                        (22)

The displayed rational is255*529/(256*256*2); its numerator exceeds
its denominator. Combining(20)--(22) gives

    A>16*C*exp(m).                                  (23)

Finally exp(1)>1+1+1/2=5/2>c, while M_max<=c^(m-1) by(7). Thus

    A>16*C*exp(m)>16*C*c^(m-1)>=2*C*M_max.           (24)

This is condition(II). Equations(18)--(24) prove the dichotomy(12)
simultaneously for all allowed parameters. In particular they include
kappa*m tending to zero, tending to infinity, bounded nonconvergent
scalings, fixed parameters, equality kappa=epsilon, and epsilon=1/2.
The proof makes no asymptotic assumption on the parameter choices.

For each pair, (17) bounds every selection, while the empty selection
attains its right side. Taking both suprema proves the exact identity(3).

## 6. Exact physical horizon and asymptotic comparison

For a fixed tau in(0,1/4], equation(6) at depth m gives

    1+2*S_m=2*v*c^m+2*(1-v)*2^m-1.

As m tends to infinity, beta tends1, r tends1, and v=beta/r tends1.
Furthermore c^m/lambda^m=(1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau). The second term
after division by lambda^m tends0 because0<1-v<1 and2/lambda<1;
lambda^(-m) also tends0. Therefore

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m ->2*exp(tau).                 (25)

The accepted ray limit from fact04219554e41d9750 is for exactly the
physical length n_m=2^m-1, with no hidden offset, rescaling, or different
benchmark recurrence. Combining it with(3) and(25) proves(4).

Using Z_1<237/100 as justified in Section7 and exp(1/4)>1+1/4=5/4 gives

    Z_1/(2*exp(1/4))< (237/100)/(5/2)=237/250<1.

For a fixed tau in[1/5,1/4], exp(tau)>=exp(1/5)>6/5 gives the parallel
bound Z_1/(2*exp(tau))<237/240=79/80.

Thus this draft supplies a full-supremum obstruction for frozen MASS
formula(5). It gives no upper bound on W_(n_m)(H_(m,tau)). Weakness of a
lower-bound formula is not a small-error certificate for its word.

## 7. Only saved, accepted benchmark data are used

Section8 of accepted fact04219554e41d9750 certifies the public table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The saved rows1023 and1024 and the hash were read, with no replay of its
generator, exact checker, dynamic program, or any benchmark calculation.

For D80=1208925819614629174706176 its saved upper endpoint is

    D80*B_1024<=H1024=19229705732238461978760749948
                    <15910*D80.                    (26)

For transparency the last product is the exact integer

    15910*D80=19234009790068750169575260160,

whose difference from H1024 is positive. This is a written comparison of
an already certified endpoint, not a new benchmark evaluation.

The square comparison19600<19602 proves sqrt(2)>140/99. Repeated
multiplication, or the identity lambda^2=2*lambda+1, gives

    lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)>6725.                (27)

Indeed (3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0.

The accepted enclosure, applied at b=1,j=10, is

    Z_1<=lambda^(-10)*(sqrt(B_1024)+3/100)^2.

Equations(26)--(27), sqrt(15910)<127, and the exact rational comparisons

    15910+381/50+9/10000<15918,
    15918*100=1591800<1593825=6725*237

imply

    Z_1<(sqrt(15910)+3/100)^2/6725
         <15918/6725<237/100.                        (28)

This is the same already supplied Z_1 enclosure used in sibling finding
8880a32b8502cf2f, with its short derivation repeated here from the accepted
fact and certified table, so that sibling draft is not a premise.

## 8. Optional exact path representation and boundary conventions

For an arbitrary nonnegative word h of length n and fixed allowed
parameters, let E_h be the set of eligible physical indices. Adjoin a
virtual source index-1 and put V_(-1)=1. For q in E_h in chronological
order define

    S(p,q)=sum_(p<t<q) h_t,
    V_q=(h_q-1)^2 * max_(p in{-1} union E_h, p<q)
          V_p/[C*S(p,q)+Q(h_q)]^2.                  (29)

Then the exact best value at these fixed parameters is

    max_(p in{-1} union E_h)
        V_p/[1+2*sum_(t>p)h_t].                     (30)

For the virtual source, S(-1,q) is the entire prefix before q, and the
tail in(30) is the entire word. The proof of(29) is to condition on the
penultimate selected checkpoint; the last factor is then exactly the
displayed edge weight. Induction on q accounts for every chronological
subset. Conditioning on the final selected checkpoint proves(30).
When n=0, the sole term is1. When an intervening sum is0, its denominator
is Q(h_q)>=A>0; zero physical steps simply occur in these ordinary sums.
Eligibility is the non-strict inequality, so threshold equalities are
included. No child parameter is changed.

This recurrence was NOT executed. It is not the comparison result:
Sections2--5 prove the stronger path domination needed for(3), by a
directionally justified deletion on the actual physical word.

## 9. Stress tests, scope, and handoff

The deletion proof was checked symbolically at the boundary cases that
could invalidate it: a first selected checkpoint, a sole selected
checkpoint, a last selected checkpoint, tied minimum levels, level0 and
empty children, and arbitrarily large Q of the next selected checkpoint.
The distinct terminal calculation(14)--(16) cannot be replaced by the
interior calculation. All comparisons keep the deleted step's full
physical size1+d. No assertion about the optimality of a record chain
or of a greedy policy occurs.

The result is deliberately for0<tau<=1/4, and does not establish(3) at
tau=1. It does not transfer any older ray/checkpoint obstruction to this
new denominator. It does not claim a consequence for formula(4),
formula(5a), or for any enlarged geometric parameter domain.

Retrieval found a generic max-plus path expansion in Proposition4 of
arXiv:2102.12264 and unrelated results for the deletion query. None
supplies or is used for this proof. The proof instead stays inside the
already specified scalar functional, finite hierarchy, and elementary
inequalities; no external theorem or unverified geometric construction
is a building block. The project glossary search returned no matches
for the new spiral/deletion notation, so all local symbols are defined
here. This is a jointly derived UNVERIFIED finding, not a verifier verdict.

The assigned one analytical round ends with(3)--(5). All mathematical
computation and formal gates remain closed. No installation, renewal,
finalization, publication, fact-store edit, or swarm control is performed.

## Glossary

L_sp: supremum of the frozen mass functional(1), over all eligible subsets
and exactly0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2, including the empty subset.
Phi: the scalar selection payoff(1).
Theta,c_sp,d_sp,T_sp,H_sp,A_sp: exactly the frozen constants in Section1.
C,A: local abbreviations c_sp,A_sp, not benchmark C_n or seed capacity.
Q(b): max{epsilon*b,A}, the exact target-checkpoint cost in(1).
lambda:1+sqrt(2).
tau,m,beta,c,a,H_j,H_(m,tau),n_m: physical word parameters and hierarchy(2).
S_j,M_j: sum of the fixed enclosing word H_j, and1+S_j.
v: a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)].
d_j,b_j,u_j: level-j checkpoint excess a*c^j, its physical step1+d_j,
and d_j/M_j.
M_max: M_(m-1), largest child mass in the depth-m word.
r: c(c-2).
gamma:4+2*sqrt(2), the coefficient in r=1+gamma*tau/m+lambda^2*tau^2/m^2.
s,t: actual ordinary sums immediately before and after a removed checkpoint.
b,d,M,S,u,z,R,E(C,u,z): local deletion notation in Sections3--4; R is the
next checkpoint's Q cost and E is the exact normalized difference(11).
X: d_sp*Theta/kappa, the activation exponent in Section5.
Omega,G,C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,Z_1: unchanged benchmark quantities from accepted
fact04219554e41d9750, fully restated in Section1.
D80,H1024: denominator and saved certified upper endpoint in Section7.
E_h,V_p,S(p,q): eligible indices, maximal partial product, and exact
ordinary gap sum in the unexecuted path representation(29)--(30).
W_n: original worst-case normalized GD error, mentioned only to delimit
scope; no upper estimate on it is asserted.

# Exact one-bridge defect lifting and the retained slack penalty

Status: complete candidate interface, NOT verified. No fact_submit or numerical
search. The proof uses only accepted interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290 and
the direct calculations below, not earlier unverified findings as premises.

## Definitions

Let h have capacity K=1+sum h_i. On a legal normalized GD trajectory with
minimizer0 define

    I_i_star=-Q_(i,star)=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=0,
    delta_end=f_n-||g_n||^2/2>=0,
    y_end=x_n-g_n,
    V_h=2sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                           -K(K-1)||g_n||^2.

The squared-distance telescope gives

    V_h=||x0||^2-||xn||^2-2sum h_i I_i_star
                         -2(K-1)f_n-K(K-1)||g_n||^2.             (1)

Call the endpoint core when x_n=g_n. In that case interpolation to/from0
forces f_n=||g_n||^2/2, and (1) reduces to

    V_h=||x0||^2-K^2||g_n||^2-2sum h_i I_i_star.                (2)

All sample data and the initial-distance term are retained.

## A. Appending at a saturated outer cut preserves a core-ended defect

Let b be any nonnegative schedule of capacity D with an actual legal
trajectory having endpoint x_m=g_m. Its dimension and all earlier values,
points, gradients and optimum interpolation slacks are arbitrary.
Let c be any nonnegative block of capacity C, and r_ext>0 satisfy

    T_tot=D+C+r_ext, r_ext T_tot=DC.                            (3)

If V_b<0 on that trajectory, then (b,1+r_ext,c) is not primitive.

Proof. Use a scalar base trajectory whose derivative is clip(x,-r_ext/C,1),
starting at D. The entire b block stays on its positive plateau and ends
at1; the bridge lands at-r_ext; c remains on its negative plateau and
ends at-r_ext/C. The base energy is D^2-T_tot^2(r_ext/C)^2=0 by(3).

For the auxiliary vector array retain EVERY supplied b sample. Write
e=g_m=x_m and append the bridge, which lands at-r_ext e. On c set the
constant gradient v_end=-(r_ext/C)e, so its initial point is C v_end
and its final point v_end. Assign each appended value
f_i=<v_end,x_i>-||v_end||^2/2. This auxiliary array may violate old/new
cross interpolation and is not claimed to be legal by itself.

Combine the base and auxiliary arrays in orthogonal coordinates with a
positive multiplier epsilon_mix on every auxiliary squared norm and value.
All base-zero constraints remain valid under this mixture:

* Within the old b samples, the auxiliary data are the original legal data.
* Within the new c samples, the auxiliary gradient is constant.
* A new-to-old base constraint can be zero only at an old query with
  zero remaining b steps, hence at its endpoint x_m=e. The auxiliary
  Q_(new,m) is zero by direct expansion using x_m=g_m=e and f_m=||e||^2/2.
* An old-to-new base constraint can be zero only at a new query with zero
  remaining c steps, hence at x=v_end=g. Direct expansion gives
  Q_(old,new)=Q_(old,star)<=0.
* Every auxiliary optimum constraint is valid: old ones by hypothesis;
  appended incoming ones are zero and outgoing ones are
  -R_i||v_end||^2<=0, where R_i is the remaining c stepsize sum.

Zero steps create only the endpoint duplicates already covered above.
All other base cross constraints are strictly negative. If E_bad is the
finite set of pairs with Q_aux>0, choose

    epsilon_mix=min(1, min_(ij in E_bad) [-Q_base,ij/(2Q_aux,ij)]),

interpreting the inner minimum as1 for an empty set. Every pair in E_bad
has Q_base<0 by the preceding audit, so epsilon_mix>0. The combined data
obey all original-index interpolation inequalities. The explicit conjugate
construction in fact1252b8823bb5a290 interpolates them by one fixed legal
convex differentiable 1-smooth function with minimizer0.

The auxiliary core endpoint permits (2). No new I_i_star is added, and
T_tot^2||v_end||^2=D^2||e||^2 by(3). Therefore

    V_aux=V_b, V_mix=epsilon_mix V_b<0.                         (4)

This is a true defect inheritance rule for the displayed core-ended
witness class. It does not assert that every failure of primitive validity
admits such a witness.

## B. Prepending has an explicit nonnegative retained-slack penalty

Let b now be any nonnegative schedule of capacity D and any actual legal
trajectory (z_j,g_j,f_j) with minimizer0. Let a have capacity A and let
r_ext>0 satisfy

    T_tot=A+D+r_ext, r_ext T_tot=AD,
    kappa_lift=(T_tot/D)^2>1.

Put p_aux=-z_0/r_ext. Before the supplied b trajectory insert constant
gradient p_aux on a, starting at A p_aux and ending at p_aux, then the
bridge1+r_ext. Its next point is -r_ext p_aux=z_0, exactly the supplied
tail start. On the inserted points use f=<p_aux,x>-||p_aux||^2/2.

The scalar base is clip(x,-r_ext/D,1), starting at A; its primitive energy
is zero by the saturated-cut identity. The auxiliary old/new data can be
mixed with this base exactly as in partA, PROVIDED

    <p_aux,y_end><=delta_end.                                  (5)

For completeness, the active cross checks differ from partA as follows.
Tail-to-inserted-end constraints have Q_aux=Q_(tail,star)<=0 because the
inserted endpoint has x=g=p_aux and f=||p_aux||^2/2. Inserted-to-tail-end
constraints have the common value <p_aux,y_end>-delta_end, which is
nonpositive exactly by(5). Every other possibly positive cross constraint
has strictly negative base slack. The same finite positive minimum
therefore gives a legal mixture, including all zero-step duplicates and
all optimum constraints. This proves interpolation, not negativity yet.

Let Sigma_I=sum_(j<m) b_j I_j_star be the weighted ACTUAL tail optimum
interpolation slack. Applying (1) separately to the tail and auxiliary
full array, and using A^2/r_ext^2=kappa_lift, gives the exact identity

    V_aux=kappa_lift V_b+Penalty,

    Penalty=(kappa_lift-1)
                  [||y_end||^2+2 I_m_star+2 Sigma_I]
              +[2T_tot(T_tot-D)/D] delta_end.                  (6)

Every term of Penalty is nonnegative. Here
I_m_star=<g_m,y_end>-delta_end; substitution of
z_m=g_m+y_end into (1) verifies (6) term by term. In particular, a negative
tail V_b alone does NOT imply V_aux<0. A valid defect-lifting certificate
must keep this complete penalty and require kappa_lift V_b+Penalty<0.

At a core endpoint, (5) is automatic and delta_end=I_m_star=0, so

    V_aux=kappa_lift V_b+2(kappa_lift-1)Sigma_I.                (7)

When additionally every weighted incoming optimum slack vanishes,
Sigma_I=0, the negative energy scales exactly by kappa_lift. The new
two-cut Huber construction is in this special class. Discarding Sigma_I
outside it would repeat the same wrong-sign loss of value information
that obstructed the earlier mixed-moment state.

## C. The exact criterion for moving the minimum to the final proximal point

There is a precise normalization test; a reset cannot be presumed. For any
finite fully legal transcript without fixing a particular optimum, define
delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 for every sample, including the terminal one. Add
the proposed optimum jet

    x_star_new=x_n-g_n, g_star_new=0, f_star_new=delta_n.

Keeping ALL original samples, a convex 1-smooth interpolant with this new
optimum exists if and only if

    delta_n<=delta_i for every i.                              (8)

Necessity and sufficiency are both explicit. The new interpolation edges
are Q_(i,star_new)=Q_(i,n)<=0 and
Q_(star_new,i)=delta_n-delta_i. The former already hold in the full
transcript; the latter are exactly(8). When they all hold, translate the
new optimum to(0,0,0) and use the finite interpolation construction of
fact1252b8823bb5a290. Translation changes no GD displacement or primitive
energy and makes x_n=g_n. This supplies a core-ended witness with exactly
the original V_h. When (8) fails, such a preserved-data normalization is
impossible.

For a concrete failure, f(x)=x^2/4, h=(5), x0=1 has
x1=-3/2, g0=1/2, g1=-3/4, f0=1/4, f1=9/16. Thus
delta0=1/8 and delta1=9/32. The proposed new optimum is at-3/4 with
value9/32, and its edge to sample0 violates interpolation by
delta1-delta0=5/32>0. The transcript has V_h=-25<0, so even a genuinely
negative primitive-energy example need not admit this particular
normalization. This does NOT exclude a different core-ended counterexample
for the same schedule; indeed this one-step schedule has others.

## Scope and useful next test

These interfaces give necessary inheritance rules at saturated primitive
cuts, and an exact penalty away from the special all-tight tail class.
They retain all actual prefix interpolation and an explicit fixed-function
realization. They neither provide an improved objective certificate nor
prove that arbitrary primitive failures have core-ended witnesses. The
preserved-data normalization is now exactly characterized by(8), and can
fail on a negative-energy instance. Existence of some OTHER core-ended
witness for every nonprimitive schedule is still a separate closure claim;
no all-schedule induction may assume it.
