# A quantitative nonperiodic policy with exact signed-history closure

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate proof, worker max, 2026-09-26. No new
verifier submission. This answers the quantitative-state assignment with an
explicit horizon-dependent family and a local invariant that controls all
remote planes. It does not prove the unrestricted exact-profile target.

All geometry and estimates used below are proved in this document. Earlier
shared Markov examples and the recursive family are motivation, not premises.
The only accepted result invoked is the definition of the exact benchmark
recursions in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c. No external paper is a black box.

## 1. An exact adjacent criterion for a structured profile class

Write an actual prescribed schedule in chronological order as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(m-1),block_m),

where every block is a finite list of nonnegative steps, s_i is its sum,
and the b_i are checkpoints. Put

    T_i=s_i+1,    A_i=b_i-1,

and assume A_i>=T_(i+1) for 0<=i<m. Thus this part covers the explicit
regime b_i>=s_(i+1)+2; it is not a condition on the unrestricted problem.

Choose positive parameters beta_i with

    beta_i<=A_i/T_(i+1),
    R_i=A_i-T_(i+1) beta_i>=0,                            (1)
    beta_i beta_(i+1)<=1,
    R_i>=beta_i R_(i+1),             0<=i<m-1.            (2)

The last two inequalities are absent when m=1. Define

    q_i=sqrt(A_i beta_i/T_(i+1)),
    a_i=sqrt(T_(i+1) beta_i/A_i),
    alpha_0=1/T_0, alpha_(i+1)=q_i alpha_i,
    zeta_0=e_0,
    zeta_(i+1)=-a_i zeta_i+sqrt(1-a_i^2)e_(i+1),
    omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i.                               (3)

The e_i are an orthonormal basis of R^(m+1). Equations (1) ensure
0<a_i<=1, so every zeta_i is unit and these are genuine Euclidean data.
Fix the SINGLE polyhedral function and its SINGLE Moreau envelope

    J(z)=max(0,<omega_0,z>,...,<omega_m,z>),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                         (4)

Start at X_0=T_0 omega_0=e_0, and set

    X_(i+1)=X_i-(s_i+b_i)omega_i,
    Z_i=X_i-T_i omega_i.                                 (5)

Then every query within ordinary block i, and the following checkpoint
query, has gradient omega_i. In particular all individual entries of each
ordinary block are executed; their order and their size need not be bounded.
At the endpoint after the final ordinary block, the exact normalized gap is

    V(h,beta)=1/T_0^2 product_(i=0)^(m-1)
                         [A_i beta_i/T_(i+1)].           (6)

The origin is an actual closest minimizer, with ||X_0||=1. Therefore (6)
is a lower bound for the true W_n(h), where n is the full supplied length.

If s_i>0 for 0<=i<=m-2, conditions (2) are also NECESSARY for the
prescribed data (3)--(5) to be supported by (4). This exactness concerns
this Markov, homogeneous-plane class, with A_i>=T_(i+1). It does not
claim that the three-profile state for general affine histories collapses.

### Full proof of all old and future comparisons

The Markov formula gives

    <zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-1)^(j-i) product_(l=i)^(j-1) a_l,
                                                        i<j.

Since T_(i+1)alpha_(i+1)=A_i alpha_i a_i, (5) yields

    Z_0=0,
    Z_(i+1)=Z_i-A_i alpha_i(zeta_i+a_i zeta_(i+1)).        (7)

Every summand at index l in (7) is orthogonal to zeta_j for j>=l+1.
Hence <Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for every j>=i. For j<i, taking the other
inner products in (7), and using a_l q_l=beta_l, gives exactly

    <omega_j,Z_i>=-alpha_j^2 H_(j,i),
    H_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1) (-1)^(l-j) R_l
                              product_(v=j)^(l-1) beta_v. (8)

Every adjacent pair of summands, starting at an even offset from j, is
its nonnegative product weight times R_l-beta_l R_(l+1). If the number
of summands is odd, its last summand is nonnegative. Thus (2) proves
H_(j,i)>=0 for EVERY lag. No inherited plane has been discarded.

At a query after an ordinary cumulative sum u in [0,s_i], the proposed
proximal point is

    X_i-(u+1)omega_i=Z_i+d omega_i,  d=s_i-u>=0.

The current plane has value d alpha_i^2. For a future plane j>i its
value is

    d alpha_i^2 (-1)^(j-i) product_(l=i)^(j-1) beta_l.

At odd separation it is nonpositive; at even separation the product
is at most one by grouping the adjacent factors in (2). Thus EVERY
future plane is at most the current one, including arbitrarily distant
ones and the final plane.

For a past plane j<i, its residual (8) is nonpositive, and

    <omega_j,omega_i>/alpha_i^2
       =(-1)^(i-j) product_(l=j)^(i-1) [T_(l+1)/A_l].

At odd separation it is nonpositive; at even separation the product is
at most one because A_l>=T_(l+1). Consequently every past plane is
also at most the current plane. The zero plane is dominated since d>=0.

It follows that omega_i is an active-plane subgradient at the proposed
proximal point. Adding ||x-z||^2/2 to its subgradient inequality proves
that this point uniquely minimizes the proximal objective. This proves
every actual gradient and GD update in the supplied chronological schedule.

For completeness, the envelope in (4) is convex, finite, differentiable,
and 1-smooth. Convexity follows by taking the infimum over z of the jointly
convex defining expression. Existence and uniqueness of the proximal point
follow from coercivity and strict convexity. Adding two proximal
subgradient inequalities gives
||g(x)-g(y)||^2<=<g(x)-g(y),x-y>, with g=x-prox_J(x).
The two minimizing inequalities then identify the derivative as g, and
the displayed bound gives its 1-Lipschitz property.

At the final endpoint u=s_m, the proximal point is Z_m and J(Z_m)=0;
thus 2f(x_n)=alpha_m^2, yielding (6). The minimum of f is zero and its
minimizer set is exactly {J=0}. For any such minimizer z,
<omega_0,z><=0. Since X_0=T_0 omega_0=e_0,

    ||X_0-z||^2=1+||z||^2-2<X_0,z>>=1.

Origin itself is a minimizer, proving the exact normalization.

For necessity of (2), at the checkpoint proximal point Z_(i+2) the
current height is zero. Formula (8) at lag two forces
R_i-beta_i R_(i+1)>=0. At the beginning of ordinary block i, with
s_i>0, the future plane i+2 has value
s_i alpha_i^2 beta_i beta_(i+1), whereas the current value is
s_i alpha_i^2. This forces beta_i beta_(i+1)<=1. Conditions (1) are
the requirement a_i<=1 in the specified Euclidean realization. Together
these prove the asserted exact local criterion.

### A completely explicit policy for every schedule in this regime

One need not assume parameters satisfying (2) exist. Set a temporary
R_m=0, and work backward by

    beta_i=min(1,A_i/[T_(i+1)+R_(i+1)]),
    R_i=A_i-T_(i+1) beta_i.                              (9)

This gives 0<beta_i<=1<=A_i/T_(i+1), all neighboring products are
at most one, and R_i>=beta_i R_(i+1) by the displayed choice. Formula
(6) is its exact computable payoff in the supplied steps. There is no
claim that (9) attains the benchmark or optimizes the complete criterion.

In fact imposing beta_i<=1 loses useful constructions. The quantitative
policy below has beta_i=3/2 at every other checkpoint. Future comparisons
require the PAIR products in (2), not the unjustified individual cap.

## 2. Robust nonperiodic transmission at fixed ordinary sum

Fix any common ordinary sum S>0 and put T=S+1. The ordinary lists may
be completely nonidentical as long as each has that sum. Choose 2k
checkpoints, in pairs indexed by j=0,...,k-1, with

    b_(2j)=T+1,
    b_(2j+1)=rho_j T+1,
    9/4<=rho_j<=5/2.                                    (10)

All rho_j may be chosen independently from this interval after the whole
schedule is prescribed. Set

    beta_(2j)=1/2,  beta_(2j+1)=3/2.

Then the residual coefficients in (1) are

    R_(2j)=T/2,
    R_(2j+1)=T(rho_j-3/2).

The critical-to-large inequality in (2) is exactly rho_j<=5/2. The
large-to-next-critical inequality is exactly rho_j>=9/4. Every adjacent
beta product is 3/4. The final residual is nonnegative too. Hence all
conditions are checked for arbitrary nonperiodic variation of the rho_j,
without a perturbative assumption or a discarded remote comparison.

Each pair has the squared-scale multiplier

    q_(2j)^2 q_(2j+1)^2=3rho_j/4>=27/16.

The precise whole-schedule payoff is consequently

    W_n(h)>=T^(-2) product_(j=0)^(k-1)(3rho_j/4)
                            >=(27/16)^k/T^2.             (11)

The cost T^2 is the actual initial-distance normalization at gradient
scale 1/T. It cannot be silently omitted when S grows with the horizon.
The next section keeps that cost explicitly while making both the total
ordinary complexity and the checkpoint amplitudes grow.

## 3. A prescribed growing family and the COMPLETE benchmark comparison

Define c=13/5, a=c^(-17), and finite words

    H_0=(), H_(r+1)=(H_r,a c^r,H_r).

Let s_r=sum H_r. The exact length and sum are

    |H_r|=2^r-1,
    s_r=a(c^r-2^r)/(c-2).                                (12)

For each integer k>=1, set

    r=floor(k/4)+18,
    block=16 concatenated copies of H_r,
    S=16s_r, T=S+1,
    rho_j=9/4+1/[4(j+1)],   0<=j<k.                     (13)

Take 2k+1 copies of this block with the checkpoints (10) between them.
The rho_j in (13) make an explicitly nonperiodic schedule. More generally,
all conclusions about the lower bound hold for ANY choices in the interval
(10). The length of the complete prescribed schedule is

    N_k=16(2k+1)(2^r-1)+2k.                              (14)

The local criterion constructs one global function on R^(2k+1), with
every gradient and query fixed and with origin its actual closest minimizer.
From (12), a=c^(-17), and r<=k/4+18,

    T<=1+(80/3)a c^r<=(211/3)c^(k/4).

Thus (11) gives the explicit bound

    W_(N_k)(h)>= (9/44521)[27/(16 sqrt(c))]^k
                 >=(9/44521)(27/26)^k.                  (15)

The last comparison uses c<169/64, so sqrt(c)<13/8. It does not hide a
parameter-dependent loss. The right side tends to infinity.

For clarity this provides a comparison to the WHOLE exact B_n sequence,
not just its exponent. In the benchmark recursions of accepted fact
0be8b54e0a33381c, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1>=1, hence U_n^ZJ=1/B_n<=1.
The binomial expansion gives (27/26)^26>2. For k>=338=13*26,

    (9/44521)(27/26)^k > (9/44521)2^13 >1.

Consequently this explicit nonperiodic family satisfies

    W_(N_k)(h)>1>=U_(N_k)^ZJ    for every k>=338.          (16)

The result is confined to the specified family; it says nothing about the
infimum over all schedules at N_k or other horizons.

## 4. The simple benchmark witnesses are weak on the SAME family

Define the full quadratic channel and the sum/single-cut channel by

    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_(t<n)(1-lambda h_t)^2,

    K_n(h)=max{1/[1+2sum h_t],
        max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/
           [(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.

These are the usual quadratic and single-cut Huber lower-bound functionals.
We will prove, on the SAME schedules (13)--(14),

    K_(N_k)<=c^2/(2S),
    Q_(N_k)<=max{1/(4 e k S),(2/3)^(4k)},                (17)

where e=exp(1), and

    max(K_(N_k),Q_(N_k))/U_(N_k)^ZJ ->0.                (18)

This assertion bounds those channels, not the actual worst case, which
grows by (15). It does not assert an upper bound on all scalar functions
or on the verified multi-cut alternating-ray channel.

### Quadratic product estimate for H_r, with all constants

Write b_u=a c^u, and set

    P_r(lambda)=product_(u=0)^(r-1)(1-b_u lambda)^(2^(r-u-1)).

For lambda in (0,1], put z=a lambda and choose the integer v>=17 with
c^v z<=1<c^(v+1)z. Let y=c^v z, so 1/c<y<=1. If a product factor
is zero all the desired upper bounds hold. Otherwise, for r>v,

    log|P_r|/2^(r-v-1)
        =sum_(j=-v)^(r-v-1)2^(-j)log|1-c^j y|.

For j<0 use log(1-c^j y)<=-c^j y. For j>=1 use
log|1-c^j y|<=j log c+log y; these upper bounds are nonnegative,
so extending just their sum to infinity increases it. The resulting bound is

    -2y[1-(2/c)^v]/(c-2)+log[y(1-y)]+2log c
      <=-(13/4)y+log(1/4)+2log c
      <=-5/4+log(169/100)
      <=-5/4+69/100=-14/25<-1/2.                        (19)

To justify the numerical coefficient in the first inequality, since
2/c=10/13 and v>=17, one has (10/13)^v<1/40. For example
(10/13)^5<27/100 and (10/13)^2<3/5, hence its seventeenth power
is less than (27/100)^3(3/5)<1/40. Substitution into
2[1-(2/c)^v]/(c-2) makes it at least 13/4. Also y>1/c=5/13
and y(1-y)<=1/4. Finally log t<=t-1 at t=169/100 proves the last
inequality. These prove (19) without a decimal estimate.

It follows that

    |P_r(lambda)|<=exp(-2^(r-v-2)) for r>v.

For r<=v all factors lie in [0,1], so |P_r|<=1. In particular
|P_r|<=1 always, including lambda=0.

### Quadratic estimate for every complete ordinary/checkpoint pair

First S>35 for r>=18. Indeed the three highest levels of H_18 give
s_18>=1+10/13+100/169=399/169, and S>=16s_18>35. Every checkpoint B
in (10) therefore satisfies

    B<=(5/2)(S+1)+1<=(13/5)S=cS.

If lambda b_(r-1)<=1, all factors of P_r are nonnegative and
|P_r|^16<=exp(-S lambda). For B lambda<=1 the entire macro factor
|P_r|^16|1-B lambda| is at most exp(-S lambda). Otherwise it is at
most (cS lambda-1)exp(-S lambda), whose maximum for nonnegative
S lambda is c exp(-1-1/c)<2/3. The latter exact inequality follows
from

    exp(18/13) > sum_(j=0)^4 (18/13)^j/j!
               =112495/28561 >39/10=(3/2)c.

If lambda b_(r-1)>1, put d=r-v>=1. The preceding polynomial estimate,
lambda S<=(80/3)c^d, and B<=3S give

    |P_r|^16|1-B lambda|
       <=80 c^d exp(-16*2^(d-2))
       <=80 exp(-7d)<5/8<2/3.                           (20)

Here c<e, as e>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3>c; also 2^d>=2d and e>2.
These justify each inequality in (20). Therefore every ordinary/checkpoint
macro, including either type and every supplied rho_j, has scalar factor

    <=max(exp(-S lambda),2/3).

There are 2k such macros. The terminal ordinary block has factor at most
one. After squaring, multiplying by lambda<=1, and maximizing, this proves
the Q bound in (17), since max_(lambda>=0)lambda exp(-4kS lambda)
=1/(4 e k S). Orthogonal diagonalization of a positive semidefinite
quadratic Hessian shows that its normalized objective is a weighted
average of these scalar objectives, so this controls all convex quadratics
in every dimension.

### Every single-cut term

For all levels u, b_u-1<=s_u. When u<=17, b_u<=1 proves it. For
u>=18, (2/c)^u<=2/5, so s_u/b_u=[1-(2/c)^u]/(c-2)>=1.
At a level-u step within a copy of H_r, both its local prefix and its
local suffix contain a whole H_u, so both have sum at least s_u.

For a checkpoint cut, its size is at most cS, and its complete prefix and
suffix sums are each at least S. Its term is at most c^2/(2S).
For an ordinary cut outside the final ordinary block, its complete suffix
contains that final block, hence has sum at least S. Its own H_u prefix
and b_u-1<=s_u bound the other ratio by one; its term is at most 1/(2S).
For an ordinary cut inside the final block, its complete prefix sum is at
least S, while the H_u suffix gives

    (b_u-1)_+^2/(1+2 suffix_sum)
          <=s_u^2/(1+2s_u)<=s_u/2<=S/32.

Its term is at most 1/(32S). The global sum Huber term is at most
1/(2S). This proves the K bound in (17), covering every cut.

### Comparison to the exact DP profile

Here is a direct algebraic bound on the same exact recursions, included
to avoid using an unverified asymptotic formula. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1.
The shifted primitive and dominant joins from fact 0be8b54e0a33381c are

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    Gmass(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

They join C_k,C_(n-k-1) and C_k,B_(n-k-1), respectively, and are
increasing in their positive arguments. For t in [0,1],

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.              (21)

One proof sets x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3) and uses x^3+y^3=1 to write

    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4
      =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.

The two reciprocals are each at least 1/3, whereas xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3.
This also covers the endpoints. Since Omega is the positive root of
X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, substituting
t=A^(3/4)/(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4)) into (21) proves

    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).

Induction in the exact primitive recursion gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).
Also Gmass(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the exact dominant recursion,
using C_0=B_0=1, now gives

    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).                                    (22)

For a split at k, the inductive upper bound is
1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3), proving (22)
for its maximum as well.

Finally N_k<=48k 2^r and, for r>=18,
S>= (40/3)a c^r. Multiplying (17) by (22) bounds the K ratio by
a constant times k^(4/3)(2^(4/3)/c)^r and the first Q ratio by a
constant times k^(1/3)(2^(4/3)/c)^r, plus smaller terms. These tend
to zero since r=floor(k/4)+18 and c^3=2197/125>16. The remaining
Q ratio is bounded by a constant times

    k^(4/3)[2^(1/3)(2/3)^4]^k,

which tends to zero because its bracket is less than one. This proves
(18) with the normalization, horizon dependence, and full B_n sequence
kept throughout.

## 5. Exact scope and the next unresolved interface

The new reusable invariant is (1)--(2), with the exact residual formula
(8) and payoff (6). It checks both cross-plane directions for all lags
and computes the genuine closest-minimizer normalization. Formula (9)
is a prescribed feasible policy for every supplied schedule satisfying
the next-block condition. Equations (10)--(18) give a substantially
stronger explicit nonperiodic policy and a growing example beyond the
quadratic and single-cut channels.

This is a structured subclass of the geometric profile state, with
homogeneous planes and zero-height checkpoint proximal points. It does
not reinstate the disproved compression of general positive-level affine
histories. Those still require Phi,Psi, the actual boundary gradient,
and their distance/sublevel profiles.

The condition b_i>=s_(i+1)+2 is a real remaining restriction. When it
fails, the old-plane correlation product T_(i+1)/A_i can exceed one;
the zero residual sign alone no longer dominates old planes at new
positive-height queries. One must use actual retained height/slack data
there. The current proof supplies neither a partition satisfying this
condition for arbitrary near-optimal schedules nor a uniform B_n
comparison outside it. All results here remain unverified research
evidence until the verifier accepts an allocated submission.

## 6. Exact retained loss when the next-block condition fails

The proof above also identifies the complete additional constraint for
arbitrary A_i=b_i-1>0, including A_i<T_(i+1). Keep definitions (1),
(3)--(8), the Euclidean constraint 0<beta_i<=A_i/T_(i+1), and
T_i=s_i+1. Define the empty products to be one and, for j<i, put

    P_(j,i)=product_(l=j)^(i-1) beta_l,
    Q_(j,i)=product_(l=j)^(i-1) [A_l/T_(l+1)].

The exact old-plane comparison on the whole of ordinary block i is

    H_(j,i)>=s_i[(-1)^(i-j) P_(j,i)
                              -P_(j,i)Q_(j,i)]_+,
    H_(j,i)>=0.                                         (23)

Indeed at its proximal point Z_i+d omega_i the old plane minus the
current plane, divided by alpha_j^2, equals

    -H_(j,i)+d[(-1)^(i-j)P_(j,i)-P_(j,i)Q_(j,i)].

This is affine in d in [0,s_i], whose two endpoints are actual samples.
Thus (23) is necessary and sufficient. Future comparisons are still the
even-lag product constraints P_(i,j)<=1 whenever s_i>0. At odd lag the
sign already makes the future plane nonpositive. These are exact all-plane
conditions; in particular the positive-part term in (23) must be kept
when a product of A_l/T_(l+1) is less than one.

They give a concrete interval for appending a new block to a legal prefix.
Suppose block i has already been constructed and A=A_i>0 and the entire
next block, with T=T_(i+1)=s_(i+1)+1, are prescribed. Its only new scalar
is beta=beta_i. For every j<i with i-j odd, use the already retained
quantities

    p_j=P_(j,i)>0,  q_j=Q_(j,i)>0,
    c_j=H_(j,i)/p_j>=0.

The exact admissible interval is

    0<beta<=Upper,
    beta>=Lower,

    Upper=min{A/T,
                  min_(j<i, i-j odd, s_j>0) 1/p_j},
    Lower=max{0,
                  max_(j<i, i-j odd)
                 (A-c_j)/[T-s_(i+1)(1-A q_j/T)_+]}.     (24)

An empty inner minimum is +infinity and an empty inner maximum contributes
zero. Each denominator is at least T-s_(i+1)=1. A nonpositive candidate
for Lower is harmless. These formulas keep every old query and do not
assert that the interval is nonempty.

To check them, (8) gives, at the new even lag from j,

    H_(j,i+1)/p_j=c_j-A+T beta.

Substituting this and the new products p_j beta and q_j A/T into (23)
is exactly the lower bound in (24). If its numerator is nonpositive the
new residual is nonnegative automatically; otherwise the same bound also
implies that nonnegativity. At a new odd lag the old nonnegative residual
is increased by a nonnegative summand and the bracket in (23) is negative,
so there is no extra condition. The immediately preceding plane likewise
has residual A-T beta>=0. A future new plane at an old positive-height
query gives beta p_j<=1 precisely for the indicated even separations;
this proves Upper. Its comparisons at zero-height old proximal points
hold by the exact forward orthogonality in (7). This proves both necessity
and sufficiency of (24) for appending the supplied complete block.

For any sequence of accepted intervals, the same fixed-origin normalization
and product payoff (6) remain exact, with all directions fixed before the
actual GD run. Choosing the largest allowed beta maximizes the immediate
new squared gradient for that retained prefix; no greedy optimality for
the entire remaining schedule is claimed.

### A precise test of the omitted positive-height term

Take three one-step ordinary blocks, each (1), and two bridges each 2.
Thus the complete schedule is (1,2,1,2,1), T_0=T_1=T_2=2 and
A_0=A_1=1. The tempting parameters beta_0=beta_1=1/2 have
R_0=R_1=0, beta_0 beta_1=1/4, so every zero-residual test and
every future-plane test passes. But (23) at (j,i)=(0,2) requires

    0 >= 1*(1/4-1/16)=3/16,

which fails. Concretely their gradients are omega_0=e_0/2,
omega_1=-e_0/4, omega_2=e_0/8. At the initial query e_0/4 of the
last ordinary block, the intended proximal point is e_0/8. The old
plane omega_0 has value 1/16 there, while the proposed current plane
omega_2 has value 1/64. This is the exact old-at-new violation.

With beta_0=1/2 retained, (24) gives Lower=4/5>Upper=1/2,
so no choice of the next correlation fixes that prefix within this
specified geometry. Re-selecting the earlier parameter does: take

    beta_0=8/19, beta_1=1/2.

Now H_(0,2)=3/19 and the right side of (23) is also 3/19;
all the other comparisons hold. Formula (6) gives payoff 1/76.
This is even the maximum in this two-parameter Markov class: its only
nonautomatic old comparison is

    beta_0(3-5 beta_1/4)<=1,

with 0<beta_0,beta_1<=1/2. The objective beta_0 beta_1/16 increases
up to beta_1=1/2, where beta_0=8/19 is its largest feasible value.
This restricted calculation is not the true W_5 of that schedule.

Thus the arbitrary-schedule obstacle has an explicit additional term and
a concrete failed extension, rather than a presumed missing feasibility
theorem. The nonperiodic family of Sections 2--4 avoids that term by an
actual prescribed mass condition. A uniform policy that controls (23)--(24)
while attaining the complete B_n benchmark remains unresolved.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001146 -->
Two exact boundary counterexamples for extending max2's one-checkpoint projection argument. Status: joint UNVERIFIED mathematics; these are not accepted facts. The complete common construction and proofs are also included in172014083da57ddf, but the explicit tests are given here.

1. A false all-count radius inequality.
Set epsilon=kappa=1/2, theta_sp=arctan2, C=(sqrt5/2)exp(theta_sp/2), T=(4/3)(exp(3theta_sp/2)-1), H=C(1+3T/2)-1 and A=1/2+2(1+H)>5/2. Let sigma=1/(A+5/2), so0<sigma<1/5. Define the finite convex maximum on R^2
phi(z)=sigma max{0, C(z_0-1+sigma/2), (z_0-1+sigma/2)/2-z_1+sigma},
and f_loc(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].
For d>0 let Hub_d(z)=0 if z<=0,z^2/2 if0<=z<=d,and dz-d^2/2 ifz>=d.
On R^3 define the STATIC function
F(x)=f_loc(x_0,x_1)/2+(25sigma^2/2)f_loc(x_1/(5sigma),x_2/(5sigma))+Hub_(25sigma^2)(x_2)/2.
This is exactly the source construction with physical word(10,10), BOTH steps selected, all ordinary blocks empty, D'_0=1,D'_1=5sigma,D'_2=25sigma^2,ell'_i=0 and actual P_i=Q_i=0. Each checkpoint meets b=2(1+epsilon^-2)=10. No constant or curve is retuned.

The local zero wedge is z_0<=1-sigma/2 and (z_0-1+sigma/2)/2-z_1+sigma<=0: both displayed endpoint planes are necessary and sufficient. A nonnegative envelope has the same zero set because its defining quadratic and phi are nonnegative and attain their infimum. Therefore the full minimum set of F is the intersection of BOTH scaled wedges and x_2<=0; its minimum is0 and it contains0.

The point x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0) is at the first center. Its second local input is(1/5,0); the second endpoint plane there is -2/5+5sigma/4<-3/20, and the horizontal plane is also negative. The tail is0. Thus x_* is a minimizer of the FULL function. The normal e_0-x_*=sigma(1/2,-1,0) is the active first endpoint normal. Every minimizer x therefore satisfies <e_0-x_*,x-x_*><=0. Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves
dist(e_0,argmin F)^2=5sigma^2/4.
The proposed all-count lower bound sigma^2[(P_0+epsilon+epsilon^-1)^2+Q_0^2] would be25sigma^2/4 and is FALSE by a factor of five. In a genuine one-stage function the tail constrains x_1<=sigma Q_0; here the genuine tail constrains x_2<=0, and the first constraint is absent.

For actual trajectory evidence, from e_0 the gradients at the two queried points are
g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
x_1=e_0-10g_0=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
x_2=x_1-10g_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).
These gradients follow from the proximal point equal to the local center at local input(1,0); sigma(1/2,-1) is an active subgradient. At each query all other local terms vanish. At x_2 both local terms vanish and F(x_2)=(25sigma^2)^2/4. Its TRUE payoff is250sigma^2, whereas the false radius would give50sigma^2.

For smoothness: if z=prox_phi(x),g=x-z, subgradient monotonicity yields ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>; comparison of the minimizing quadratics gives gradient g and the 1-smooth upper inequality. In F, the coefficients1/2 count each coordinate at most twice, proving the 1-smooth upper inequality for F. Applied to F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x with move -grad F(x)+grad F(y), it gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2. Hence EVERY directed original interpolation row, including the terminal point and minimizers, holds. This is a genuine static convex 1-smooth test, not independent local jets. It is OUTSIDE the H_(m,tau) family and proves NO escape on that family.

2. Equal ordinary sums do not fix actual backward outputs.
Keep epsilon=kappa=1/2 and the same C. Let theta_*=arctan(3/4), R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=4R_*/5,q_*=3R_*/5 and A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C. The backward endpoint is(1/2,-1). For a final ordinary step1, the cap condition1<=kappa[epsilon+(1-1)C]=1/4 fails. The uncapped equation <(1/2,-1),e(theta)-2t(theta)>/R(theta)=0 reduces to2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0, giving precisely theta_*; its normal n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1) supports the spiral arc since exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta) is maximized atdelta0. The new state is(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*).

For chronological block(A_*,1), the preceding A_* caps, because the cap right side is1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Thus its actual outputs are P=A_*C+p_*,Q=q_*>0. For chronological block(1,A_*), its last step already caps since A_*>1+3/(2C); the earlier unit step stays capped. Its outputs are P=(A_*+1)C,Q=0. Both sums equal A_*+1 but their outputs differ, with p_*<C. This refutes a sum-only output or deletion shortcut. It does not refute the proved lower bound P+Q>=sum alpha, which follows individually from every arc slope p+q>=1, and it does not refute the actual full construction.

These tests support only the explicit failed extrapolations just stated. The all-count actual-family comparison on small tau and the original exact-profile goal remain OPEN. No mathematical program, solver, numerical evaluation, or fact_submit was used.

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/shared_evidence/5bd01e2b19002a59.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001147 -->
# Variable-matrix repair of a terminal-chord certificate class

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No new solver or
fact_submit call. All identities below are proved directly. The theorem has
arbitrary positive length and does not require a saturated composition cut.
It is a support-and-cumulative-sign theorem, not general credit re-selection.

## 1. Definitions and exact statement

Let n>=1 and let h_0,...,h_(n-1)>0. Put

 P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, p_i=1+P_i, s=P_n, C=1+s,
 R_i=sum_(t>=i) h_t=C-p_i, with R_n=0.

Use the actual GD substitutions x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                            -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Suppose a real signed array lambda cancels every value coefficient in
V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij and its FULL remainder M is PSD on independent
g_0,...,g_n. Suppose its only possibly nonzero entries are

 lambda_(i,i+1)=a_i, lambda_(i+1,i)=b_i       (0<=i<n),
 lambda_(i,n)=c_i                           (0<=i<n-1).

The latter are the nonadjacent terminal chords; the adjacent last incoming
edge is a_(n-1), not another c entry. Set c_(n-1)=0 for summation and

 zeta_k=sum_(i<=k)c_i,     0<=k<n.

Assume the following cumulative condition at each long nonfinal step:

           zeta_k<=0 whenever k<n-1 and h_k>1.             (1)

Then the following array is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_h:

 a_i^0=2P_(i+1)p_i/h_i,
 b_i^0=2P_(i+1)p_(i+1)/h_i,                              (2)

on the two adjacent directions, with all other entries zero. Its complete
formal remainder is

          M^0=sum_(i<n) w_i ||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2,            (3)
 w_i=(P_(i+1)/h_i)[p_i-(h_i-1)p_(i+1)]>=0.

In particular, if the input is an actual E_theta certificate represented
by lambda, with lambda_(i,n)>=-theta h_i, 0<=theta<1, and all other entries
nonnegative, it can be repaired to the ordinary certificate (2). The proof
does not need the numerical bound theta once the displayed support, full
PSD, flow and cumulative condition are assumed. The incoming chords c_i
can have both signs; only their indicated cumulative sums are restricted.

No property is asserted for a certificate with extra remote prefix edges,
extra outgoing terminal edges, or positive zeta_k at a step h_k>1. All these
are genuine remaining cases for the general cone question.

## 2. Full affine-kernel identity and weighted circulation

This section holds for any full-support real multiplier array. Let e be
the all-one column, L=diag(lambda e)-lambda the row Laplacian, and let A
have A_ii=1, A_it=h_t for t<i and zero entries for t>i. Put

 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D_quad=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).

Value cancellation is exactly L^T e=omega, and expansion gives

                M=D_quad+sym(L A),   sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.         (4)

For completeness, the position terms in sum lambda Q are sym(L(A-I)).
The squared-gradient differences contribute sym(L)-diag(omega)/2.
Adding the diagonal gradient terms of V gives (4).

Evaluating this value-canceling quadratic on a constant-gradient legal
affine segment gives e^T M e=0: all Q vanish, and V=0. Such a segment can
be realized by a scalar Huber function started sufficiently far along an
affine ray. Since M is PSD, M e=0. Equivalently this follows by considering
the quadratic on e+t v for arbitrary v and both signs of t.

As A e=p, equation M e=0 yields

                 Lp=-2D_quad e-A^T omega=diag(p)omega.         (5)

Here, for i<n,

 (A^T omega)_i=-2h_i+2h_i P_(i+1),
 -2h_i(2-h_i)-(A^T omega)_i=-2h_i p_i;

and the terminal component is 2s(s+1). These explicitly verify (5).
Combining (5) with L^T e=omega shows that

                 mu_ij=lambda_ij p_j                    (6)

has equal incoming and outgoing sums at every node. Indeed (5) says
sum_j lambda_ij p_j=p_i sum_j lambda_ji. Thus mu is a real circulation,
though its terminal edges can still be negative.

The separate value-flow condition must be retained: with u_i=1/p_i,

          sum_j mu_ij(u_j-u_i)=omega_i.                  (7)

Circulation alone does not imply (7), positivity, or PSD.

## 3. The input adjacent coefficients are forced by the chords

Sum the exact value flows over vertices 0,...,k. The only crossing edges
are the adjacent pair k,k+1 and the terminal chords from this prefix, so

               a_k-b_k+zeta_k=-2P_(k+1).                 (8)

Sum the circulation balances (6) over the same prefix:

               a_k p_(k+1)-b_k p_k+C zeta_k=0.           (9)

For k=n-1 the formula counts the last adjacent incoming edge only in a_k;
c_(n-1)=0 prevents double counting. Solving (8)-(9) gives

 a_k=a_k^0-(R_k/h_k)zeta_k,
 b_k=b_k^0-(R_(k+1)/h_k)zeta_k.                         (10)

This retains all actual coefficients rather than selecting an assumed
matrix. It follows from the full input PSD through its affine kernel.

## 4. A diagonal test in the full gradient-difference coordinates

For each k<n let z^(k) be the formal gradient column equal to1 on indices
0,...,k and0 thereafter. This is a legitimate direction in the FULL
original gradient space, regardless of whether it is itself a legal run.
For ANY real array satisfying value cancellation and (5), a direct
calculation yields

 (z^(k))^T M z^(k)
     =-P_(k+1)^2
          +sum_(i<=k<j)lambda_ij[1+p_j-p_(k+1)].         (11)

Here are all terms of that calculation. Set every value to zero. In a Q
with both indices in the prefix, the gradient coefficient is p_i-p_j.
For a prefix-to-suffix pair it is p_i-p_(k+1)+1/2. For a suffix-to-prefix
pair it is1/2, and it is0 inside the suffix. Sum (5) over prefix rows to
replace the first group and the p_i-p_j part of the forward crossing group
by -2sum_(i<=k)h_i p_i. The remaining forward coefficient is
p_j-p_(k+1)+1/2. By the value cut flow, the total reverse crossing mass is
the forward mass plus2P_(k+1); this adds another1/2 to each forward weight
and contributes P_(k+1). Finally

 sum_(i<=k)h_i(1-h_i)-2sum_(i<=k)h_i p_i+P_(k+1)
       =-sum_(i<=k)h_i^2-2sum_(i<=k)h_i P_i
       =-P_(k+1)^2.

This proves (11) without suppressing any matrix direction.

For the stated adjacent-plus-terminal support, (11) becomes

 (z^(k))^T M z^(k)
       =-P_(k+1)^2+a_k+(1+R_(k+1))zeta_k
       =w_k+[(h_k-1)R_(k+1)/h_k]zeta_k.               (12)

The second equality follows from (10), R_k=h_k+R_(k+1), and
a_k^0-P_(k+1)^2=w_k. Because M is PSD, the left side is nonnegative.

If h_k>1 and k<n-1, (1) makes the last term in (12) nonpositive, whence
w_k>=0. If k=n-1 it vanishes because R_n=0, with the same conclusion.
If 0<h_k<=1, the formula

 w_k=(P_(k+1)/h_k)[(2-h_k)p_k+h_k(1-h_k)]

is strictly positive directly. Thus every w_k in (3) is nonnegative.

## 5. Complete repaired certificate

The coefficients (2) are nonnegative. Across edge k their net forward
flow is a_k^0-b_k^0=-2P_(k+1), so the difference between adjacent cuts
is -2h_k, with the terminal flow2s. Hence every original value coefficient
cancels. Direct expansion of the adjacent Q terms gives a tridiagonal
gradient matrix. Its affine kernel vanishes, as can be checked from (2)
or (9) with zeta=0. Its off-diagonal entry on edge k is

 [ -a_k^0+(h_k-1)b_k^0 ]/2=-w_k.

Therefore the full matrix is precisely the weighted path Laplacian (3):
the diagonal entries are w_(k-1)+w_k, with endpoint conventions. Since
all w_k>=0, (3) proves PSD on ALL independent original gradients. No equality
between two formal gradients, no rank-one test, and no primal-witness-only
PSD inference is used.

The construction actually changes the matrix. It does not assert that
M^0-M is PSD, and the next family shows that difference can be indefinite.

## 6. A nonsaturated signed input family at every positive length n>=2

Take h_i=t=4/3 for all i<n, let d_i=g_i-g_(i+1), and put

 w_k=(k+1)[2-t^2+k t(2-t)],
 epsilon=(2-t^2)/(4t n^2)=1/(24n^2),
 theta=epsilon/t=1/(32n^2).

Start with (2) and add these four signed changes:

 Delta lambda_01=n epsilon,
 Delta lambda_10=(n-1)epsilon,
 Delta lambda_0n=-epsilon,
 Delta lambda_1n=epsilon.                              (13)

For n=2 the last change is added to the existing adjacent coefficient.
Every actual E_theta multiplier is nonnegative: add theta h_i=epsilon
to every incoming terminal edge. In particular the only negative signed
entry, lambda_0n=-epsilon, becomes0. Value flow is unchanged at every node.
For n>=3 the chord cumulative sums are zeta_0=-epsilon and zeta_k=0 for
k>=1. For n=2 the sole relevant cumulative sum is zeta_0=-epsilon.
Thus (1) holds.

For a complete independent check of input PSD, expand (13):

 Delta M=epsilon[nQ_01+(n-1)Q_10-Q_0n+Q_1n]
   =-epsilon(n-1)(t-1)||d_0||^2
      -epsilon sum_(k=1)^(n-1)[1+t(n-1-k)]<d_0,d_k>.    (14)

This follows by collecting g_0^2, g_1^2 and g_0g_1 and then using
g_j-g_1=-sum_(k=1)^(j-1)d_k. All value coefficients in (14) are zero.
Write beta_k=1+t(n-1-k). Completing squares gives

 M=sum_(k=1)^(n-1) w_k ||d_k-epsilon beta_k d_0/(2w_k)||^2
       +[w_0-epsilon(n-1)(t-1)
                   -epsilon^2/4 sum_(k=1)^(n-1)beta_k^2/w_k]||d_0||^2.
                                                                  (15)

Here w_0=2-t^2=2/9, w_k>=(k+1)w_0>0, beta_k<tn, and
sum_(k=1)^(n-1)beta_k^2/w_k<t^2 n^3/w_0. Consequently the two deductions
from w_0 in the last coefficient of (15) are respectively less than
w_0/4 and w_0/(64n). Their sum is strictly less than w_0, so the last
coefficient is positive. This proves full input PSD (strict positivity
in the difference coordinates) for every n>=2.

The repair (2) removes (13), producing sum w_k||d_k||^2. By (14), M^0-M
has a nonzero d_0 d_1 coefficient but zero d_1^2 coefficient. Restricting
to (d_0,d_1) gives determinant -epsilon^2 beta_1^2/4<0. Thus the matrix
change is indefinite, even though both complete endpoint matrices are PSD.

There is no saturated cut in any nonempty constant-t block. For a cut at
step i, let A_cut=1+it, D_tail=1+(n-i-1)t and r=t-1=1/3. Since A_cut,D_tail>=1 and
A_cut+D_tail=2+(n-1)t, their product is minimized at an endpoint and obeys

 A_cut D_tail>=1+(n-1)t=(4n-1)/3,
 rC=(4n+3)/9,
 A_cut D_tail-rC>=(8n-6)/9>0.

Thus lengths n>=3 in this family are outside the recursive class requiring
an exactly saturated first cut and a <=2-step eventual continuation leaf.
The new support-based repair is not merely that saturated-cut argument.

## 7. Scope and remaining interface

Condition w_k>=0 is equivalent to

 h_k<=[1-p_k+sqrt(p_k^2+6p_k+1)]/2<2.

Thus every schedule admitted by this repair theorem has C<1+2n. This is
an unbounded-length re-selection class, not an improving primitive seed
family or an asymptotic separation from the exact objective profile.

The circulation equations (6)-(7) isolate why deleting negative chords
arbitrarily is invalid: both conservation and reciprocal-p drift must hold.
The argument succeeds here because the support forces (10) and the full
PSD then forces each coefficient of the independent repaired path squares.
Section 8 below allows arbitrary backward edges and also gives an explicit
weighted-cut criterion for full-support arrays. No result here is claimed
as a new fact until verifier acceptance; no verifier allocation was used.

## 8. General full-support cut identity and a stronger repair theorem

Return to a real array with FULL support, exact value cancellation, full
PSD, and nonnegative entries except possibly into the terminal. Define,
for each k<n, the following weighted actual forward and backward cut sums:

 F_k=sum_(i<=k<j)lambda_ij[p_j-p_(k+1)],
 B_k=sum_(j<=k<i)lambda_ij[p_k-p_j].                       (16)

The adjacent edge contributes zero to either sum. Every term in B_k is
nonnegative; F_k can be negative only through signed incoming-terminal
edges. There is no omitted interpolation coefficient in these definitions.

The exact FULL matrix identity on the cut direction is

 (z^(k))^T M z^(k)=w_k+[(h_k-1)F_k-B_k]/h_k.             (17)

To derive it, let O_k and I_k be the total forward and backward crossing
masses without weights. The value-flow cut says O_k-I_k=-2P_(k+1).
The weighted circulation cut says

 sum_(i<=k<j)lambda_ij p_j
                   =sum_(j<=k<i)lambda_ij p_j.

The left side is p_(k+1)O_k+F_k and the right side is p_k I_k-B_k.
Using p_(k+1)-p_k=h_k therefore gives

 O_k=[2p_k P_(k+1)-F_k-B_k]/h_k.

Formula (11) equals -P_(k+1)^2+O_k+F_k, which becomes (17) because
2p_k P_(k+1)/h_k-P_(k+1)^2=w_k. This proves (17) for the complete
multiplier array and full original matrix.

**Full-support repair criterion.** If at every k with h_k>1 the input
array satisfies the explicit inequalities

                       (h_k-1)F_k<=B_k,                 (18)

then the ordinary array (2) and full PSD remainder (3) repair it.
Indeed (17) and full input PSD force w_k>=0 at such k; at h_k<=1 the
direct positive formula in Section 4 does so. Section 5 checks every
actual coefficient, value flow and formal PSD direction of the output.
Condition (18) is a checkable sufficient condition on a supplied full
certificate, not a proposed characterization of all credit certificates.

**Arbitrary backward edges.** In particular, the original repair remains
valid if ANY additional nonnegative backward edges i->j with i>j are
allowed. Assume every forward edge with j<n is adjacent, while arbitrary
signed incoming-terminal chords obey (1). Then

 F_k=R_(k+1) zeta_k, B_k>=0.

At a long nonfinal step (1) gives F_k<=0; at the final step F_(n-1)=0.
Thus (18) holds. Extra terminal-to-prefix edges and long backward prefix
edges are all covered, with no assumption that their coefficients vanish.
The signed constant-4/3 family in Section 6 supplies nonordinary inputs at
arbitrarily large lengths in this class, outside the saturated-cut class.

**Quantitative constraint on genuinely long steps.** Conversely, if
w_k<0, then (17) forces

                    (h_k-1)F_k-B_k>=-h_k w_k>0.          (19)

Such a step necessarily has h_k>1. Hence some positive forward multiplier
crosses the cut and lands strictly beyond k+1; adjacent-only forward mass
cannot supply (19). If the input is an actual E_theta certificate with
nonnegative multipliers Lambda, so that

 lambda_(i,n)=Lambda_(i,n)-theta h_i,

and other lambda entries equal their actual Lambda entries, define
F_k^actual by (16) with Lambda. Then the exact conversion is

             F_k=F_k^actual-theta P_(k+1)R_(k+1),

and (19) becomes

 (h_k-1)F_k^actual-B_k
      >=theta(h_k-1)P_(k+1)R_(k+1)-h_k w_k.              (20)

Thus terminal credit does not eliminate the positive forward-cut weight
obligation for these long steps. It increases its required actual weight
by the displayed amount. Equation (20) is a necessary coefficient bound,
not a bound on what those weights can achieve and not a universal repair.
No conclusion about asymptotic objective minimax follows from it alone.

# Matched initial maximizers: exact H4 expansion and two scoped obstructions

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation81, ended baseline80.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; guidance7ffa301a1ff95b8b.
One invocation, completed ceiling81/attempted82, controller cap1.
The active TASK prohibits formal calls/fact_submit. No assertion below is
a new accepted fact. All new identities and supporting ingredients are
derived together in this report. The original minimax target remains OPEN.

## Verdict

The instructed choice eta=M-2S3 really does align the initial maximizer.
Substitution of the COMPLETE left H3 identity gives the exact H4 expansion
(4.3), retaining four physical H2 certificates, both H3 bridge terms,
the parent bridge term, every actual auxiliary projection, and all actual
maximization remainders. Its new signed exchange Xi is given explicitly
in (4.2), (5.2), and (5.3). The common-p0 normal terms and squares have
been combined algebraically; no positivity is inferred merely from their
common center. In particular, the left terminal auxiliary qL cancels out
of the combined expression, but a negative terminal-direction term remains.

Two PRECISE sufficient transfer rules are refuted on actual legal H4
histories using the original same-m steps:

* Transfer A: the combined auxiliary/radial exchange Xi is nonnegative.
  On D0=[-1,1], x0=1/100, and d=1, the entire actual H4 stays in the
  quadratic core, and Xi<-6|x7|^2. Every physical and auxiliary normal
  vanishes. The same inequality fails on the inherited cap/cap left H3,
  where Xi<-16(k/C)^2 and BOTH physical H2 reserves are zero.
* Sole repair B: allow the exchange to consume the ENTIRE left H3 bridge
  square and both of its normal terms, while retaining the parent bridge
  as a separately nonnegative term. The candidate Xi+B_i>=0 is false on
  the ONE fixed body D0=[-k/C,1], start x0=C, and the explicit finite
  parameter d=1+2hK(k/C)/sqrt(delta). Its exact residual is
  -delta+h^2K^2(k/C)^2/D^2<-3delta/4, where delta>0 is defined in (8.1).

These are counterexamples to the stated transfers, NOT to H4 U. The core
example has full H4 ratio below3479/10000. The cap/cap example has a
strictly positive parent-prefix U gap; the unused parent bridge pays its
negative repaired exchange. Thus the broader idea of a simultaneous
combination of ALL bridge terms remains open. We do not label a formal
negative coefficient a counterexample, or claim that the entire aligned
H4 strategy is impossible.

One substantive mechanism, including its sole repair, was used. No H5/H6
search or second all-depth mechanism is opened. There is no all-body H4
proof and no quantified all-depth total loss. The stopping point is the
exact parent-bridge payment needed by the cap history, in (8.5).

## 1. Actual word, parameters, physical indices, and scope

Fix one integer m>=1536, and throughout all children set

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
 H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.

Use the following local names, which never reset m:

 A=1+a, B=1+beta, t=S2=2A+B, C=1+t=4+2a+beta,
 k=a*c^2=beta*c, K=a*c^3=beta*c^2,
 ell=C+k, s=S3=t+ell=2C+k-1,
 b=1+K, h=s+b, S4=2s+b.

Here ell and h are prefix MASSES, not individual physical steps. H4 is

 (A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A,1+K,A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A).

For one fixed nonempty compact convex body D0 containing0 in a finite
dimensional Euclidean space, let P be its Euclidean projection and set

 F(x)=max_(g in D0)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(x)=max_(g in D0)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.

Physical points obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i P(x_i). Set g_i=P(x_i),
f_i=F(x_i), and retain the terminal x15,g15. Label

 x=x0, u=x3, z=x4, v=x7, w=x8, uR=x11, zR=x12, y=x15.

The left H3 is x to v; its H2 children are x to u and z to v.
The right H3 is w to y; its H2 children are w to uR and zR to y.
Thus w=v-b*g7 and z=u-(1+k)*g3.

For every d>=1 define the actual auxiliary denominators

 D=d+2s, M=d+2S4=D+2h,
 eta=M-2s=D+2b, E=eta+2t, E_R=d+2t.

The identities M=E+2ell and D=E_R+2ell hold. All auxiliary gradients
below are projections onto the SAME D0:

 p0=P(x/M), qL=P(v/eta), rL=P(z/E), rO=P(w/D),
 rR=P(zR/E_R), q=P(y/d).

The left H3 initial maximizer is precisely p0. The right H3 initial
maximizer is precisely rO. None of qL,rL,rO,rR,q is identified with
another formal gradient without a legal construction proving equality.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixes the original interpolation orientation;
its necessity proof was read. Accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10-11
were read, but its exact-root theorem is not used at a perturbed bridge.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 requires a full original-function-class upper
before its separation conclusion applies. No such upper is asserted.
No universality claim about projection bodies or affine-height lifts is
used. The original normalization W=2R and PROBLEM.md remain unchanged.

## 2. Projection identities, every remainder, and radial terms

Projection optimality is <x-P(x),g-P(x)><=0 for g in D0. It follows
by differentiating squared distance on segments; conversely the square
expansion proves sufficiency. Strict convexity gives uniqueness.
Adding the two projection conditions proves
||P(x)-P(y)||^2<=<x-y,P(x)-P(y)>, hence P is1-Lipschitz.
The maximum defining F is attained at P(x); its two maximizing
inequalities bound the first-order remainder by
||v||||P(x+v)-P(x)||. Thus F is convex, differentiable and grad F=P.

At any physical or auxiliary sample of F define the signed ordered row

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
     =<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                         (2.1)

For a word of mass T, input X and endpoint Y, its physical primitive
energy is

 V=2sum h_i(f_i-f_N)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -T(T+1)||g_N||^2
  =||X||^2-||Y+T P(Y)||^2.                         (2.2)

The equality follows by telescoping the squared physical iterates and
substituting f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. It does not assert V>=0.

For N>0, p=P(X/N), and any Z in D0 define

 R(X,N,Z)=N||p-Z||^2+2<X-Np,p-Z>.                  (2.3)

Both terms are nonnegative. If Z=sum theta_i g_i is a convex mixture,

 R=N||p-Z||^2-2N sum theta_i Q_(i,p).

The sample p has input X/N. Also, without omitting its normal term,

 N R(X,N,Z)=||X-NZ||^2-||X-Np||^2.                 (2.4)

For a word of mass T with trailing d>=1 put N=d+2T,
qY=P(Y/d), and Z=(X-Y+(d+T)qY)/N. The physical gradient weights in
X-Y are the actual nonnegative steps, and the extra qY weight is d+T;
their sum is N, so Z belongs to D0. Expanding (2.4) gives

 U_N(X)-U_d(Y)
   =[V+L(T,Y,d)]/N+R(X,N,Z),                       (2.5)

 L(T,Y,d)=2T<Y,P(Y)-qY>
                     +T^2(||P(Y)||^2-||qY||^2).   (2.6)

For 1<=d1<d2 and q_i=P(Y/d_i), optimality gives
d1(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)<=2<Y,q1-q2>
<=d2(||q1||^2-||q2||^2). Consequently both the squared-norm and
inner-product differences are nonnegative, proving L>=0. Thus every
subtraction of L or R below is an actual subtraction of a nonnegative
term, not an ignorable error.

## 3. Complete H3 library, derived in this joint draft

The full H2 certificate is in AppendixA with all eight ordered rows,
three actual adjacent-gradient squares and their explicit coefficients.
For a generic left word of mass T with capacity C0=1+T, endpoint V0,
gradient A0=P(V0), and actual bridge1+k0, set

 W0=V0-(1+k0)A0, H=C0+k0,
 N=J+2H, r=P(W0/J),
 ZP=(X-W0+(J+H)r)/N,
 Np=<V0-A0,A0-r>, Nr=<W0-Jr,r-A0>.

For J>0 and k0>=0 both normals are nonnegative. Define

 Theta(C0,k0,J)=H[C0*J-k0(C0+J+k0)]/(J+k0),
 B(C0,k0,J;V0,A0,r)
  =Theta||A0-r||^2
   +2H(1+C0/(J+k0))Np+2H*C0/(J+k0)Nr.             (3.1)

These normals are -Q_(r,A0) and -J Q_(A0,r) on actual augmented
samples. With X0=V0+T A0 and e=A0-r, projection normal algebra gives

 Np+Nr=(J+k0)<A0,e>-J||e||^2,
 <X0,e>=C0*J||e||^2/(J+k0)
                     +(1+C0/(J+k0))Np+C0*Nr/(J+k0).

Since W0+H r=X0-H e, expansion proves

 B=||V0+T A0||^2-||W0+H r||^2,
 U_N(X)-U_J(W0)=[V_left+B]/N+R(X,N,ZP).           (3.2)

This derivation allows a signed V_left. If C0*J>=k0(C0+J+k0), the
displayed representation of B is nonnegative.

For either H3, use its actual two H2 children of mass t, the bridge
excess k, and an arbitrary trailing parameter e>=1. Let its input X,
bridge query U, right input Z, endpoint Y, denominators J=e+2t and
N=J+2ell, and r=P(Z/J), qY=P(Y/e) be ACTUAL. Then

 U_N(X)-U_e(Y)
   =V_H2,left/N+(V_H2,right+L(t,Y,e))/J
    +B(C,k,J;U,P(U),r)/N
    +R(X,N,(X-Z+(J+ell)r)/N)
    +R(Z,J,(Z-Y+(e+t)qY)/J).                       (3.3)

Equation (3.3) follows by adding (3.2) and (2.5) for the actual right
H2. Every term is nonnegative: C>5, k<C/2, J>=2C-1 imply

 C*J-k(C+J+k)>(C/2)(2C-1)-3C^2/4=C(C-2)/4>0.

The H2 certificate establishes both V signs. This is a joint derivation
of H3 U; its earlier unverified report is not used as an accepted brick.

For the outer bridge we also have C0=1+s=2C+k>13, K<25/4<C0/2,
D>=2C0-1. The same calculation proves its Theta positive.
Useful elementary parameter bounds, proved in Section6, include C>11/2.

## 4. Instructed substitution with every term retained

Introduce the actual mixtures

 ZO=(x-w+(D+h)rO)/M,
 ZI=(x-z+(E+ell)rL)/M,
 ZF=(x-v+(eta+s)qL)/M,
 ZL=(z-v+(eta+t)qL)/E,

 RO=R(x,M,ZO), RI=R(x,M,ZI), RF=R(x,M,ZF),
 RL=R(z,E,ZL), B_i=B(C,k,E;u,g3,rL),
 B_o=B(1+s,K,D;v,g7,rO).

All four mixtures are convex mixtures of actual physical gradients and
the indicated actual auxiliary gradient; their weights were established
in Sections2-3. RO, RI and RF share p0 but are distinct remainders.

Write V00 and V01 for the left H3's two H2 primitive energies. Its
COMPLETE identity at eta is

 G_L:=U_M(x)-U_eta(v)
 =V00/M+(V01+L(t,v,eta))/E+B_i/M+RI+RL.           (4.1)

The full-left canonical identity (2.5) gives exactly
V_left=M G_L-L(s,v,eta)-M RF. Substitute this, including BOTH negative
terms, into the outer prefix identity (3.2). Define the signed exchange

 Xi=M(RO+RI+RL-RF)-L(s,v,eta)+(M/E)L(t,v,eta).      (4.2)

The result is the exact full-parent identity

 U_M(x)-U_d(y)
   =V00/M+V01/E+(B_i+B_o+Xi)/M+G_R,               (4.3)

where G_R is NOT an unspecified scalar hypothesis. Its full expression is

 G_R=V10/D+(V11+L(t,y,d))/E_R+B_r/D+RPr+RRr,
 B_r=B(C,k,E_R;uR,g11,rR),
 RPr=R(w,D,(w-zR+(E_R+ell)rR)/D),
 RRr=R(zR,E_R,(zR-y+(d+t)q)/E_R).                 (4.4)

V10 and V11 use the physical H2 indices8..11 and12..15 respectively.
Equations (4.1)-(4.4), (3.1), (2.3), and AppendixA specify EVERY square,
normal row, actual projection and coefficient of this expansion. A
term occurring with a negative sign in Xi remains present. Nothing in
(4.3) proves Xi>=0.

The simplest scalar-only version is also transparent. Dropping the
internal structure of G_L leaves the proposed transfer term

 B_o/M+RO-L(s,v,eta)/M-RF
                  =U_eta(v)-U_D(w).              (4.5)

It is exactly a single long-bridge U gap. This follows by subtracting
the two exact canonical identities, not by resetting the initial point.

## 5. Actual combination of the common-p0 squares and normals

Let A0=x-Mp0, a7=g7. Equation (2.4) keeps the complete normal portions
of the common-p0 remainders. Their exact combination is

 M(RO+RI-RF)
  =||w-(D+h)rO||^2+||z-(E+ell)rL||^2
          -||v-(eta+s)qL||^2-||A0||^2.           (5.1)

The fourth remainder is
M RL=(M/E)[||v-(eta+t)qL||^2-||z-E rL||^2].
For T=t or s, direct expansion of (2.6) gives

 ||v-(eta+T)qL||^2+L(T,v,eta)
       =||v+T a7||^2-(eta+2T)U_eta(v).

Substitution into (4.2) cancels the qL terms with their exact weights:

 Xi=||w-(D+h)rO||^2+||z-(E+ell)rL||^2
    -(M/E)||z-E rL||^2-||A0||^2
    -||v+s a7||^2+(M/E)||v+t a7||^2.             (5.2)

This is the requested algebraic combination, not a return to V_left.
Expanding only the rL terms, using M=E+2ell, gives a second full form:

 Xi=||w-(D+h)rO||^2-||A0||^2
    -(2ell/E)||z||^2+2ell<z,rL>+ell^2||rL||^2
    -||v+s a7||^2+(M/E)||v+t a7||^2.             (5.3)

The common initial maximizer leaves the negative square -||A0||^2;
normal terms have not silently been set to zero. There is also a signed
terminal combination involving the independent physical a7 and v.
Feasibility imposes projection relations on these vectors, but does not
make the displayed expression nonnegative. Sections6-8 realize negative
values on single legal bodies with ALL their projection relations.

One can alternatively complete the three remainder squares at the
formal point ZO+ZI-ZF, leaving the cross term
-2M^2<ZO-ZF,ZI-ZF>. That formal point has a negative qL coefficient
in its displayed mixture and is not asserted feasible. We do not use
that completion as a nonnegative maximization remainder.

## 6. Transfer A fails on a legal actual H4 core history

The precisely tested claim A is: for every fixed compact convex D0
containing0, every actual original H4 history, and every d>=1, the
exchange Xi of (4.2) is nonnegative. If it were true, (4.3)-(4.4)
and the H2 certificates would close H4. It is false.

The parameter bounds used below are intentionally coarse and symbolic.
We have beta>9/10, c<5/2, a>1/3, and a<sqrt(2)-1. Therefore

 11/2<C<6, 2<k<5/2, K<25/4<7,
 t<5, ell<9, 11<s<27/2.

For the lower bound C, use C>4+2/3+9/10=167/30>11/2.
For k>2, use c>lambda>12/5 and beta>9/10.
Put Q=a^2 beta; then

 1/10<Q<1/5.                                    (6.1)

The lower bound follows from a>1/3 and beta>9/10. For the upper bound,
(sqrt(2)-1)^2=3-2sqrt(2)<1/5 since sqrt(2)>7/5.

Choose the SINGLE body D0=[-1,1], start x=1/100, and d=1.
Every multiplier of a physical core step has magnitude a, beta, k, or K.
A prefix has at most two factors k and one K; all remaining factors have
magnitude below1. Hence every prefix product has magnitude below
k^2 K<(5/2)^2*7=175/4<100. Induction in physical order therefore keeps
every point, including x15, strictly inside[-1,1]. This is a symbolic
guard for all fifteen steps, not an executed trajectory. Every physical
gradient equals its input. Every auxiliary input is divided by a number
at least1, so every auxiliary projection is also its input. All normal
rows among these samples vanish.

The actual landmarks, obtained by multiplying the displayed factors, are

 u=-Qx, z=kQx, v=-kQ^2 x, w=-K v,
 y=-K(kQ^2)^2 x,
 p0=x/M, qL=v/eta, rL=z/E, rO=w/D.                (6.2)

In particular v!=0 and z/v=-1/Q. The actual remainders are

 M RO=h^2 w^2/D^2, M RI=ell^2 z^2/E^2,
 M RF=s^2 v^2/eta^2, M RL=(M/E)t^2 v^2/eta^2.

For T=t,s, L(T,v,eta)=[2T(1-1/eta)+T^2(1-1/eta^2)]v^2.
Thus the exact exchange, with all four remainders and BOTH radial
corrections still accounted for, is

 Xi/v^2=h^2 K^2/D^2+ell^2/(E^2 Q^2)
        -s(s+2)+2s/eta
        +(M/E)[t(t+2)-2t/eta].                    (6.3)

Here D=1+2s, h=s+1+K<s+8<D, eta>D>22, E>22, and
M/E=1+2ell/E<2. The five terms in (6.3) have the strict upper bounds

 49, 17, -143, 1, 70,

respectively. The second uses ell^2/(E^2 Q^2)<8100/484<17.
Consequently Xi/v^2<49+17-143+1+70=-6. Claim A is refuted with a
strict margin on a complete legal history of the requested original H4.

The same history also refutes scalar-only exchange (4.5), because
U_eta(v)-U_D(w)=v^2(1/eta-K^2/D)<0, with eta>D and K>1.

It does NOT refute H4 U. Its full terminal ratio at d=1 is

 U1(y)/U_M(x)=M[K(kQ^2)^2]^2.

Since Q<1/5 and k<5/2, kQ^2<1/10; hence K(kQ^2)^2<7/100.
Also M=1+2(2s+1+K)<71. Therefore the full ratio is
less than71*(7/100)^2=3479/10000<1. Positive terms excluded from Xi
remain essential in (4.3). No claim that Xi exhausts that identity is made.

## 7. Mandatory inherited cap/cap diagnostic in the actual H4

Use the SINGLE body D0=[-r0,1], where r0=k/C, and initial x=C.
Each prefix of the first H2 with mass T in[0,t] has point C-T>=1
and gradient1; u=1. The inner bridge sends it to z=-k. Each prefix of
the next H2 with mass T in[0,t] has point -k+T r0<=-r0 and gradient
-r0; v=-r0. Thus V00=V01=0 and all their adjacent-gradient reserves
are zero. Every row of the inner primitive-join E combination is zero:
its column at u or v has zero normal, and the other gradients on the
right agree. The outer bridge is actual and sends v to w=K r0.

The complete right H3 is retained. To specify all remaining physical
points without an unproved face pattern, let
T_l(Z)=Z-l*max(-r0,min(Z,1)), and set successively

 x9=T_A(w), x10=T_B(x9), x11=T_A(x10),
 x12=T_(1+k)(x11), x13=T_A(x12), x14=T_B(x13),
 x15=T_A(x14).

These finite displayed formulas are the exact right-child history on
the same body. No separate interval, restart or replacement terminal is
used. The counterexamples below involve Xi, which depends on this
right child through its actual mass; they require no assumption about
its faces. Its full nonnegative gap (4.4) remains in the parent identity.

For every d>=1 the relevant left and outer auxiliary projections are

 p0=C/M, qL=-r0/eta, rL=-k/E, rO=K r0/D.           (7.1)

All are interior: E>C, eta>=1, M>C, and w=K r0<7/2<D.
The physical normals at u=1 and v=-r0 are zero, so both inner and outer
bridge normals vanish as well. The actual remainders satisfy

 M RO=h^2K^2r0^2/D^2, M RI=ell^2 k^2/E^2,
 M RF=s^2r0^2/eta^2, M RL=(M/E)t^2r0^2/eta^2.

Consequently

 Xi/r0^2=h^2K^2/D^2+ell^2 C^2/E^2-s(s+2)+2s/eta
                         +(M/E)[t(t+2)-2t/eta].   (7.2)

At d=1, the same bounds as in Section6 apply, with the second term now
below81*36/484<7. Thus Xi/r0^2<49+7-143+1+70=-16.
This tests the aligned expansion on the mandatory zero-child-reserve
history. Its negative exchange cannot be paid by adjacent H2 squares.

## 8. Sole repair B also fails, with an explicit finite trailing parameter

Repair B is the precisely stated stronger attempt to make

                     Xi+B_i>=0                  (8.0)

hold for all actual H4 histories and d>=1. It consumes the COMPLETE
left H3 bridge square and both original auxiliary normal rows, while
leaving B_o as a separate nonnegative parent term. It still keeps all
remainders and radial corrections. We make no second repair.

Continue the SAME cap/cap body and physical history of Section7. Put

 delta=(2C+k)^2 r0^2-C^2.                         (8.1)

This delta is strictly positive. Let u0=sqrt(2)-1. We have
C=5-epsilon+2u0(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon),
k=lambda(1-epsilon^2), and lambda=5u0+2u0^2. Subtraction gives

 k-u0 C=epsilon[u0+4u0^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda epsilon]>0.

The bracket is positive already from u0>2/5, lambda<5/2 and
epsilon<1/8. Since k>u0 C, k(2C+k)>C^2, proving (8.1).
The actual left primitive energy is C^2-(2C+k)^2r0^2=-delta;
this is a diagnostic value, not an assumed primitive lower bound.

On this history B_i=Theta(C,k,E)(1+k/E)^2. Inserting (7.2) and its
explicit Theta gives the exact cancellation

                  Xi+B_i=-delta+h^2 w^2/D^2.      (8.2)

For a direct algebra check, the inner bridge coefficient expands to

 B_i/r0^2=ell(C-k)C^2/k^2-2ell C^2/E-ell^2 C^2/E^2.

Its last term cancels the second term of (7.2). Use M/E=1+2ell/E,
s=t+ell, t+1=C, and E=eta+2t. The remaining terms with1/E or1/eta
cancel, while the constant part is
C^4/k^2-(2C+k)^2=-delta/r0^2. This proves (8.2) directly from the
actual auxiliary values, not by reintroducing V_left as an unexplained
nonnegative or bounded quantity.

Choose the explicit finite real parameter

                 d_cap=1+2h w/sqrt(delta).        (8.3)

It is greater than1, and changes NO physical step or body. Its associated
D=d_cap+2s is greater than2h w/sqrt(delta). Hence (8.2) is strictly
less than-3delta/4. Repair B is refuted on a completely legal original
H4 history. Both primitive H2 reserves are exactly zero there.

The missing parent direction is explicit. Here

 a7-rO=-r0(1+K/D),
 B_o=Theta(1+s,K,D)r0^2(1+K/D)^2>0.               (8.4)

Any repair allocating only a fraction theta of this parent bridge to
pay (8.2), while using no other terms on this history, necessarily needs

 theta>=[delta-h^2K^2r0^2/D^2]
                /[Theta(1+s,K,D)r0^2(1+K/D)^2].   (8.5)

The numerator is positive at d_cap. As d tends to infinity this lower
bound tends to delta/[h(1+s-K)r0^2]>0. Thus an unconditional zero-cost
separation of the parent bridge is impossible. This is an exact
necessary cap-history allocation, NOT a uniform sufficient weight and
NOT a bound on its total accumulation over a tree. In particular no
epsilon*C_j^2 term has been relabeled O(epsilon).

For scope checking, the full cap-history prefix U gap is positive:

 U_D(w)/U_M(C)=K^2 k^2 M/(C^4 D)<46875/58564<1.    (8.6)

Indeed M/D<3 for all d>=1, C>11/2, k<5/2 and K<25/4. These give
the displayed rational bound. Its right H3 gap (4.4) is nonnegative
within this joint derivation. So even the complete cap H4 is not a
counterexample to U. The parent bridge supplies the missing payment.

## 9. Mandatory U-only3/2 calibration retained

This separate calibration is not substituted for an actual H4 child.
On a fixed body, one step l=3/2 has the universal U law for every d>=1.
To derive this jointly, put y=x-l a0 with a0=P(x), q0=P(y/d), N=d+3,
Awt=Nl/(N-1), Bwt=N-Awt, Z=(Awt*a0+Bwt*q0)/N.
Both weights are nonnegative and Awt<=2. Expansion gives

 N[U_N(x)-U_d(y)]
 =Awt(2-Awt)||a0-q0||^2
  +2Awt<x-a0,a0-q0>+N R(x,N,Z)>=0.

Before substituting the defining Awt, the mixed coefficient is
2[Nl-(N-1)Awt]<q0,a0-q0>=0; this checks the identity.

Yet two such children and the bridge9/4 on D0=[-1,1], starting at1,
give exactly 1,-1/2,5/8,-5/16. Both children satisfy the universal
hypothesis, and with C_L=5/2, k0=5/4, D=d+3 the scalar bridge condition
is C_L D-k0(C_L+D+k0)=(5/4)(d-3/4)>0. At d=1 the parent ratio is
575/512>1. Thus dropping retained reserves down to scalar child U
claims remains invalid. Sections6-8 establish NEW obstructions using
the actual H4; they do not disguise this3/2 calibration as an H4 history.

## 10. Limits, retrieval, and closeout

The exact aligned identity has been derived. Transfer A and its sole
repair B are legally refuted. The whole joint quantity B_i+B_o+Xi with
its remaining physical and right-child reserves is not refuted. Proving
its universal sign, or transporting an explicit bounded share of parent
bridge and normal data, remains open. No all-depth transfer is claimed.
No H5/H6 casework, extra mechanism, feasible-matrix oracle or solver was
introduced. We stop the allocated round after the one repaired template.

Recall used TASK, local notes, latest shared guidance7ffa301a1ff95b8b,
the full previous H3 draft870832ac79589e74, relevant dead ends and
accepted interpolation/composition proof sections. Initial broad output
was truncated; the relevant draft portions and accepted proof sections
were reread in bounded calls. Fact search returned no existing H4 U fact.

Skills applied: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples.
One Matlas search for auxiliary-projection concatenation returned
inapplicable short-step projected-gradient, convex-hull, and logistic
statements. Web background retrieval found Zhang--Jiang's original
paper, https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12395. No external theorem or snippet
was used as a premise, and no new literature theorem was imported.

Resources: zero mathematical programs, numerical evaluations,
arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, optimizers, benchmark
DP, formal calls/fact_submit, installations, consults, subagents,
outward publication, finalization, revocation, worker/service control,
shutdown or Goal completion. Actions were textual reasoning, public or
shared text retrieval, and administrative text writes/reads/hashes.
No live TASK overwrite or automatic renewal. No post-return completed
counter or process exit is claimed. Original target OPEN; no new fact_id.

## Appendix A. Complete actual H2 primitive certificate

This appendix supplies the physical certificates used jointly above.
For a step of length l from sample i to j, expansion of the two rows
in (2.1) gives

 V_l=-2Q_ij-2(1+l)Q_ji+(2-l^2)||g_i-g_j||^2,
 ||g_i||^2-||g_j||^2
   =(2/l-1)||g_i-g_j||^2-(2/l)(Q_ij+Q_ji).         (A.1)

For general left/right capacities P0,C0 and bridge1+t0 with0<=t0<C0,
let p be the bridge query, z0 the terminal, and j run through the
nonterminal right queries with physical step b_j. Define

 kappa=(2P0+C0+t0)/(C0-t0),
 Delta=P0*C0-t0(P0+C0+t0), R0=(P0+t0)Delta/(C0-t0),
 E0=sum b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z0,p)+t0 Q_(p,z0)+sum b_j Q_(j,z0).

Direct substitution of the actual updates yields

 V_parent=V_left+kappa V_right-(kappa-1)E0
                           +R0(||g_p||^2-||g_z0||^2). (A.2)

To check every coefficient, E0's value part is
2sum b_j f_j+(t0-C0)f_p+(2-C0-t0)f_z0; its gradient part is
sum b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(C0-t0)/2-t0^2]||g_p||^2
+(C0+t0)||g_z0||^2/2. All mixed terms cancel after inserting the right
displacement sum b_j g_j. The identities
(kappa-1)(C0-t0)=2(P0+t0),
P0^2-kappa*t0^2=R0,
kappa*C0^2-(P0+C0+t0)^2=-R0
verify (A.2) by multiplication by C0-t0.

For H2=(A,B,A), let C1=1+A=2+a and set

 kappa1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),
 Delta1=C1^2-beta(2C1+beta),
 R1=(C1+beta)Delta1/(C1-beta), deltaA=2-A^2.

With u0=sqrt(2)-1, a=u0(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon), and2u0+u0^2=1,

 u0 C1-beta=epsilon(1+epsilon-2u0^2)/(1+epsilon)>0,
 Delta1=(u0 C1-beta)(lambda C1+beta)>0.

Thus R1>0, deltaA>0,1<A<sqrt(2),1<B<2 and kappa1>1.
Apply (A.2) to the two A leaves and use (A.1) on the bridge B and
the final A leaf to expand its norm-drop term. On its four original
gradients the result is

 V_H2=-sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij
       +e0||g0-g1||^2+e1||g1-g2||^2+e2||g2-g3||^2,

 e0=deltaA,
 e1=R1(2/B-1),
 e2=kappa1*deltaA+R1(2/A-1),

 Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2(1+A),
 Lambda_12=2R1/B,
 Lambda_21=(kappa1-1)A+2R1/B,
 Lambda_13=(kappa1-1)beta, Lambda_31=kappa1-1,
 Lambda_23=2kappa1+(kappa1-1)A+2R1/A,
 Lambda_32=2kappa1(1+A)+2R1/A.                      (A.3)

All other multipliers vanish. All displayed coefficients are positive.
For example the value coefficients collect to(2A,2B,2A,-2t), since
-2A+(kappa1-1)(C1-beta)=2B. Thus no actual gradient or independent
adjacent square has been deleted. Translating the indices in (A.3)
supplies V00,V01,V10,V11 in (4.3)-(4.4).

# Growing Newton temporal spaces on the actual critical word

Status: complete draft candidates, UNVERIFIED. No solver, numerical evaluation,
installation or fact_submit. Accepted facts 1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and
normalization) and b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark and its elementary
envelope) are the only correctness inputs. Every new estimate is proved here.
These are obstructions for specified growing temporal spaces, not an upper
bound for the unrestricted worst-case objective and not the original target.

## 1. Actual word, queried prefix and a genuinely restricted growing space

Fix m>=128 and keep the following parameters fixed within that horizon:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, q=n-1.

The prescribed queried-prefix steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are H_m with its last
entry d_0 removed. Queries are indexed 0,...,q; the last free step is b in
[0,2], including the original b=d_0. Write

    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), 0<=i<=q.

Choose a REAL monic polynomial chi of degree r, with 1<=r<=q. For a
polynomial p let [p] denote its remainder modulo chi. This is an r-dimensional
real vector space; use coefficients in 1,z,...,z^(r-1) as its real coordinates.
Let T be ANY real linear map from this space into any finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, with ||T[1]||=1. Put

    x_i=T[P_i],       g_i=T[z P_i],     0<=i<=q.          (1)

The map, ambient dimension, metric, coefficients and polynomial can all depend
on m. Singular maps are allowed. Initial correlations are unrestricted. The
identity P_(i+1)=P_i-h_i zP_i proves the ACTUAL GD recursion for (1).

All queried values f_i remain joint variables. Assume that the data (1), those
values and the optimum jet (0,0,0) belong to ONE convex differentiable function
F with 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus the initial distance to this designated
minimizer is exactly one. All conclusions below hold for EVERY interpolant of
these jets; no terminal gradient identity is assumed after the last step b.

This is a fixed r-dimensional temporal space, even when the physical image
dimension is larger. For r=m it retains m independent Newton coefficients.
More generally the theorem below permits a degree much larger than m but
strictly smaller than the full n+1-node representation of arbitrary data.

## 2. The full computable all-pair law and function realization

Let J be multiplication by z modulo chi, let v_0 be the coefficient vector of
1, and define

    v_(i+1)=(I-h_i J)v_i,    w_i=Jv_i,    M=T^T T.

Then x_i=T v_i, g_i=T w_i, M is a REAL PSD matrix, and v_0^T M v_0=1.
Conversely every real PSD M has a real Euclidean factor T. With
v_star=w_star=0 and f_star=0, the necessary and sufficient full interpolation
law is, for EVERY distinct ordered pair i,j in {star,0,...,q},

 f_i-f_j+w_i^T M(v_j-v_i)
                   +(w_i-w_j)^T M(w_i-w_j)/2 <=0.       (2)

This is exactly Q_ij<=0 in fact 1252b8823bb5a290, evaluated on the stated
vectors. No pair or common value is replaced by a componentwise condition.
For feasible data that fact constructs an actual function explicitly:

 cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,
 H(v)=max_j {cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2},
 F_max(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.

The maximum includes the optimum jet. The function is finite, convex and
1-smooth, interpolates every queried value and gradient, and has minimum zero
at zero. This provides a function realization, not just a Gram representation.

For a Newton basis with roots theta_1,...,theta_r, repetitions allowed,
the basis polynomials are N_k(z)=product_(ell=1)^k(z-theta_ell), 0<=k<r.
Multiplication obeys zN_k=theta_(k+1)N_k+N_(k+1), with N_r=0 in the quotient.
Thus J is a bidiagonal Newton chain in that basis. For nonreal conjugate
roots the original real monomial coordinates, or real and imaginary parts of
the Newton coordinates, give the same REAL law (2); a complex matrix is not
being called a real PSD metric.

## 3. Exact level resonance: a complete growing-span annihilation obstruction

The level d_j occurs 2^(m-j-1) times in H_m. In the retained prefix the
counts are

    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1), 1<=j<m.              (3)

Let mu_j=1/d_j. Suppose

    chi(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j)^(r_j),
    0<=r_j<=N_j,     1<=sum r_j<=q.                    (4)

Since P_q(z)=product_j(1-d_j z)^(N_j), chi divides P_q. Thus x_q=g_q=0
in (1), for EVERY map T and every common metric, regardless of its condition
number or initial correlations. Every legal interpolant has

                    2F(x_q-b g_q)=0                    (5)

for every real b. This includes one independent Newton direction at EVERY
recursive large-step level (r=m), as well as every growing multiplicity
